# Backlog tasks

> Paths and identifiers written before 2026-10-10 may predate the #336 refactor: see [`renames.tsv`](renames.tsv).

> Some entries filed on 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25 were first stamped 2026-10-26; the stamps now carry the date of the commit that wrote them. Evidence directories and plan files from those days keep their 2026-10-26 names (for example `LLM/evidence/2026-10-26-568/`, `LLM/superpowers/plans/2026-10-26-491-558-560-562-570.md`), because they are paths other documents link to.

One line per open task. Each has a code/doc pointer so a fresh model can
start without a full context load. Source of truth for detail:
[`scope-backlog.md`](scope-backlog.md) (section letters in brackets),
`what-this-proves.md` (what already shipped), `git log`, and the memory
notes under
`.claude/projects/-home-vivo-repo-PORTUALE-portuale/memory/`.

Rules that apply to every task: take expected output from real Portage
(container bed, host `emerge`, or an upstream resolver test), pin it in
`pytests-contract-suite/test_emerge_pretend_contract.py` /
`test_portuale.py`, keep `test_output_invariants.py` green, and run the
full verification pass (`AGENTS.md` step 8). The suite and the container
bed live in the sibling `pmtest` repo since 2026-09-18
(`agent-context.md`, "Where the tests live"); `TEST/...` and `tests/...`
paths in the entries below are the pre-move spelling of what is now
`../pmtest/differential-test-bed/...` and
`../pmtest/pytests-contract-suite/...`. The Python mirror
(`python/emerge_pretend_reference.py`) was removed 2026-09-15 —
[`history/second_python_copy_removal.md`](history/second_python_copy_removal.md); older
entries below that say "dual-language" / "Rust==Python" describe how
they shipped, not a rule for new work.

63. **DONE 2026-09-16 — `EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS` is applied as prepended CLI defaults.** Shipped: config resolution moved above argv parsing in `pretend::run` (real's two-pass dance collapses to one — portuale has no `--config-root` CLI; `--help` still returns first), `args_with_emerge_defaults` prepends `shell_split(config.other_vars["EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS"])` unless `--ignore-default-opts`, and both that flag and `--ask-enter-invalid` moved out of the recognized-but-unimplemented table into the parser (the latter was the only host default with no implementation at all; `classify_yes_no`/`ask_confirm`/`ask_select` now reproduce `UserQuery.query`'s `enter_invalid` gate; `ASK_ENTER_INVALID` is reset per `run`). Host check: `--getbinpkg=y` from `/etc/make.local` now yields `[binary gU]` entries. Tests: `pretend::tests::emerge_default_opts_are_prepended_to_argv` (+ the extended `classify_yes_no_*` unit test), contract `test_emerge_default_opts_env_applies_and_argv_wins` / `..._from_a_sourced_make_conf`, help pin updated; `cargo test --release` 1125 passed, pytest 1697 passed. Residue found and filed as #69. Original report follows. **`EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS` is never applied (getbinpkg/usepkg/… ignored).** `resolve_config` stores the variable (`portage-profile` `ENV_SCALAR_VARS` `:1301` → `config.other_vars`), but `pretend::run` (`pretend.rs:7494`) parses argv directly and nothing consumes it, so the host's `EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS="--binpkg-respect-use=y --load-average=11 --autounmask=y --ask-enter-invalid --usepkg=n --getbinpkg=y"` (set in `/etc/make.local`, `source`d by `/etc/portage/make.conf`) is inert. Live: real lists `dev-lang/go`/`dev-build/cmake` as `[binary gU]` + `[f1r] Local copy of remote index…`; portuale lists both as `[ebuild]` (evidence `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-world-uDpvN/`). Passing `--getbinpkg` on the command line already works (`portuale emerge -pv --getbinpkg --oneshot dev-build/cmake` → `[binary gU]`), so only the defaults plumbing is missing: real prepends `shlex.split(settings["EMERGE_DEFAULT_OPTS"])` to argv in a second `parse_opts` pass (`_emerge/main.py:1221-1255,1352-1360`), argv winning. Plan: [`history/01.063-emerge_default_opts.md`](history/01.063-emerge_default_opts.md).

64. **DONE 2026-09-16 — `@world` now includes the `@profile` set.** Shipped: `Config::profile_packages` (unstarred `packages` lines of chain levels whose repo declares `profile-set`, stacked with `stack_mask_lines`; `portage-profile`'s `repo_profile_formats`/`repo_containing` reused), added in real's set order to both `@world` expansions (`pretend.rs` selected/system arms) and to `complete_seed_atoms`; `@profile` is now a first-class target. Committed fixture declares only `portage-2`, so every existing pin stays byte-identical; the positive path is pinned through a copied configroot (`test_profile_set_packages_join_world_and_are_a_target`) plus two `portage-profile` unit tests. Host effect: rclone/linux-firmware/vte/vte-common and weston now resolve, the libdisplay-info→kwin/mesa `rR` cascade disappears, and the `@world` list drops 68→50 with 24 binaries. Original report follows. **`@world` is missing the `@profile` set (rclone/linux-firmware/vte/weston dropped).** Real `@world = @profile @selected @system` (`portage/_sets/__init__.py:97-98`); `@profile` is the **unstarred** `packages` lines of profile levels whose repo declares `profile-set` (`_sets/profiles.py:13-38`), while `@system` keeps the `*`-starred lines of every level. Portuale computes only `system_packages` (`portage-profile/src/lib.rs:3152-3168`) and expands `@world` as selected+system (`pretend.rs:10093-10110`, seed `:10468`), so the host buildovl profile's 100-line `packages` file is dropped: real updates `net-misc/rclone`, `sys-kernel/linux-firmware`, `x11-libs/vte`, `gui-libs/vte-common`; portuale omits them. Missing `dev-libs/weston` also hides its `<media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0:=` bound, letting portuale upgrade libdisplay-info and cascade `rR` onto `kwin`/`kwin-x11`/`mesa`. The committed fixture repo declares only `portage-2` (no `profile-set`), so its pins must stay byte-identical; the host bug is reproduced with a copied configroot. Plan: [`history/01.064-world_profile_set.md`](history/01.064-world_profile_set.md).

67. **DONE 2026-09-16 — USE incremental `-<prefix>_*` wildcard removal missing (VIDEO_CARDS/SANE_BACKENDS `-*`).** S1 `c1447df` added the USE-scoped `apply_use_incremental(_iter)` (dead code + tests), S2 `1065ecc` migrated every USE token-list call site in `portage-profile` and `portage-repo::effective_use_flags_uncached` (the generic/mask/force/keywords folds deliberately keep `apply_incremental`), S3/S4 (this commit) added the `dev-libs/videodrv` fixture + contract pin (copied configroot, real-oracle cells) and the docs. Host effect: xorg-drivers and the three xf86-video-* drivers and sane-backends all leave `@world` (5 packages, nothing added). Original report follows. **USE incremental `-<prefix>_*` wildcard removal missing (VIDEO_CARDS/SANE_BACKENDS `-*`).** The host profile sets `VIDEO_CARDS="-* intel …"` / `SANE_BACKENDS="-* hp …"`; portuale prefixes `-*` to `-video_cards_*` (`prefix_tok` in `portage-profile/src/lib.rs` `resolve_config`) but `apply_incremental_token` only knows literal `-*`, so the clear is a no-op and parent-profile cards plus the ebuild's `+sane_backends_*` IUSE defaults survive. Live (still present post-#63, `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-post63-probes/`): portuale reinstalls `x11-base/xorg-drivers` and pulls `xf86-video-{dummy,fbdev,vesa}`, and reinstalls `media-gfx/sane-backends` with every `+sane_backends_*` on; real does neither. Real expands `-*` to `-<var>_*` per profile level (`config.py:2837-2866`), then the USE loop's `x[-2:] == "_*"` arm removes by prefix (`config.py:2925`); the fix must stay USE-scoped (mask/force/keywords folds and generic incrementals have no wildcard arm). Plan reviewed 2026-09-16 (call-site list completed: three `"USE" =>` arms, per-level and conf USE_EXPAND folds, UNPREFIXED, global user USE, `effective_use_flags_uncached`). Slices: S1 easy, S2 medium, S3 medium, S4 trivial. Land **before** #69. Plan: [`history/01.067-use_incremental_prefix_wildcard.md`](history/01.067-use_incremental_prefix_wildcard.md).

68. **DONE 2026-09-16 — false "unsatisfied block" when the blocked installed version is being replaced (host `@world` exits 1).** S1 `9da318f` drops an installed match replaced in its slot (the host shape: `emerge -uDpvN @world` now rc 0, gtk-doc upgraded); **display repair 2026-09-17 (`#68/#72 B0b`–`B5`, branch `backlog/68-74`):** the satisfied `[blocks b      ] <dev-util/gtk-doc-1.36.1 …` row now prints inline right after `gtk-doc-1.36.1` with `Conflict: 1 block (all satisfied)` (B2, the stuck-serializer predicate B0b confirmed), the removal `[uninstall]`/inline `b` rows + `uninst` counter + `--json` entry match real (B3/B4), and the `--columns`/`-q`/`--color` shapes are pinned; host `-uDpvN @world` is 27 identical packages with the same `Conflict:` line. Residues filed: #75 (`--tree`), #76 (`||` wait chains), #77 (nomerge-owner removal rows); evidence `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#68/#72 B0b"–"B4"; S2+S3 `6e2f743` read the owner's operation from its final entry, mark merge-bound matches with a merging parent unresolved, ignore an installed parent's installed matches, apply the soft same-slot skip, and replace the vdb reverse scan with the walked-graph parents test (the 2026-09-16 follow-up below adds the required-set closure after L0 caught the over-narrowing); the renderer gained the teal `b`/red `B` split and the `(all satisfied)` counters suffix. Container oracle cells a–h in `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#68 S0". **S3 follow-up 2026-09-16:** the graph-parents test alone regressed L0's `sys-apps/systemd-utils` (real `B`/rc 1 from installed systemd's `@system` reachability, portuale satisfied); `ResolveCtx::installed_closure` now backs the arm and L0 is back to the pre-existing classes (clean 100 / parity 0.833 / 34 unexplained, `l0-20260916T191018Z`). Residues filed: #72 (`[uninstall]` rows), #73 (set-argument parents). Original report follows. **False "unsatisfied block" when the blocked installed version is being replaced (host `@world` exits 1).** `<dev-util/gtk-doc-1.36.1` from `gtk-doc-am-1.36.1` matches installed `gtk-doc-1.34.0`, but `1.36.1` replaces it in the same run, so real prints `[blocks b]`/`Conflict: 1 block (all satisfied)` and exits 0; portuale's `resolve_blockers` sets `unsolvable = blocked_installed && installed_has_foreign_dependents(...)` with no replaced-in-slot check, so it prints `[blocks B]`/`1 unsatisfied`, the `* Error: … cannot be installed at the same time` block and exits 1 (still present post-#63). **Plan review 2026-09-16 corrected the real model** (`depgraph.py:9095-9255`, read in full): `blocked_final` (package tracker) holds merge-bound matches *and* surviving installed packages; a match is uninstall-ordered when the parent merges and the match is installed, or when the parent is nomerge (uninstall the parent); only a merge-bound match with a merging parent is unresolved outright; an uninstall is abandoned if the installed instance is a digraph node with parents; installed parents' `blocked_initial` matches are ignored; soft same-slot matches are skipped. Portuale gaps: no replaced check, merge-bound matches never unsolvable, same-version reinstall deduplicated into the installed row, `PendingBlocker` has no owner merge/nomerge bit (blockers are also pushed from installed parents in `enqueue_dependencies`), pulled-in test is a vdb reverse scan instead of walked graph entries. Free unsatisfied oracle: `emerge -puDvN --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-containers/runc` → real `[blocks B]`. Slices: S0 oracle cells a–h medium, S1 replaced-in-slot (fixes host rc) medium, S2 merge-bound + `owner_merging` hard, S3 graph-entry pulled-in test medium, S4 pins easy. Plan: [`history/01.068-blocker_replaced_version.md`](history/01.068-blocker_replaced_version.md).

69. **DONE 2026-09-16, repaired 2026-09-17 (`#69 R0`–`R3`, branch `backlog/68-74`) — `--getbinpkg`/`--usepkg` + `--newuse` drops a needed rebuild (found while shipping #63).** Repair (R0–R3, plan [`history/02.68-74.md`](history/02.68-74.md) Phase B): the R0 oracle showed the no-ebuild comparison is reached under `--usepkg` but never under plain `--usepkgonly` (real's respect-use default, `create_depgraph_params.py:47-55`); R1 compares the no-ebuild binary with the profile's selection over its IUSE (real `pkgsettings.setcpv(pkg)`), R2 subtracts force/masked flags (real `flags -= forced_flags`), R3 closes the docs. Bed cells n1–n4 and the host `@world` diff re-verified (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#69 R0"–"#69 R2"). The 2026-09-16 closure note follows. S0 `ed11de3` staged the `binnew` fixture and captured a real-built binary matrix (12 cells; the crafted fixture `.tbz2`s turned out to be portuale-only, so the oracle pipeline builds one with real `--buildpkgonly` + `--usepkgonly` then adds `newflag` to the ebuild); S1 `33c0479` reworked `binpkg_respect_use_ok` into real's gate (`reinstall_use or (not installed and respect_use)`) with `_reinstall_for_flags`'s arms against the same-version ebuild's IUSE/USE; S2/S3 (this commit) pinned the six cells and closed the docs. Host: `-uDpvN --oneshot dev-qt/qttools` prints `[ebuild R] … LLVM_SLOT="22 -17 … (-23%)"` and qttools is back in `@world`. Original report follows. **`--getbinpkg`/`--usepkg` + `--newuse` drops a needed rebuild (found while shipping #63).** With remote binpkgs eligible (host defaults `--getbinpkg=y --binpkg-respect-use=y`), portuale omits `dev-qt/qttools` from `emerge -uDpvN --oneshot dev-qt/qttools` and from `@world`; real rebuilds it (`[ebuild R] … LLVM_SLOT="22 -17 … (-23%)"`, the ebuild's IUSE gained `llvm_slot_23`). `--usepkg` (local only), `--ignore-default-opts` and `--getbinpkg --usepkg-exclude=dev-qt/qttools` rebuild it; `--binpkg-respect-use=n` does not. **Plan review 2026-09-16 corrected the diagnosis:** real's binary rejection (`depgraph.py:8260-8294`) runs when `reinstall_use` (`--newuse` or any `--reinstall`) **or** respect-use, and compares the binary's baked USE/IUSE against the **same-version ebuild's** profile USE/IUSE (`_reinstall_for_flags`, `:3134-3162`: IUSE symmetric difference counts under `--newuse` or plain respect-use); a rejected binary is skipped and the ebuild is compared with the vdb. `identical_binary` (`:8001-8017`) keeps the *installed* candidate and is not involved (the first plan misread it). Likely root cause: `binpkg_respect_use_ok` runs only under respect-use and evaluates the binary's own IUSE, so an ebuild-only flag is invisible and the binary becomes `best`, after which the newuse diff (binary baked flags vs vdb) is empty. Slices: S0 local-binhost oracle matrix medium, S1 real's rejection gate + ebuild IUSE on both call sites medium, S2 pins + host probes medium, S3 trivial. Land after #67. Plan: [`history/01.069-getbinpkg_newuse_rebuild.md`](history/01.069-getbinpkg_newuse_rebuild.md). Evidence: plan §1 probe table, `docs/history/evidence/2026-09-16-post63-probes/`.

70. **DONE 2026-09-16, repaired 2026-09-16/17 (`#70 R1`–`R5`, branch `backlog/68-74`) — fetch transport overrides are ignored: `FETCHCOMMAND`/`RESUMECOMMAND` (and protocol variants) plus `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (found while consolidating #14).** Repair (R1–R5, plan [`history/02.68-74.md`](history/02.68-74.md) Phase A): the S1 parser mangled `\${VAR}`/`\"` (R1), an absent `FETCHCOMMAND` errored instead of defaulting to make.globals (R2), the `${FILE}` refusal was stricter than real (R3), the substitution variables were incomplete (R4); R5 proves a live `ebuild … unpack` download with the default and with a user `FETCHCOMMAND`, verifies the `.mirror-cache.json` round-trips both ways and re-runs L2 (0 unexplained). Shipped: `portage-fetch` gained `FetchCommands` (per-protocol selection, real's `${FILE}` refusal) + `download_with_commands` (POSIX split, `${DISTDIR}`/`${URI}`/`${FILE}` substitution, fresh-failure cleanup) and a public `split_shell_words`; `WgetFetcher`/`Director::fetch` and `portuale::fetch` both take the resolved family (`fetch_commands_from_config` reads the config scalars, `resolved_fetch_commands` resolves the chain in `ebuild_phases`); `FetchOptions::ro_distdirs` (`resolved_ro_distdirs`, existing dirs only) is checked before the fsmirrors copy and every candidate, layout-resolved, digest-checked and symlinked. Unit + integration tests on stub commands and the RO symlink; `FEATURES=distlocks` stays the documented cut. Original report follows. **Fetch transport overrides are ignored: `FETCHCOMMAND`/`RESUMECOMMAND` (and protocol variants) plus `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (found while consolidating #14).** `portage_fetch::download_via_wget` hardcodes `make.globals`'s default wget template, so a user's `FETCHCOMMAND="curl …"` (or `FETCHCOMMAND_<PROTO>` and the matching `RESUMECOMMAND*`) is never used; real picks the protocol variant, falls back to the plain name, substitutes `${DISTDIR}`/`${URI}`/`${FILE}` and refuses a command without `${FILE}` (`fetch.py:1652-1700`). Two call sites need it (`portuale/src/fetch.rs` and `mrg-director`'s `Director::fetch`). `PORTAGE_RO_DISTDIRS` (`fetch.py:1055-1059`, `:1456-1475`): existing dirs only, layout-resolved, digest-checked, then **symlinked** into a writable `DISTDIR`; portuale only lists the name in `phase_environ`. Slices: S1 FETCHCOMMAND family medium, S2 RO_DISTDIRS medium, S3 close-out trivial — [`history/01.014-fetch_candidates_mirrors.md`](history/01.014-fetch_candidates_mirrors.md) §5. `FEATURES=distlocks` stays a documented cut.

88. **DONE 2026-09-20 — `bin_dir()`'s per-process PATH overlay is never removed (found auditing pmtest's own /tmp exhaustion, 2026-09-18).** One `libc::atexit` registration where the overlay is created (`ebuild_phases.rs::bin_dir`) removes `$TMPDIR/portuale-bin.<pid>` on every normal process exit -- a single site covering all seven call sites across three files, instead of per-command cleanup on every exit path. Fallback paths (vendored/checkout used directly) register nothing; signal kills still leak (same as any tmp dir). Measured: one phase-touching test process leaves 1 dir before, 0 after; `remove_bin_overlay_dir` idempotency pinned by unit test. The pmtest harness-side sweep stays as defense in depth. Original entry follows. **`bin_dir()`'s per-process PATH overlay is never removed (found auditing pmtest's own /tmp exhaustion, 2026-09-18).** `ebuild_phases.rs:666` makes a fresh `$TMPDIR/portuale-bin.<pid>` directory of symlinks (the `PORTAGE_BIN_PATH` overlay: vendored `bin/` over the `3rdparty/portage` checkout) on every process that runs real ebuild phases, and nothing ever deletes it -- not on success, not on error, not `atexit`. It hid inside `/tmp`'s general churn; pointing `$TMPDIR` at a dedicated directory (`../pmtest/USAGE.AGENTS.md` "Spazio /tmp") made it visible as ~500 tiny leftover directories per full contract-suite run, growing without bound run after run until pmtest started sweeping them itself from the *outside* -- a real leak in the product, not an artifact of the harness sweeping around it. Fix needs to run inside the same process (the harness can only clean up *after* the fact): either an RAII guard (`Drop` removing `overlay` when the last user is done) or an explicit `std::fs::remove_dir_all(&overlay)` on every exit path of the command that calls `bin_dir()` -- whichever fits the existing process lifetime better, since `bin_dir()` itself is a `OnceLock`, resolved once and read from many call sites for the rest of the process. Trivial in isolation; the only judgment call is where in the process's own shutdown path the removal belongs. Evidence: `../pmtest/pytests-contract-suite/conftest.py`'s `_reclaim_portuale_bin_overlays` (the harness-side sweep, commit `0a3397d` in pmtest) and its own commit message for the measured before/after counts.

89. **DONE 2026-09-18 — `/etc/portage/package.*` directory fragments recurse with real's name filters.** `portage_util::recursive_config_files` ports `_recursive_file_list` exactly (arbitrary depth, one ascending sort per directory over files+dirs with in-place expansion, `VCS_DIRS` + dotfile + `~`-backup skips at every level including the root, symlinks followed), shared by `read_config_lines` (every `package.*`/`binrepos.conf`/profile read) and the `repos.conf` scan (which real also reads recursively); both halves landed together since recursion without filters would activate `.git/`+backups. Pins: `recursive_config_files_matches_reals_traversal_and_filters` unit test (mirrors the S0 `_recursive_file_list` oracle table incl. symlinks and root filtering). No existing fixture uses the directory form, so zero existing pins moved. Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #89. Original entry follows. **`/etc/portage/package.*` directory fragments are read one level deep, and real's name filters are missing (reported from the VM bed, 2026-09-18).** Real reads every one of these paths with `grabfile_package(..., recursive=1)` -> `portage/util/__init__.py::_recursive_file_list`, which walks a directory to **arbitrary depth**, skipping `VCS_DIRS` (`CVS RCS SCCS .bzr .git .hg .svn`, `const.py:278`), dot-prefixed names and `~`-suffixed backups (`_recursive_basename_filter`), and yielding files in **one merged ascending sort over files and directories, with each directory expanded in place** (stack DFS: `aa/inner` sorts between `00-first` and `nn-mid`; `zz/inner` before `zz-last`). Portuale's `portage-profile/src/lib.rs::read_config_lines` (~`:2044`) instead does a single `read_dir` filtered by `.filter(|p| p.is_file())` -- a subdirectory is silently dropped -- and applies **no** name filter, so it reads exactly the files real ignores. A second, independent copy of the same logic sits in `portage-repo/src/lib.rs` (~`:1143`) for `repos.conf`, which real also reads recursively (`repository/config.py:943-950`). Both halves are divergences and they point opposite ways, so **adding recursion without the filters makes things worse**: `.git/` and editor backups under `package.use/` would become live config. Reported from the VM bed as an `INVALID` run (`MERGE_RC=2`): the stock cloud image ships `/etc/portage/package.use/releng/no-filecaps` (`*/* -filecaps`), real honours it and merges the htop binpkg, portuale ignores the fragment, computes `filecaps` from the IUSE default, sees a binpkg USE mismatch and rebuilds from source; the same probe in the container bed (no `releng/` fragment) has both PMs agree, which is what isolates it. Reproduced host-side 2026-09-18 on a copied configroot: with `package.use/releng/frag` = `*/* nestedtestflag` and `package.use/zz-flat-control` = `*/* flattestflag`, real's `portage.config(config_root=...)['USE']` carries **both**, portuale's `emerge --info` carries only `flattestflag`; adding `zz-flat-control~` and `.hidden-frag` shows the mirror defect (real ignores both, portuale applies both). One `read_config_lines` feeds every `/etc/portage/package.{use,env,license,mask,unmask,accept_keywords,properties,accept_restrict}`, `binrepos.conf`, and the profile/repo-side `package.*`/`use.*`/`arch.list` reads, so the fix is two functions, not twenty call sites. No committed fixture uses the directory form at all today, which is why nothing caught it. Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) "#89". [C]

94. **DONE 2026-09-23 — declaration builtins assignment-expand an expanded name.** Shipped as Phase 6 S1–S4 (plan `01.94-brush-declaration-assignment.md`, batch `batch-2026-09-21.md`; fork branch `fix/declaration-assignment-expansion` S1 `dd016ba6` + S2 `38447d84`, fork `main` squash `eb3b6c7b`; portuale branch `backlog/94-brush-declaration-assignment`; re-pin `4edb1f43` → `eb3b6c7b`). S1 re-examines the expanded string inside the builtins (split on the first `=`, honour `+=`, compound `name=(…)` values untouched); S2 adds the compat matrix and unmarks 13 healed known-failures; S3 stages `docs/brush-pr/06-*`; S4 adds the `exportexpandpkg` fixture + `expanded_name_export_assigns_in_both_backends` guard and re-pins. Verified: brush-compat 2576 ran / 2120 succeeded / 0 unexpected, portuale workspace green, pmtest 1773 passed / 0 failed, live `ebuild --shell brush media-gfx/gimp-3.2.6 pretend` exits 0 past `pkg_pretend`. Original entry follows. `--shell brush` silently drops `export ${var}=value` (declaration builtins never assignment-expand an expanded name), killing `toolchain-funcs.eclass`'s `_tc-getPROG`; real `emerge --shell brush media-gfx/gimp` dies in `pkg_pretend` (filed 2026-09-20). Live: `portuale emerge --shell brush media-gfx/gimp-3.2.6` prints ` * Your current compiler does not support OpenMP!`, then `error: command not found: -E` twice, then `die "Active compiler does not have required support for OpenMP"`; the default `--shell bash` path is unaffected. Chain: `toolchain-funcs.eclass:25-45` `_tc-getPROG` ends with `export ${var}="${prog[*]}"; echo "${!var}"` (reached whenever `CC`/`BUILD_CC`/… is unset, i.e. the normal emerge env), and `tc-getCC` (`:54`/`:67`) therefore echoes **empty** under brush -- not an error, an empty string with status 0. `_tc-has-openmp` (`:647`) then runs ` -fopenmp test.c -o test` (the empty command expansion leaves `-fopenmp` as argv[0], `command not found`, stderr swallowed by its own `>&/dev/null`), returns nonzero, and `tc-check-openmp` (`:730`) `eerror`s + `die`s; its own diagnostics (`tc-is-gcc`/`tc-is-clang` -> `tc-get-compiler-type`, `:913-942`, `res=$($(tc-getCC "$@") -E -P - <<<"${code}")`) are where the two visible `command not found: -E` come from. Root cause is in brush, reproduced minimal on the pinned rev: `var=CC; prog=( gcc ); export ${var}="${prog[*]}"` leaves `CC=[]` (exit 0) under the embedded pin `b9524ad5` **and** under upstream `reubeno/brush` main `6bada559` (built and run 2026-09-20; still unfixed, no compat test covers it). brush's parser only recognizes an assignment word when the name is a *literal* identifier (`brush-parser/src/word.rs:1263` `assigned_scalar_name`), so `export ${var}=…` is a plain word; after expansion the builtin receives `CommandArg::String("CC=gcc")` and `export.rs:72-95`'s `String` branch only marks an *existing* variable exported -- a silent no-op (`declare`/`local` print `not a valid variable name`, `declare.rs:299`). bash instead performs the assignment after expansion for declaration builtins (`export`/`declare`/`local`/`readonly`/`typeset`; POSIX XCU 2.9.1), which is what every `_tc-getPROG` user relies on. Fix is upstream-first (sixth staged fix, e.g. `fix/declaration-assignment-expansion`: split `CommandArg::String` on the first `=` in the declaration builtins, or defer assignment detection for declaration commands), plus a brush compat case for the literal/`${var}`/quoted/append + `declare`/`local` forms and a Rust guard on the pin (an `export ${var}=`-using fixture, `heredocpkg`-style); this is a second, independent blocker to flipping the `--shell` default back to brush (alongside #5's PRs), and `docs/brush-pin.md` "What is *not* tracked here" records it. **Update 2026-09-20:** still unfixed on the 2026-09-20 re-pin (`4edb1f43`, upstream base `6bada559`); upstream's open, breaking [reubeno/brush#1280](https://github.com/reubeno/brush/pull/1280) ("centralize assignment expansion + overhaul declaration builtins") is the fix path — no sixth fork-staged fix was carried, and #5 now has four PRs to open, not five. **Update 2026-09-23:** shipped as Phase 6 S1–S4 (see the DONE line); the pin moved to `eb3b6c7b` carrying fix 06, and #5 is back to five PRs to open. [G]

95. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/95-package-env`: S0 `eb8bd23` + pmtest `30397dc`, S1 `f0a26bc`, S2 `0571aa3` + pmtest `64d4b9e`) — `package.env` vars now use real `_grab_pkg_env`'s acceptance set.** `match_package_env_vars` no longer narrows to `pretend::BUILD_VARS`: it rejects only real's `_non_user_variables` (dynamic `PROFILE_ONLY_VARIABLES` ∪ `env_blacklist` ∪ `CONFIG_PROTECT`), `PKGUSE`/`USE` (the latter on `Config::package_env_use`), and the keys portuale's own phase pipeline owns (`ENVIRON_FILTER`, `PORTUALE_COMPUTED`); empty values are kept (real blanks) and incrementals append within the container then fold onto the caller's base env with `-*`/`-tok` pruning and a sorted union (`regenerate()`). Pins: the accepted/rejected matrix, the three S0 incremental captures, scalar last-wins/empty-blanks (Rust units), the extended `penv-buildflags`/`penvbuildpkg` fixture pins, and the new hermetic `penvccpkg` `tc-getCC`/`tc-is-lto` end-to-end test with a stub compiler. Residues filed: #98 (`FEATURES`), #99 (`PORTAGE_TMPDIR`), #100 (standalone run-wide env), #101 (calling-env vs `pkg` layer precedence). Plan: [`history/01.95-package_env_acceptance.opus.md`](history/01.95-package_env_acceptance.opus.md); evidence: pmtest `differential-test-bed/findings/l2.md` "## #95 S0". Original entry follows. **`package.env` build vars are filtered to `pretend::BUILD_VARS`, dropping `CC`/`CXX`/`CPP`/`AR`/`NM`/`RANLIB`/`LD`/`RUSTFLAGS`/`CGO_*`/… — real merges `mail-client/thunderbird-156.0`, portuale dies in `pkg_pretend` -> `tc-is-lto` with `cc1: error: unrecognized argument to '-flto=' option: 'thin'` (found 2026-09-20, host `llvm-lto` env; not a brush bug — reproduced with the default `--shell bash`).** Host mapping: `/etc/portage/package.env/llvm-lto.conf:30` (`mail-client/thunderbird llvm-lto`) + `/etc/portage/env/llvm-lto` (`COMMON_FLAGS`/`CFLAGS`/`CXXFLAGS`/`FCFLAGS`/`FFLAGS` with `-flto=thin`, plus `CC=clang`, `CXX=clang++`, `CPP=clang-cpp`, `AR=llvm-ar`, `NM=llvm-nm`, `RANLIB=llvm-ranlib`, `LD`/`LDFLAGS=-fuse-ld=lld`, `RUSTFLAGS`, `CGO_*`; the host's other env files add `FEATURES` and `PORTAGE_TMPDIR`). `match_package_env_vars` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_phases.rs:1060`, shared by the merge path's `emerge_build::entry_package_env_vars` and the standalone path's `phase_standalone_base_env`) keeps only keys in `pretend::BUILD_VARS` (`rust/portuale/src/pretend.rs:6863`: `CFLAGS CXXFLAGS CPPFLAGS LDFLAGS FFLAGS FCFLAGS ASFLAGS MAKEOPTS CHOST CBUILD CTARGET`), so the same file's `CFLAGS=-flto=thin` lands but `CC=clang` is silently dropped; `tc-getCC` falls back to gcc and `tc-is-lto` (`toolchain-funcs.eclass:1268`, the gcc branch) fails. Evidence: real's resolved config for the atom (`portage.package.ebuild.config.config.setcpv`) is `CC=clang`, `CFLAGS=…-flto=thin…`; portuale alone reproduces it hermetically (`PORTAGE_TMPDIR=… portuale ebuild /var/db/repos/gentoo/mail-client/thunderbird/thunderbird-156.0.ebuild pretend` -> rc 1, identical trace, `temp/die.env` carries `CFLAGS='…-flto=thin…'` and **no `CC`**). **Real's acceptance model, verified in `3rdparty/portage`:** `_grab_pkg_env` (`lib/portage/package/ebuild/config.py:2269-2300`) copies **every** key of the env file into `configdict["pkg"]` except (a) `protected_keys` = the keys already in the pkg layer before package.env (`{PKGUSE}` at `setcpv`, `config.py:2029-2038`; `USE` is popped and re-appended around `package.use`, which therefore wins) and (b) `_non_user_variables` (`config.py:724-733` = the profile's dynamic `PROFILE_ONLY_VARIABLES` (`ARCH ELIBC KERNEL IUSE_IMPLICIT USE_EXPAND_*…`) ∪ `env_blacklist` (`_config/special_env_vars.py:15-81`: A AA BDEPEND CATEGORY DEPEND … SLOT SRC_URI …) ∪ `global_only_vars` (`CONFIG_PROTECT`)); a rejected key prints `!!! Illegal variable '<k>' assigned in '<file>'`; `INCREMENTALS` (`lib/portage/const.py:125`: `ACCEPT_KEYWORDS CONFIG_PROTECT_MASK ENV_UNSET FEATURES USE USE_EXPAND…`) **append** to the layer's current value instead of replacing; a `*/*` package.env entry goes to the global `conf` layer (`config.py:905-920`). So the legal per-package set is everything else: toolchain selectors (`CC CXX CPP AR NM RANLIB LD AS STRIP`), language/build flags (`RUSTFLAGS`, `GOFLAGS`, `CGO_*`, `NINJAOPTS`, `EMAKEOPTS`, `COMMON_FLAGS`, `LTOFLAGS`, `INSTALL_MASK`, `MAKEOPTS`, `DISTDIR`, `PORTAGE_TMPDIR`, `PORTAGE_NICENESS`, …), and the incrementals above. **Fix:** replace the `BUILD_VARS` filter with real's acceptance set — portuale already has the exclusion constants (`portage_profile::phase_environ::{ENVIRON_FILTER,ENV_BLACKLIST,PORTUALE_COMPUTED}`, `phase_environ.rs:64-240`; `ENV_BLACKLIST` is byte-for-byte real's `env_blacklist`) — keep `USE` on the existing `package_env_use` path, and give the incrementals append semantics. **Residues to file with the slice (narrower, deeper plumbing — currently resolved before/independently of package.env):** per-package `FEATURES` must reach `MergeOptions::features` and the `feature_enabled` gates (plus the phase env's folded `FEATURES`), and per-package `PORTAGE_TMPDIR` must feed the builddir path (the host's `tmpfs_on_disk` env is a live consumer). Pins: a fixture env file setting `CC` + representative selectors/incrementals, a `match_package_env_vars` unit test over the accepted/rejected sets (mirroring `_non_user_variables`), and an end-to-end `ebuild <fixture> pretend` in the `tc-getCC`/`tc-is-lto` shape with a stub compiler. [D]

96. **DONE 2026-09-20 — merge writes are atomic: `merge_tree` materializes the new file/symlink at a `.{name}._portage_merge_.{pid}` sibling and `rename(2)`s it over the destination (real `movefile`'s own same-device rename / copy-to-`#new`-then-rename), so the existing inode is never written to.** Shipped in `rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs`: `replace_file_atomic`/`replace_symlink_atomic`/`unique_sibling_path` (temp cleaned up on any failure), used by `merge_tree`'s obj and sym branches. Pins: `merge_tree_never_writes_the_existing_inode` (an open fd still reads the old bytes after the merge while the path carries the new content on a fresh inode — this is the mmap'd-library property, and it fails under the old `std::fs::copy`), `merge_tree_replaces_a_running_executable_atomically` (a live `/bin/sleep` copy survives the replacement, which the old code aborted with `ETXTBSY`), `merge_tree_replaces_a_symlink_without_temporaries`; workspace `cargo test --release` green. **Merge-path safety gate added** to `docs/agent-context.md` ("Merge-path safety gate") and `AGENTS.md` step 8: any merge-path change must be verified by a successful test merge of `sys-libs/glibc` and `app-shells/bash`. The bed cell is `differential-test-bed/atomlists/l1-merge-gate.txt` (pmtest) with `L1_CONSUME_REINSTALL=1` (`layers/l1/consume.sh`/`run/l1-merge-from-binpkg.sh`; forces `--reinstall-atoms`, records same-cpv reinstalls in the snapshot): live run `l1-20260920T141149Z` re-merged `sys-libs/glibc` + `app-shells/bash` over portuale's own running system — merge_rc 0, 4006 files compared, **0 hard / 0 unexplained**, only non-fatal mtime diffs. Original report follows. **`merge_tree` rewrites regular files in place (`std::fs::copy`), corrupting live mmap'd files: merging `sys-libs/readline` crashed every running `bash` (SIGSEGV in `_rl_forward_char_internal`), and the same path on `glibc` would take down the whole system (found 2026-09-20, host merge of `app-shells/bash`).** Live on this box: portuale merged `readline-8.3_p6` at 13:05:22 — `/usr/lib64/libreadline.so.8.3` `ctime=13:05:22` with `mtime=11:39:01` (the binpkg's own mtime copied onto the **existing** inode), no `PORT_LOGDIR` build log (real always logs; the p6 log does not exist, the `bash-5.3_p20` real-recovery log does), vdb entry written 13:05:22, and the preserved-libs registry still lists `readline-8.3_p3 -> /usr/lib64/libreadline.so.8.3` (every running bash had p3 mapped). First coredump 4 s later (13:05:26), then 13:06:13/38/52 and 13:08/13:09 — all `SIGSEGV`, all `_rl_forward_char_internal (libreadline.so.8 + 0x40616)` -> bash's fatal-signal re-raise (`si_code: SI_USER`) -> Konsole's `Program '/bin/bash' crashed.` The subsequent bash merge then aborted mid-`merge_tree` (all `/usr`+`/etc` files copied, `/bin/bash` not) and wrote no vdb entry, which is why real's recovery found the p20 doc/build-id files "unclaimed" with p15 still installed (`/var/log/portage/build/app-shells/bash-5.3_p20:20260920-110801.log.gz`). Root cause: `merge_tree`'s obj branch replaces a regular file with `std::fs::copy(&src, &write_dest)` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs:1774`, binary merge via `merge_binpkg` `:3880`) — an `O_TRUNC` rewrite of the **existing inode**. Kernel asymmetry: `ETXTBSY` protects a running *executable* (the `/bin/bash` copy fails and aborts the merge), but an mmap'd *shared library* opens for write fine (verified live on this box), so the running processes' code pages are rewritten underneath their p3-resolved relocations and they segfault. Real `movefile` never touches the old inode: same-device `os.rename` (`3rdparty/portage/lib/portage/util/movefile.py:231`) or cross-device copy to `dest#new` then rename (`:346`). **`/etc/ld.so.cache` is eliminated** (portuale runs the same `<root>/sbin/ldconfig -X -r <root>` as real, `env_update.rs:206-226` vs `env_update.py:309`, after the merge; the loader reads the cache only at exec and it cannot segfault a running process). **Fix (this slice):** port real `movefile`: write the new content/owner/mode/mtime to a temporary sibling (`.{name}._portage_merge_.{pid}`, unique) and `rename(2)` it over the destination; same for symlinks (create temp link + rename, not remove+recreate). Regression test: a child process executing (and a second mapping) a file the merge replaces must survive the merge, the new content must land, the inode must change, and no temp sibling may remain. **Mandatory merge-path gate (added to `docs/agent-context.md` and `AGENTS.md` step 8): any change to the merge path must be verified by successful test merges of `sys-libs/glibc` and `app-shells/bash`** — the packages whose running files every process depends on — before the slice is considered done.

97. **DONE 2026-09-20 — `mrg`'s remote merge driver replaces files and links atomically (backlog #96 residue).** `MERGE_FLOW`'s obj branch now materializes the new file at `.${basename}._portage_merge_.$$` in the destination directory (`cp -p` + `chmod`/`chown --reference` applied to the temporary) and `mv -f`s it over the destination; the sym branch builds the link at the same temporary (`ln -s`, `touch -h -r`, `chown -h`) and renames it. Only a *symlink* destination is unlinked first, exactly like real `movefile`'s `os.unlink(dest)` for a symlink dest, so `mv` can never resolve a symlink-to-directory as a directory and the regular-file path never touches the live inode. The old `cp -p "$src" "$dest"` / `rm -f` + `ln -s` pair had the #96 failure mode on the client: `ETXTBSY` on a running executable, silent page rewrite for an mmap'd shared library. Pins (both execute the real generated driver locally through `run_merge_stage`): `merge_driver_never_writes_the_existing_inode` (an open fd still reads the old bytes after the merge while the path carries the new content on a fresh inode, no temporary left) and `merge_driver_replaces_a_running_executable_atomically` (a live `/bin/sleep` copy survives; the old code failed its `cp` with `ETXTBSY`). This is the driver-level half of the merge-path safety gate for `mrg` (a full client/server bed cell is not needed: the tests run the exact script the client executes); the local gate re-run after the change is `l1-20260920T145853Z` (glibc+bash re-merge, 0 hard / 0 unexplained). Original entry follows. **`mrg`'s remote merge driver still writes regular files with an in-place `cp -p` (backlog #96 residue).** The generated client shell in `rust/portuale/src/remote.rs`'s `MERGE_HELPERS` obj branch does `[ -L "$dest" ] && rm -f "$dest"; cp -p "$src" "$dest"` (`:2305`) and the sym branch `rm -f "$dest"; ln -s "$target" "$dest"` (`:2313-2314`) -- the exact non-atomic replacement #96 removed from `merge_tree`. On a client merging glibc/bash (or any package whose libraries/executables are live) this corrupts the running inode the same way: `ETXTBSY` on the running executable, silent page rewrite for mmap'd libraries. Fix: the same temp-sibling + `rename(2)` shape (the local `replace_file_atomic`/`replace_symlink_atomic` are `pub`-able for reuse or the shell gets an equivalent `mv` from a `$dest.merge.tmp.$$` sibling), plus the `mrg` variant of the merge-path safety gate (the remote driver's own glibc/bash client test). Not on the reported incident's path -- `mrg` is the opt-in remote driver -- hence filed rather than fixed with #96.

262. **DONE 2026-10-01 (batch-2026-09-29 Track M / M1: portuale `3ab54bdc`, merged `3b7b8490`; no pmtest half) — the leak is a staging-path bug, not an unscoped variable.** No portuale helper assigns an unscoped `x` in the env-save path (`bin/misc-functions.sh:388`, `bin/phase-functions.sh:798/1092/1177` all scope or `unset` it; the generated `mrg` scripts assign nothing). The `x=""` *value* is baked into the build-time `environment.bz2` shipped in the gpkg -- and the *leak* is that the regen never replaces it: `install_file_atomic`'s SSH arm staged the regen'd bytes into `TempDir::new("portuale-regen-install").keep()`, and since backlog #260 (`14456168`) `TempDir::new` **creates a directory**, so `std::fs::write` to that path fails `Is a directory (os error 21)` on every SSH install and the vdb keeps the build-time env verbatim (`!!! … merge-time environment regen failed at install, keeping build-time environment.bz2`). That is the whole intermittency: 09-26 leaked (pre-#171, no regen at all), 09-27/28 clean (regen + install worked), 09-29/30 leaked (#260 broke the staging). Filtering `x` would have masked the install failure and left the stale `buildpkg` FEATURES too, so the fix is at the source: stage into `server_dir.join("regen.env")` and clean up with `remove_dir_all`. TDD `remote::tests::install_file_atomic_ssh_stages_bytes_before_transport` watched RED (`Is a directory (os error 21)`, the exact bed error) and GREEN (the failure moves past staging to transport). Merge-path note in the commit: the client install is still `mv tmp dest` (rename(2), old inode never written). **Bed proof:** `l31-20261001T041932Z` and `l31-20261001T042840Z` both **0/0/0** -- the three `[VDB] porttest/{installmask,phases,setuid}-1.0/environment` rows are gone; `l32` 0/0/0 on 7 cells, unchanged; `l3-smoke` `l3-20261001T051513Z` hard 1 / explained 1 / 0 unexplained (glibc+bash re-merged from source, the SOURCE half of the gate); the consume-reinstall gate `l1-20261001T072430Z` rc 0, merged_count 2 (`app-shells/bash-5.3_p15`, `sys-libs/glibc-2.43-r2`), **0 hard / 0 explained / 0 unexplained**. *Original entry follows.* **OPEN (filed 2026-09-29, batch 09-28_244 Z0, `l31-20260929T152118Z` and re-run `l31-20260929T170017Z`) — the `mrg` merge path leaks a `declare -- x=""` global into the saved `environment`.** Both `l31` candidate runs of 2026-09-29 report 3 unexplained VDB rows: `porttest/{installmask,phases,setuid}-1.0/environment` carry one extra `declare -- x=""` line on the `mrg-remote` side, absent on the Portage side, which merges the same `PKGDIR`. The 09-26 runs (`l31-20260926T061121Z`, `…T061237Z`) had it too; the 09-27 and 09-28 candidates did not. It reproduced twice on 09-29, so it is not a one-off flake, and no merge-path code changed in between (`7f0fc8f3..feedb558`). 401. **DONE 2026-10-18 (batch/2026-10-18 S7: `-f`/`--fetchonly` and `-F`/`--fetch-all-uri` (implies `-f`), alone and in bundles. `--pretend` prints the `fetched` header, the resolution notice and, per distfile, one line of every candidate URI (no merge rows, no `[fetch ]` marker: Portage has none); `--ask` prompts `Would you like to fetch the source files for these packages?`; a real run prints `>>> Fetching (i of n) cpv::repo[ for ROOT]`, `>>> Downloading '<uri>'` per attempt and the ` * file HASHES size ;-) ... [ ok ]` verification line, exits 1 after `>>> Failed to emerge` on a failed fetch but goes on with the other packages, and writes nothing to the vdb, world or resume list. Oracle `LLM/evidence/2026-10-18-401-s0/`: six cells (-pf, -pF, -pfv, -f, -F, failing -f) run on Portage 3.0.82.2 and portuale over one hermetic root are identical in stdout, stderr, rc and DISTDIR, modulo the seconds portuale cuts and the trailing `/` of ROOT. Side fixes found by that diff: GENTOO_MIRRORS from `make.conf` was ignored (env only), `FETCHCOMMAND`/`RESUMECOMMAND` from the calling env were ignored, `pkg_nofetch` ran for every failed fetch (Portage: only with RESTRICT=fetch or a defined `pkg_nofetch`), the failure wording differed, `-f` demanded superuser (Portage: portage group). Residues #414, #415) — was: OPEN (filed 2026-10-17, owner request) — implement `emerge --fetchonly` (`-f`) and `--fetch-all-uri` (`-F`).** Today the options are accepted by `emerge_options.rs` but not implemented: a build action with `--fetchonly` still goes down the normal merge path, and `pretend/merge_setup.rs` notes that `--fetchonly`/`--fetch-all-uri` do not suppress the world-favorites record as Portage's `saveNomergeFavorites` gate would. Portage's behaviour to port and ground in the bed (L1/L3 with the distfiles proxy mirror): `_emerge/actions.py` (`action_build`), `Scheduler` fetch-only job path (`FetchlistTask`/`EbuildFetcher`, no phases run, no merge, no vdb write), the `--fetchonly` pretend output (`[fetch ...]` merge-list marker), exit codes on a failed fetch, and the interaction with `--resume`. Needs an oracle pass first (portage-oracle) before slicing.

408. **DONE 2026-10-18 (batch/2026-10-18 S1; the stdin diagnosis below was WRONG: the freeze comes from `process_group(0)`, not stdin; fix = `setsid` for phase scripts, the five `_global_pid_phases` stay in portuale's group, stdin `/dev/null` unless `config` or raw `PROPERTIES` names `interactive`; see `LLM/code-notes/portuale/ebuild_phases/scheduler_registry.md`, evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-10-18-408-after.md`; residues #411 signal forwarding, #412 scheduler interactive handling) — was: OPEN (filed 2026-10-18, owner report; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-18-sigttin/`) — a long `emerge -1a` build freezes forever: the build's process group gets SIGTTIN because phases inherit the terminal as stdin.** Symptom: `portuale emerge -1a <many pkgs>` (foreground in `lxc-attach` → tmux → ssh) stops mid-`compile` of `app-shells/bash-5.3_p20-r1` and waits indefinitely; every process of the phase is `T` (`do_signal_stop`), portuale sleeps on a futex. **Root cause (established from the captures, not a guess):** (1) `spawn_trackable` (`ebuild_phases/scheduler_registry.rs:88`) puts every phase in its own process group (`process_group(0)`, pgid 580605), (2) `run_phase_command` (`ebuild_phases/commands.rs:~560-593`) never sets `stdin`, so the whole phase tree keeps `/dev/pts/1` as fd 0 (`fd.580605`..`fd.619348`: `0 -> /dev/pts/1`), while the terminal foreground group stays portuale's (`psall`: TPGID 131382 for all, PGID 580605 ≠ 131382), (3) bash's own test suite (`make check` → `run-all` → `execscript` → `bash -i exec8.sub`, `cmdline.619348`) starts an interactive shell on that tty; `initialize_job_control` sees `tcgetpgrp(tty) != getpgrp()` and does `kill(0, SIGTTIN)` (gdb: `Program received signal SIGTTIN` in `__GI_kill`, `gdbinfo`), which stops the *entire* group 580605 (`ShdPnd` shows bit 21 = SIGTTIN on the bash instances, `ps` state `T`). Nothing ever moves the group to the foreground or continues it, so it hangs. It fires "at an unpredictable moment" because it needs a package whose build/tests open the tty (bash `tests`, `bash -i`, anything reading stdin/`/dev/tty`); `-a` (ask) just guarantees stdin is a tty. **Portage reference:** `_emerge/AbstractEbuildProcess.py:138-147` gives every phase except `config` and `PROPERTIES=interactive` `/dev/null` as fd 0 (`_phases_interactive_whitelist = ("config",)`). Portuale diverges. **Fix to make (one slice):** in `run_phase_command`, `cmd.stdin(Stdio::null())` unless phase is `config` or the ebuild has `PROPERTIES=interactive` (then keep the tty and make that child group the terminal foreground with `tcsetpgrp` around the wait, ignoring SIGTTOU in the parent, and restore portuale's group after). Audit the other inheriting spawns (`ebuild_phases/*`, `mrg.rs:2654/2759/2951` already null; hooks, `pkg_pretend`/`pkg_setup`, binpkg fetch/`FETCHCOMMAND` which Portage runs interactive-capable) for the same pattern. **Tests:** Rust unit test spawning a phase with a pty on fd 0 whose script does `bash -i -c true` — must finish (today it stops); with `interactive` it must get the tty in the foreground. Pretend/contract suites are unaffected (no output change). **Mitigation until fixed:** from another shell `kill -CONT -<pgid>` does not help (bash re-sends SIGTTIN); run with stdin redirected (`emerge … </dev/null` loses `--ask`) or `kill -KILL` the group. **Safety gate:** touches the phase spawn path, not the merge path; still run the bash build with tests (`FEATURES=test`) as the live check. **Residue to consider:** the running container can still yield `/proc/<pid>/stack`/`tcgetpgrp` of 99's tty if the owner wants proof the foreground group is 131382 (already shown by TPGID column).

409. **DONE 2026-10-18 (batch/2026-10-18 S2; `logical_lines` keeps a quoted trailing `\` for `varexpand` to drop, and `read_env_file_kv` now uses `logical_lines`; oracle `LLM/evidence/2026-10-18-409-s0/`; residue #410: an unquoted `a\<nl>b` gives `a` where Portage gives `a b`; the `Unable to unshare` line was not compared) — was: OPEN (filed 2026-10-18, owner report, input confirmed by owner) — a `\`+newline continuation inside a double-quoted value of a `package.env` env file leaves a literal `\` word in the value: `=app-containers/aardvark-dns-2.1.0::gentoo` builds under Portage and fails in `compile` under portuale.** Reproduce: `emerge -1 --nodeps =app-containers/aardvark-dns-2.1.0::gentoo` with `/etc/portage/package.env` mapping the package to `/etc/portage/env/llvm-lto`, which holds `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-cpu=znver3 -C strip=debuginfo -C opt-level=3 \<newline>-Clinker=clang -Clinker-plugin-lto -Clink-arg=-fuse-ld=lld"` (plus `CFLAGS="${COMMON_FLAGS}"` style expansions, `USE="${USE} lto"`). Symptom: `cargo build --release` runs `rustc … -C opt-level=3 '\' --crate-type …` and fails `multiple input filenames provided (first two filenames are `-` and `\`)`; the stray `\` is the line continuation that was not removed. Portage: `getconfig` (shlex, `portage/util/__init__.py`) removes `\<newline>` inside double quotes. portuale: the per-package env reader is `expand_package_env_files` called from `portage-profile/src/resolve/package_env.rs:44-47` (the same make.conf-style reader should be checked for the main `make.conf` too: a continued value there is likely the same bug). **Fix to make (one slice, F/M):** make the reader drop `\<newline>` in double quotes (and outside quotes), exactly as `getconfig`; check the single-quote case (kept literally) and `\<newline>` at end of an unquoted value. **Tests:** `portage-profile` unit test with the owner's `llvm-lto` file as fixture (`package_env_vars` must contain `-opt-level=3 -Clinker=clang …` with one space and no backslash) plus the same continuation in `make.conf`; expected value from Portage's `emerge --info`/`portage.util.getconfig` on the same file (name the oracle in the docstring); pmtest `emerge --info` pin. Separate, probably unrelated line in the same log: `!!! Unable to unshare (…); src_* phases run without namespace isolation` (LXC without the capability; check that Portage prints the same under the same conditions). Tier 1. [A]

Rare; fix in `logical_lines`/`shlex_token`, test from the oracle row. Tier 1. [F] **Fixed 2026-10-19:** `logical_lines` keeps a `\`+newline glued to a bare word and `shlex_token` reads it as a space (test `backslash_newline_in_an_unquoted_word_reads_as_a_space`). 425. **DONE 2026-10-20 (S-20, batch/2026-10-19; bed G-P green 2026-10-20) (filed 2026-10-19 from #421) — `emerge --resume` output differs from Portage's wording and order.** The b421 captures (`LLM/evidence/2026-10-19-b421/{portage,portuale}.txt`) show: `--resume -p` lacks the `\nThese are the packages that would be merged, in order:\n\nCalculating dependencies ... done!\nDependency resolution took N s.\n` header and prints `to <root>` without the trailing `/`; a real resume prints `*** Resuming merge...` plus `>>> Verifying ebuild manifests` where portuale prints `>>> Resuming merge of N package(s)...`; and the `Jobs: N of M complete` status lines appear where Portage shows its normal `>>> Emerging (i of n)` stream. [A] Strong review `LLM/2026-10-20-world-plan-preelaboration.strong-review.md` (2026-10-20): L3a replay unsafe (news notice, binhost refresh), review 1's `-D` re-walk fix needs a Portage oracle check, no frozen benchmark; follow-ups #454, #455. **Progress 2026-10-21:** S0 (#455) and S1 first slices shipped under #457 (see there): frozen benchmark 40.3 s -> 28.0 s wall; S2 snapshots not started. **Progress 2026-10-22 (S2 measure-first, branch `backlog/441-s0-s1`, evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-10-22-s2-measure/`):** re-profiled the frozen `emerge -uDpvN @world` abort path after S1 (wall ~24.2 s, `resolve_once` median 14.255 s, stdout hash `8eccc0a1…` identical across 6 runs): repo md5-cache reads ≈ 2% (process memos already hold; `read_md5_cache` 0 samples), VDB reads ≈ 14%, atom/dep parse+match ≈ 16% (pure CPU, not snapshot work; `parse_atom` proper 4%), `Packages` parsing 0 samples, network binhost refresh 40% (freshness+download, not legally skippable — strong review §2.1). Controls: `--no-cache`, `PORTUALE_NO_CACHE=1`, `PORTUALE_CACHE_DEBUG=1`, `PORTUALE_CACHE_VERIFY=1` (hit also recomputes fresh, exit 99 on divergence); on by default only with a configured/writable dir. 31 Rust tests (replay==fresh, one sensitivity test per key component each shown to fail with its line removed, corruption/permission/schema fallbacks, `--ask`/merge exclusion, live news, verify accept/reject); full verification + the pmtest double pass with verify owed at the time. **Verified 2026-10-22 (no product-code change):** `cargo build --release`, `fmt --check`, `clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, `cargo test --release` 31 suites ok (portuale main 1232 passed / 0 failed); pmtest plain 4262 passed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed, and the `PORTUALE_CACHE_DIR` + `PORTUALE_CACHE_VERIFY=1` double pass twice the same counts with zero exit-99 divergence and zero corpus drift (no pmtest edit needed). Frozen `@world` benchmark: cold 24.3 s, warm hit 10.1 s, no-cache 25.1 s, byte-identical stdout, abort path cached (see `what-this-proves.md` S3). Still OPEN; residue: suite-scale hit rate bounded by the 32-entry GC cap (double pass mostly re-misses on evicted keys; sampled repro proves the hit path); residue: `PORTUALE_PROFILE=1` disables the cache so warm plan part is wall-delta only.

443. **DONE 2026-10-20 (pretend cells; residues #452, #453) — record Portage and portuale terminal sessions with `app-misc/asciinema` and compare the two recordings.** Idea from the owner: asciinema records a terminal session as timestamped output events (how it works: https://docs.asciinema.org/how-it-works/; file format asciicast v3: https://docs.asciinema.org/manual/asciicast/v3/), so the same `emerge` invocation run under Portage and under portuale would give two recordings in which not only the text but also *when* each message appears (relative intervals, progress lines, spinner and status redraws, the order of stdout/stderr output) can be checked. Today the contract suite and the bed compare final bytes only, so timing and the way messages are produced are not verified. Needs: (1) a recorder wrapper for the contract suite and the bed cells (asciinema available in the bed image, or a small pty recorder that writes asciicast v3 directly); (2) a tool that compares two asciicast files: parse the header and the event stream, normalise (absolute paths, durations, PIDs), compare the text as a sequence of events, and report timing differences against a tolerance, separating a real ordering/burst difference from scheduling noise; (3) a decision on which cells are worth recording (long builds, `--resume`, keep-going notices, `--ask`) and whether timing is an assertion or only a report. **Owner answers to the open questions (2026-10-20):** (a) asciinema's pty should behave very much like the terminal `emerge` expects; (b) recording size depends on how much text is written and how much is erased, not on elapsed time, so a `--pretend` run is harmless while the full log of a ten-package merge whose builds print a lot may be unmanageable (record pretend/short cells, cap or filter build output for long merges); (c) timing is probably not stable enough to gate on, but is good enough to correlate operations and functions inside a run, so timing is a report/correlation aid, not an assertion. [A] **Done (plan `LLM/06.443-asciinema-recording.opus.md`, pmtest `scripts/cast_record.py`, `cast_compare.py`, `cast_cells.py`, tests `test_cast_tools.py`):** `asciinema rec --headless` records an argv in a 120x40 pty; the comparer reports text, output-event chunking and timing (timing never fails); `cast_cells.py` runs 8 pretend cells under Portage and portuale on staged fixtures and writes `report.md`. First result: exit codes agree on 8/8, but under a pty the text and chunking differ on every cell (Portage prints the spinner, banner, terminal title and about 60 writes; portuale one write) — this is what the byte-compare suites could not see.

452. **DONE 2026-10-20 (branch `backlog/452-long-cells`; plan `LLM/06.452-long-cell-recording.opus.md`; residue #456) — record long cells.** `pmtest/scripts/cast_cells.py --cells long|all` records seven real-merge recordings of fixture packages under both PMs (`sudo --preserve-env`, scratch `PORTAGE_TMPDIR`/`PKGDIR`, `FEATURES="-strict -sandbox …"` because the fixtures carry no Manifest): `--keep-going`, stop at the first failure, a failing dependency, a clean multi-package merge, `--resume` (two recordings in one stage) and `--ask` answered `n` and `y`. Build output is capped (`cast_compare.cap_cast`, default 20000 bytes per cast, head and tail kept, one marker for the middle, `--cap-bytes 0` = off); `report.md` carries a landmark timeline per cell (`>>> Emerging`, `>>> Install`, `Failed to emerge`, `>>> Completed`, `*** Resuming`, failure summary) with seconds since start for both PMs — a report, never a gate. `--ask` needs a real terminal on stdin, so `cast_record.record_with_answers` runs non-headless asciinema inside an outer pty and types the reply when the prompt regex matches. Bed image: `PMTEST_CAST=1` makes `lib.sh` bind-mount the host's `asciinema` read-only into the guest (the image has none; the host's glibc binary runs there unchanged). [A]

453. **DONE 2026-10-20 (branch pty-output-453; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-10-20-b453/`) — tty-only output differences checked against Portage and the four real gaps fixed.** Checked against Portage 3.0.82.2 source and live probes (`oracle.md`): the spinner animation is not observable in a recording, and the banner, the `Calculating dependencies ... done!` line, the timing line and the global-updates pass print the same on a pipe (the global-updates repeat is a pmtest staging artifact). Fixed: (A) colour of the abort banner, the timing line and the masked/missing-dependency blocks under `--color=y` (`timing_line`, `abort_preamble` in `pretend/merge_list.rs`; `problem_reports.rs`); (B) `--nospinner` accepted as a no-op; (C) the terminal-title escape on stderr at start and exit, under Portage's conditions (`crates/portuale/src/title.rs`); (D) the leading blank line before the banner on an aborted resolve (a one-newline drift in 340 corpus entries, blessed in the pmtest commit). Pinned by seven new tests in `test_emerge_pretend_contract.py` plus Rust unit tests. Recorded cuts: the `for <root>/` suffix, and portuale's backtrack count on `missingdep` (0/20 against Portage's 1/20). 475. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-22 from #441/#457) — the `-D` second walk and the remaining walk-inherent cost.** `resolve_phases.rs` re-walks with seeds when `auto_enable` is set; skipping or sharing that walk needs the ResolverPlayground oracle first (strong review §2.2: the seeded walk exists because the unseeded one can misfire a probe). **Oracle check 2026-10-22 (`LLM/evidence/2026-10-22-d-rewalk-oracle/`): the second walk cannot just be dropped.** Portage never walks twice (`_complete_graph` extends the same graph); portuale's second walk is seeded (`with_seeds=true`), so it is not a replay. Keeping the unseeded result changes output on 42 of 666 contract-suite hits (23 tests fail). Seeding the first walk and skipping the second is green on the suite but changes the host `@world` abort stdout (extra karchive block) and needs an unseeded fallback that costs ~14 s on the frozen benchmark; the frozen benchmark never runs the second walk anyway (first-walk abort), and the world-shaped bench gains only ~0.22 s (21%). Decision: not worth doing; only the walk-inherent items below remain. Remaining profile after #457: `record_unresolved_dep` ~11%, `enqueue_dependencies` ~8%, binary pool builds ~6%, `vdb_aux_get` ~6%, `parse_atom` ~5%. Tier 2. [A]

Pins: oracle cell `-pu --deep=1 dev-libs/instusedepconsumer` (L0 fixture oracle, parity), contract test `test_update_keeps_installed_dependency_when_newer_tree_candidate_lacks_the_use_dep_flag`, unit `update_keeps_an_installed_use_dep_satisfier_the_tree_version_lacks` (fails without the fix) and `backtracker_feedback_slot_conflict_masks_the_installed_instance_too`. 483. **DONE 2026-10-22 (branch `backlog/477-world-trajectory`; filed from #482) — `@world` ends with an unsatisfied soft block `plasma-login-sessions` vs `plasma-workspace-6.8.0` where Portage schedules `[uninstall] plasma-login-sessions-6.7.5` and reports a resolved block `b`.** The tree has no plasma-login-sessions 6.8.0, the installed `plasma-meta-6.7.5` still requires `>=plasma-login-sessions-6.7.5:6[X]`, and `plasma-workspace-6.8.0` has `!kde-plasma/plasma-login-sessions`. Portage (Portage 3.0.82.2, `LLM/evidence/2026-10-22-world-divergence/portage.txt` L354-355) unmerges the installed package to resolve the soft block; portuale prints `[blocks B ]` with no uninstall, on every `@world` run since #476. *Original question:* why Portage may uninstall a package an installed, kept parent depends on (complete-graph rule, `_validate_blockers` / `_dynamic_config._blocker_uninstalls`), and where portuale's block resolver refuses. Tier 1. [A] **Fixed:** the cause was `ResolveCtx::blocker_retry_closure`, the required sets' forward-installed closure, built from the installed state before any merge: installed `plasma-meta-6.7.5` and `plasma-workspace-6.7.5-r1` (both replaced by 6.8.0, which no longer needs `plasma-login-sessions`) kept it "reachable", so the closure veto in `blocker_unsolvable` called the block unsolvable. Portage's `_validate_blockers` (`depgraph.py:9216-9224`) asks for a digraph node with parents, and a replaced installed package is not in the graph. The veto now also needs a live parent: `live_closure_targets_of` (`blockers.rs`) takes the targets of the closure members this run does not merge (`InstalledReachability::targets_of`, which ignores blockers and self-edges: the installed `plasma-login-sessions` itself carries `!plasma-login-sessions:5`). Pin: pmtest `f483-*` fixtures and `test_f483_soft_block_uninstalls_a_target_whose_parent_is_replaced_like_portage` (Portage-sourced rows). Host `@world` now equals Portage on all 375 package blocks. Residue #488.
484. **DONE 2026-10-22 (branch `backlog/477-world-trajectory`; filed from #482) — `@world` rebuilt `app-misc/mime-types` and upgraded `sys-firmware/edk2-bin` where Portage keeps both.** Two causes, both fixed. (1) mime-types: a merge-bound binary (build 1, `-nginx`) and the installed twin of the same cpv (build 2, `nginx`) are two packages in one slot for Portage; the installed node's parent `www-servers/nginx` wants `[nginx]`, so the slot conflict removes the binary. Portuale never compared the use-deps of an `AlreadyInstalled` visit with the merge node of the same version (`merge_instance_fails_use_deps`, `unresolved_dep.rs`); the direct solve then removed the binary but printed a skipped-update block Portage does not print when the kept installed build is newer (`Package.__gt__` compares `BUILD_TIME` for two built packages): `installed_twin_is_newer_build`, `direct_solve.rs`. The notice now also names `binary` and the build id (`SkippedUpdate::skipped_build_id`, `GraphEntry::build_time`). (2) edk2-bin: Portage's `find_existing_node` reuses an installed node that an earlier parent already put in the graph (qemu's baked `~edk2-bin-202408`) for a later bare atom, including a `||`-chosen one (`graph_installed_reuse`, `pass_helpers.rs`, applied in `outcome_fallbacks.rs`). Both are Portage quirks, documented in `LLM/portage-quirks.md` Q1 and Q2. The filed `gpep517-23-1 [binary Rg]` gap and the meson/nginx binary-vs-ebuild difference (#485) were artifacts of #487 (Portage could not read the `[gentoo]` binhost index); both disappear with a sound oracle. The mime-types and edk2-bin divergences are real: the pre-fix binary still differs from Portage on the repaired host `@world`. Pins: pmtest `f484-*` and `f484b-*` fixtures. On the repaired oracle the host `@world` now differs from Portage only in #483 (soft block `b`/`B`, plasma-login-sessions) and the libdisplay-info skipped-update block (S4). [A]

Tier 1. 487. **DONE 2026-10-22 (branch `backlog/477-world-trajectory`) — portuale corrupted the shared binhost index cache, which hid the `[gentoo]` binhost from Portage.** `stamp_download_timestamp` (`emerge_getbinpkg.rs`) cut the fetched `Packages` text before the header's blank separator and then appended the rest, so the cached file had two blank lines after the header. Portage's `PackageIndex.read` takes the second blank line as the end of the index and reads zero packages (17491 entries became 0), then keeps the cached copy for `TTL` (3600 s) without any message. Every Portage run on this host for up to an hour after each portuale `--getbinpkg` refresh therefore ignored the `[gentoo]` binrepo, and the reference outputs captured in that window (today's `portage.txt`, `pd.out`) showed ebuilds where Portage with a sound cache uses binaries. Fixed by cutting after the header's last newline; unit test `stamping_the_download_timestamp_keeps_one_blank_line`. Repaired the host cache by removing the extra blank line. Lesson for oracle captures: check that Portage reads every binrepo (`[<name>] Local copy ...` lines on stderr, and a package count) before trusting a capture; recorded in memory `portage-oracle-binhost-cache`. [A]
488. **DONE 2026-10-22 (branch `backlog/477-world-trajectory`; filed from #483) — the `[uninstall]` row lacks the `USE="..."` Portage prints with `-v`.** Host `@world`: Portage `[uninstall     ] kde-plasma/plasma-login-sessions-6.7.5:6::gentoo  USE="X wayland" `, portuale the same row without the USE part (`print_uninstall_row`, `pretend/display/entry_line.rs`). Portage runs `_display_use` for every package including uninstall rows (`resolver/output.py` `Display.__call__`), from the installed instance's IUSE/USE, with no change markers (the instance is its own previous package). Needs a fixture with an installed package that has IUSE, then the vdb USE rendering shared with the other rows. Tier 1. [S] **Fixed:** `fill_uninstall_displays` (`use_display.rs`, called after `file_blocker_conflicts` in both resolve paths) renders the removed instance's vdb `IUSE`/`USE` (no change markers, force/mask parens kept) and `print_uninstall_row` appends `use_suffix`. Pin: `f483-t` vdb now has `IUSE="bar foo"`, `USE="foo"`; Portage 3.0.82.2 prints `USE="foo -bar" `. The #68 B2c control pin gained `USE="flip"` (it had pinned the missing part).

489. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-22; owner decision on `LLM/portage-quirks.md` Q1) — `portuale mrg` should always pick the newest visible version, not reuse an installed node already in the graph.** `portuale emerge` copies Portage's `find_existing_node` reuse (`graph_installed_reuse`, `pass_helpers.rs`) so the merge list matches; `mrg` may differ, and the owner chose to diverge: independent of walk order, a bare atom resolves to the newest visible candidate, a slot conflict with an installed parent's pin then yields the usual skipped-update notice. Needs the `mrg` applet switch for the resolver option, fixture `f484b-*` as the case. Q2 (BUILD_TIME-dependent notice) stays copied by `mrg` (decided). Tier 1. [A]

Portage's `_complete_graph` only follows deps of graph nodes. With the conflict gone the libdisplay-info block prints; its text now matches Portage byte for byte (build id on an installed binary parent `weston-16.0.0-1`, no ` in '/'` at ROOT=/, forced `(64)` on installed parents, atom-long `^` marker with the violated `[nginx]` span). `causing rebuilds` rows carry the build id too. Residue: Portage prints the skipped-update block on stderr, portuale on stdout (pre-existing). Tier 1. [A]

[B] **OPEN — the rest of the trajectory; analysed 2026-10-23, not fixed.** Per-pass inputs (Portage `--debug`, `/var/tmp/s0/kdb/pd.out`; portuale `PORTUALE_BT_TRACE=1`, `u4.trace`): Portage's pass 0 already holds `libplasma` 6.8.0 (binary, from polkit-kde-agent) and the installed 6.7.5 (kept by the installed `:6/7=` consumers) in one slot, so it reports slot conflicts on `libplasma`, `plasma-activities`, `kio`, `kwallet*`, `kservice`, `ki18n` and more, and `_slot_operator_trigger_reinstalls` then runs the missed-slot-ABI probe on every installed consumer (`want_update` is true under `-uD`). Portage plans the same binary first, then its slot-conflict handler removes it (`Removing package: mime-types-2.1.54-1`) and prints the conflict as a notice with rc 0; portuale prints no conflict and keeps the row. #484 fixed this shape for `@world` (`merge_instance_fails_use_deps`, `unresolved_dep.rs`, which compares an `AlreadyInstalled` visit's use deps with the merge node), but here `nginx` is not walked by the request, so there is no visit to compare: the use deps of the installed consumers the complete graph holds must reach the same check. Premise: confirmed 2026-11-09 by running both sides on the host tree. Resolution: two causes. (1) `-D` re-walks the complete-mode pass with `complete = false`, so the USE-break table never filled; the scan now reads `complete || portage_complete`. (2) A USE-break pin of a same-version reinstall nobody requested by name is now enforced (the installed twin meets every consumer, so the reinstall is held back, as Portage's direct solve removes the merge instance); a requested package keeps the conflict block and rc 1 (`UseBreakSource::reinstall`, `rev_dep.rs`). The pin's mask now reads the entry's displayed USE, so a binary's baked `-nginx` counts (`merge_use_meets_pin`). Pin: pmtest `test_f695_*` (the `f484` fixtures, @world extended with `f484-ng`); unit test `reverse_dependency_constraints_enforces_a_use_break_when_only_a_dependency_pulls_the_reinstall`. Fix direction: key the binary mask by build id (`MaskEntry` for binary instances) and let the binary candidate list skip only that build. The trace also shows a plain `!=pipewire-1.6.11` missing-dep trigger for the same parent (`missing_dep.rs`), which Portage keys by package object too; it needs the same per-build treatment, and the contract fixtures need a multi-build binary (BUILD_ID 3 and 4) to pin it. Premise: confirmed 2026-11-09 by running both sides on the host tree. Tier 3.

Real's `_slot_operator_trigger_reinstalls` is gated by `_allow_backtracking` (`depgraph.py:2131-2132`), cleared by `--backtrack=0` (`:12188-12190`), so with backtracking off neither the rebuilds nor the provider update happen; E0's real `--debug` confirms it (the bt0 cell has no `causing rebuilds` output, bt20 has it). **T13 correction:** the original entry claimed real's resting behaviour "relies on the built-atom dynamic-deps model (#26) which portuale does not model" -- portuale *does* model it (`installed_dep_string` + `built_slot_operator_atoms`, unit-tested) and gates it behind `PORTUALE_DYNAMIC_DEPS_APPEND`. E0's control experiment (host-exact slotop fixtures, both sides, portuale with and without the flag) gives **verdict (b)**: the flag does not reproduce real -- with `--backtrack=0` it only re-tags the provider (`r U`) and leaves the consumers unbuilt; with backtracking on it makes portuale print an **empty plan** where real rebuilds the three consumers and upgrades the provider -- and both #24 pins named in the gate's comment fail with it on (`slotchange-case4`'s libarchive `rR` and `slotundo-cascade`'s soccascb `rR` disappear). So the item is not the append gate; it needs the #26-family slot-op probe ordering (the gate's own doc comment already names it), re-scoped there rather than fixed by flipping the default. **Review refinement (2026-09-18, `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.4):** E0's "real's empty list is the backtracking gate, not a dynamic-deps model difference" is stronger than its evidence. `_allow_backtracking` explains why real schedules no *rebuilds*; it does not explain why real does not *upgrade the provider*, which portuale does in the same bt0 cell even with the append on (`r U`). Suite: 1724 passed (only the 3 pre-existing movepkg-dirt failures), Rust workspace green, clippy/fmt clean. **Residue (walk-sequence, NOT scheduler-verdict):** real leads the p2b tree with the merge owner and trails a nested new-slot owner occurrence, and nests the dep child as `[N]` where portuale nests `[U]`; u6 differs by the `blockerpkg-1.0` line position only (multislotparent is byte-identical).
