Building the guard fixture exposed the larger half: `print_tree`'s `required_by` fallback for a deps-less entry is cp-keyed and `required_by` lists every owner that pulled *any* slot of the cp, so a slot an owner never asked for gained that owner as a tree parent; the fallback now skips an owner whose own atom already resolved to another instance. Container oracle (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#82"): `--tree dev-libs/slotorderroot` is byte-identical to real 3.0.82.2 for the first time (the slice's one corpus drift, reviewed against that capture and blessed), pinned by `test_oracle_82_tree_edges_follow_the_resolved_dep_target`; new fixtures `dev-libs/treeslotpkg`/`treeslotparent`/`treeslotuser` + two `merge_order` unit tests pin the vercmp tie-break and the `merge_bound_only` split. The invariant checker's `--tree` row dedupe is gone (dead: `rows_key` already drops every `[nomerge]` row) with `test_checker_flags_a_merge_row_printed_twice_in_tree_mode` as the guard; the bidirectional `hidden_path_to` is **kept** — its reason is the unparsed `[blocks …]`/`[uninstall …]` rows collapsing the parsed depth (B0b's p2c), not the `[nomerge]` rows, and the comment now says so. Bed 28 probes / 22 clean / 18 explained / 0 unexplained. Residue: backlog **#85** (the two new slots merge in the opposite order, so the `treeslotuser` tree cannot be byte-pinned). Original entry follows. **`print_tree`'s edge selection is a drifted second copy of `build_digraph`'s, and the tree cross-mode invariant was weakened (#75 C1 review residue).** Two small, independent defects found reviewing the `backlog/75-79` merge (`docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.1/§7.2). (a) `pretend.rs::print_tree` re-implements `build_digraph`'s dependency-edge narrowing instead of reusing it, and ranks the tie with a **string** compare (`jv.cmp(bv) == Ordering::Greater`, `pretend.rs:1853`) where `merge_order.rs:1772` uses `portage_versions::vercmp` — so `1.9` outranks `1.10` and slot `9` outranks slot `10`, and any atom matching two merge-bound entries of the same `cp` can nest under the wrong instance in tree mode; no pin catches it because the only committed multi-slot shape is `1.0`/`2.0`, where the two comparisons agree. Its `merge_bound` tie-break arms are also dead code (the candidate loop already skips non-merge-bound entries). Fix: export `build_digraph`'s narrowing as one shared helper, the way `#76 B1` did for `suppressed_alt_edges` — that precedent is exactly why the `||` selection cannot drift today — or at minimum swap in `vercmp` and delete the dead arms, with a multi-slot `9`/`10` fixture as the guard. (b) `tests/output_invariants.py::check_cross_mode`'s `if mode == "--tree": got = sorted(set(got))` is unnecessary: `rows_key` already filters `kind != "nomerge"`, so the ancestor occurrence never reaches `got`; removing the two lines leaves `tests/test_output_invariants.py` + `tests/test_emerge_pretend_contract.py` green (1222 passed, 5 xfailed), and keeping it hides exactly the duplicate-row bug the invariant exists to catch. The same function's `hidden_path_to(a,b) or hidden_path_to(b,a)` relaxation *is* needed, but should be narrowed to the `[nomerge]` ancestor rows that motivate it. Evidence: `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.

83. **DONE 2026-09-18 (branch `backlog/75-79-review-merged`) — the all-installed blocker scan makes a narrow `emerge -p` slower, and re-runs on every backtracking pass (#77 A1 review residue).** Profiled first (`TEST/findings/l0.md` "#83"): on `-p --oneshot app-editors/nano`, where the walk reaches four packages and the scan visits 2084, the cost is two halves — 391 ms in `list_candidates` locating each package's live ebuild metadata and 435 ms in `use_reduce_flat_disjunctive` (57 ms in the vdb reads, 0.5 ms listing the vdb). The reduce is now skipped when the dep string has no `!`: a blocker token always begins with one (`portage_dep::Blocker`) and `use_reduce` drops and expands tokens but never rewrites them, so the filter is exact; 1804 of the 2084 packages on this host are skipped. **1.22–1.30 s → 0.99–1.01 s** on `nano` (real 3.0.82.2: 2.23–2.61 s for the same argv), 13.86 → 13.3 s on `-puDvN --oneshot app-containers/runc`, ~57 → ~54 s on `@world`. Two cheap guards from the parallel review join it: the scan returns before touching the vdb when **no** entry is merge-bound (every row it could produce is `owner_installed`, so `resolve_blockers` would take the `!owner_merging && installed_match && !merge_bound_match` early `continue` on all of them -- an exact shortcut, not an approximation), and the walked-cp test is a `HashSet` instead of a linear `entries` scan per installed package (O(installed x entries) -> O(installed)). Neither moves this host's numbers (both shapes measured here have merge-bound entries and are I/O-bound), so they are complexity removal, not a measured win. A third variant of the `!` prefilter was assessed and **rejected**: reading the raw vdb `IDEPEND`/`PDEPEND`/`RDEPEND` *before* the live tree lookup would skip `list_candidates` too, but real reads these through the `FakeVartree` dbapi (`depgraph.py:9016` `vardb.aux_get`), whose `_apply_dynamic_deps` substitutes the **live ebuild's** deps under the default `--dynamic-deps=y` (`FakeVartree.py:142-146`) -- so an ebuild revised after the install to add a blocker is a blocker real sees and that prefilter would not. The cost it targets is #86's, and #86 removes it without the fidelity loss. Two further mitigations were measured and deliberately not taken: hoisting `PassState::installed_meta_memo` into `ResolveCtx` buys ~57 ms per extra pass (~170 ms on the four-pass `runc` probe) for a `RefCell` borrow across four call sites, and the residual `list_candidates` cost is the cold fill of its own process-global cache, free from the second pass on. The filing's "~4x" headline is corrected by the numbers: the scan is work real does too, and portuale answers the same narrow query in under half real's time. Residue: backlog **#86** (follow real's `myrepo=pkg.repo` instead of searching the repos). Original entry follows. **The all-installed blocker scan makes a narrow `emerge -p` ~4x slower, and re-runs on every backtracking pass (#77 A1 review residue).** `#77 A1`'s cost note ("56.2 s -> 58.3 s") measured the one shape where `collect_unwalked_installed_blockers` is nearly free: a complete-mode `@world` walk already has an entry for almost every installed package, so the scan's skip filter fires at once (measured 4.4–5.0 ms/pass). Instrumented on this host at `f818d1d`: `-p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano` goes **0.32 s -> 1.26 s** (854 ms of scan in a single pass), `-puDvN --oneshot app-containers/runc` 12.65 s -> 14.15 s (282–290 ms x 4 passes), `sys-fs/udisks` 9.63 s -> 11.01 s. The per-pass repetition is structural: `run_pass` runs once per `Backtracker` iteration (up to `--backtrack`, default 20) and `PassState::installed_meta_memo` is per-pass. Three behaviour-preserving mitigations, in order: (1) hoist the metadata memo into `ResolveCtx` so passes 2..N reuse it; (2) `if !depstr.contains('!') { continue; }` before the token split — a blocker token always starts with `!` and `use_reduce` never introduces one, so it is exact; prototyped here it cuts the recurring pass 282 ms -> 149 ms and `nano`'s first pass 854 ms -> 676 ms (280 of 2088 installed packages carry a `!` in `IDEPEND PDEPEND RDEPEND` at all); (3) if that is not enough, cache the scan's blocker-token output per installed instance for the process lifetime — the only entries-dependent part is `disjunction_preference`, which matters solely for a blocker inside a `|| ( … )` group. Evidence: `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.3.

84. **DONE 2026-09-18 (branch `backlog/75-79-review-merged`) — the kept-`|| ( … )`-branch set was re-derived once per blocker row (`#76 B2` review residue).** `replacement_wait_index` (`pretend.rs`) called `portage_repo::kept_alt_branches` on every invocation, and `blocker_row_disposition` invokes it once per `Replacement` row from four walks (`collect_inline_blocker_lines`, `trailing_blocker_lines`, `count_blocker_rows`, the `--tree` node walk), so one row paid the O(graph) derivation four times over. The derivation itself was never wrong — it shares `suppressed_alt_edges` with `build_digraph`, so the #53 self-branch exclusion cannot drift — only its placement. `kept_alt_for_display` now derives it once per **walk** and threads `&[HashSet<usize>]` down into `replacement_wait_index`, which reads a missing entry as "suppressed" through `get` (so an empty set cannot panic); the `print_entry_line` call site passes `&[]` outright, since only `unsolvable` rows reach it and `resolve_blockers` writes `satisfied_by: None` whenever `unsolvable`. **The hoist is per walk, not per list:** `trailing_blocker_lines` is called once per display entry from `print_entry_line`, so deriving at list level instead paid it once per entry — measured here as a 12.4 s -> 15.8 s regression on `emerge -p @world` before the gate was narrowed to the rows each walk actually dispositions. With that gate the cost is at most four derivations per run and zero when no `Replacement` row exists, against the old once-per-row-per-walk; no measurable change on this host's shapes (`-p @world` 12.3 s, `-p --oneshot app-editors/nano` 0.99-1.00 s, `-puDvN @world` 53.5-54.0 s, all within noise of the pre-slice build), so this is an algorithmic ceiling removed, not a measured win. Pin: `kept_alt_for_display_is_derived_once_and_shared` (the shared set equals the derivation it replaces; the owner-restricted form derives only for the owner carrying the row; the kept branch waits and the empty set hides). Evidence: this commit; plan `docs/history/02.75-79.md` §7.6.

85. **DONE 2026-09-18 — S0 proved this is not the #17 wall: real's `_minimize_children`.** Real's `--debug` digraph dump shows `treeslotuser` has NO edge to 1.10: `_minimize_children` (`depgraph.py:4751-4856`) eliminates the redundant 1.10 selection (`:0` matches only 1.9, the bare atom matches both), so the bias ties 1-1 and discovery order decides. Ported into the shared `select_dep_target` (both `build_digraph` and `print_tree` via `resolved_dep_targets`); NVC entries are excluded from elimination (they are never selected packages -- real yields `(atom, None)`), preserving the `opartlya` disclosure order the elimination otherwise flips. The bed cell is byte-equal flat and tree; the `treeslot-new-slot-merge-order` allowlist entry is deleted. Pins: two `merge_order` unit tests + `test_oracle_85_unqualified_atom_collapses_onto_the_slot_qualified_target` (flat `-v` and `--tree` byte pins). Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #85. Original entry follows. **Two new slots of one cp, pulled by different parents, merge in the opposite order (#82 residue).** Found while building #82's tree fixture, and the reason that fixture gets no byte pin. `dev-libs/treeslotuser` pulls `dev-libs/treeslotpkg:0` (`1.9`), `dev-libs/treeslotparent` (which pulls `:1`, `1.10`) and a slot-unqualified `dev-libs/treeslotpkg`; real 3.0.82.2 merges `1.9, 1.10, treeslotparent, treeslotuser` while portuale's `build_digraph` discovery order reaches the slot-1 node first and emits `1.10, 1.9`. Set, versions, flags and `Total:` all match — only the sequence differs, the standing #17 merge-order class. It propagates into `--tree`, whose display list is the reversed merge order: after #82 the tree *edge set* is real's (`treeslotparent`'s only child is `1.10`), but the nesting still differs because the walk meets the slots in the other order. Capture: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#82", bed cell `-v dev-libs/treeslotuser` with the `treeslot-new-slot-merge-order` allowlist entry (`owner: portuale-bug`), which is deleted when this closes.

86. **DONE 2026-09-18 — installed-package live metadata comes from the vdb-recorded repository.** `live_metadata_for_installed` threads the recorded `repository` through all four `installed_dep_string` call sites (scan, resolver loop, `--deep` recursion, merge-order closure) as one shared helper, replacing `list_candidates` priority search; missing/removed recorded repo falls back to the old search (minimal fixture vdbs predate the file), and a version gone from the recorded repo falls back to Raw. Pins: `live_metadata_for_installed_prefers_the_recorded_repo` unit test + `tworepoowner`/`tworepotarget` two-repo fixtures + `test_oracle_86_installed_metadata_comes_from_the_recorded_repo` (old binary printed a spurious uninstall+b pair; fixed binary and real print the clean upgrade). Timing re-measured 2026-09-18 on the #83 probe shape (`-p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano`, this host, interleaved warm runs): 0.99-1.01 s -> **0.83-0.85 s** (cold first run 4.09 s), against real 3.0.82.2's 2.23-2.61 s -- a real win, below the 0.60 s upper bound exactly as predicted (the faithful single-file read keeps more than the unfaithful skip). Order-bed re-runs pending (no container image in this environment; see the close-out). Plan: [`history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md`](history/02.78-87-tier2-closeout.md) #86. Original entry follows. **The live ebuild metadata of an installed package is found by searching the repos, not by its recorded repository (#83 residue).** Real's `FakeVartree._aux_get_wrapper` passes `myrepo=pkg.repo` — the vdb-recorded `repository` — straight to `portdb.aux_get` (`FakeVartree.py:136`), so the dynamic-deps view of an installed package always comes from the repo it was built from. Portuale resolves it with `list_candidates(repos, cat, pkg)` + `max_by_key(repo_priority)` at every `installed_dep_string` call site (`collect_unwalked_installed_blockers`, `enqueue_dependencies`, the `--deep` display-list recursion), which differs whenever the same version exists in two repos and the recorded one is not the highest-priority. It is also the single biggest remaining cost of the #83 scan: a `read_dir` per installed package (391 ms for 2084 on this host) where the recorded repository would need one file read (~5 ms total). The prize is measured, not estimated: a third review branch (`backlog/r01oc`) skipped the per-package lookup outright — by the unfaithful raw-record route this item exists to replace — and `emerge -p --ignore-default-opts --oneshot app-editors/nano` went **0.99-1.00 s -> 0.60 s** on this host, interleaved, three runs each. That is the win available here faithfully. Fidelity change, so it needs its own oracle — a two-repo fixture where the same cpv exists in both, with the vdb `repository` naming the lower-priority one — and all the call sites must move together. Evidence: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#83".

USE=""`) and resolves rc 0. Fix direction: evaluate `=`-suffixed USE deps of installed parents against the candidate's built USE (vdb for installed, built metadata for binaries, profile-folded USE for ebuilds) at selection time, not display time. **Owner decision O3 gates any code**: the fix changes portuale's exit code on the live tree (rc 0 → rc 1 on `net-libs/rest`, `sys-devel/gcc`), so every affected pin moves in the same slice. 167. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G2 S1 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, run `l32-20260926T073144Z`; review fix round 1, 2026-09-26) — portuale writes `/var/lib/portage/preserved_libs_registry` unconditionally, with real-divergent JSON, and with the wrong owner cpv / path order.** Real `PreservedLibsRegistry.store()` is `json.dumps(..., indent="\t", sort_keys=True)` and rewrites the file only when the registry changes: with no preserved-lib activity the packaged empty file stays **0 bytes** (C1 `-C`/`--depclean`, C3 `CONFIG_PROTECT`, C4 `--resume`), and an empty registry real *does* write is exactly `{}` (**2 bytes**, no trailing newline; C2 soname bump). Portuale's hand-rolled `ebuild_merge::write_plib_registry` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_merge.rs:1116`) is called unconditionally from `register_preserved_libs` (`:1414`), `unregister_preserved_libs` (`:1203`) and `prune_unused_preserved_libs` (`:1594`), so every merge/unmerge rewrites the file even when nothing is preserved, and it emits `"{\n"` … `"}\n"`, so an empty registry is `{\n}\n` (**4 bytes**) and a non-empty one carries a trailing newline real omits. The comparator reports `SIZE /var/lib/portage/preserved_libs_registry` in all four cells (`0 vs 4` in C1/C3/C4, `2 vs 4` in C2). **The C2 *content* is the second half of the same class, and it was triaged in review fix round 1 (it is the exact shape the C2 cell exists to check).** After the soname bump, portuale's `preserved_libs_registry-after.txt` records the owner cpv `l32/sonamelib-1.0` (the pre-bump cpv, which no longer owns the files) and the path order `[…/libl32soname.so.1, …/libl32soname.so.1.0.0]`, while real records owner `l32/sonamelib-2.0` and the **reversed** order `[…/libl32soname.so.1.0.0, …/libl32soname.so.1]` — exactly the S0 oracle's blessed keys (`findings/l5.md` `## Group 2`: "the owner cpv `l32/sonamelib-2.0`; the path list order (`.1.0.0` then `.1`)"). The comparator surfaces only the size row for this file (the size difference dominates); the content divergence was found by inspecting both `preserved_libs_registry-after.txt` files. Fix: write only on an actual registry change, serialize empty as `{}` (and drop the trailing newline), assign the registry entry to the **new** (post-bump) cpv, and order the preserved path list `…so.1.0.0` before `…so.1` — the same registry and the same `write_plib_registry`/`register_preserved_libs` call family, hence one item, not a sibling. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l5.md` "## #32 S1" class A, and `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l32-20260926T073144Z/C2/candidate/{portuale,portage}.logs/preserved_libs_registry-after.txt`. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `2d2246ec`; gate `l1-20260926T224350Z` green and `l32-20260926T230337Z` shows the SIZE row gone C1–C4+F2 with C2 content equal. [I]

168. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G2 S1 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, run `l32-20260926T073144Z`) — `--resume` is dead after a SIGKILL: the resume list is never written before the merge.** Real's `Scheduler._save_resume_list` records `mtimedb["resume"]` (`favorites` reversed, the full `mergelist`, `myopts`) at merge start, so a `kill -9` mid-merge leaves it and `emerge --resume` (and `--resume --pretend`) replay from the start of the list with rc 0, completing all three packages. Portuale only calls `mtimedb::write_resume_list` on a *clean failure* (`rust/portuale/src/pretend.rs:5085`, `:13108`, `:13146`) and rotates an existing list to `resume_backup` before a fresh merge (`:13002`); nothing is persisted before the first merge executes, so the C4 kill left `mtimedb` with no `resume` key (`resume key present: False`). Both `emerge --resume --pretend` and `emerge --resume` then exit 1 with `emerge: could not find a valid resume list` (`pretend.rs:4928`) and `l32/slow-c` is never merged (vdb 2 vs real's 3; comparator row `MISSING /usr/share/l32/slow-c`). `read_resume_list`/`run_resume` already replay a saved list — the gap is the up-front save. Fix: persist the resolved plan's resume list (`favorites`/`mergelist`/`myopts`, with each entry's `ResumeEntryKind`) into `mtimedb` before the first merge runs. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l5.md` "## #32 S1" class B. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `8f60c891`; gate `l1-20260926T224828Z` green, with C4 proof on `l32-20260927T052653Z` and on the #177 tip `l32-20260927T061016Z` (resume key present, `--resume` rc 0, 0 unexplained). [I]

169. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G1 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, candidate run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) — remote merge drops `setuid`/`setgid` (and `sticky` for a non-root client); the receive-stage `tar -xf` is only one of the two sites.** The bed's candidate pair (`mrg --remote-binpkg` over a loopback sshd into the container ROOT; run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) reports `[MODE] /usr/bin/pt-setuid` `4711` → `711` and `[MODE] /usr/bin/pt-setgid` `2755` → `755`; `pt-sticky` survives `1750`. Re-deriving S0's boundary overturned its single-site localization. **(a) Root client:** the receive `tar -xf "$BUNDLE"` preserves all three bits, and the loss is the merge copy driver's order — `MERGE_FLOW`'s obj branch does `chmod --reference "$src" "$tmp"` **then** `chown --reference "$src" "$tmp"` (`rust/portuale/src/remote.rs`), and `chown(2)` clears `S_ISUID`/`S_ISGID` on a regular file even when the owner is unchanged (the sticky bit is not cleared): final `0711`/`0755`/`1750`. **(b) Non-root client** (S0's `uid 1000` host capture): the receive `tar -xf` **without `-p`** strips all three at extraction (`4711/2755/1750` → `711/755/750`; `tar -xp` keeps them), and the copy stage never sees them. Both sites are product bugs: add `-p` to `unpack_script`'s `tar -xf`, and re-chmod (or chown before chmod) in the obj/sym branches. Read-only reproduction in the bed image: `cp -p` alone and `cp -p` + `chmod --reference` keep `4711/2755/1750`; adding `chown --reference` yields `711/755/1750`; `chown` then `chmod` keeps all three. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l4.md` "## #31 S2"; `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l31-20260926T061556Z/candidate/mrg.files.tsv` (`pt-setuid 711`, `pt-sticky 1750`). **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track L): portuale `171f2abb`; gate `l1-20260926T223731Z` green and `l31-20260926T225952Z` shows the `[MODE]` rows gone. [I]

170. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G1 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, candidate run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) — remote vdb never writes Portage's merge-time aux files `BINPKGMD5` and `INSTALL_MASK`.** Four VDB rows (both packages): present for the reference real Portage, absent for `mrg`. Neither file is in the gpkg — the gpkg `metadata/` (= the shipped `build-info/`) carries `BUILD_ID`/`FEATURES`/`environment.bz2`/… but not these two (verified on `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/_l31-pkgcache/porttest/setuid/setuid-1.0-1.gpkg.tar`). Real Portage synthesizes both at merge: `BINPKGMD5` = the md5 of the source archive actually merged, `INSTALL_MASK` = the resolved client config at merge (real capture writes `BINPKGMD5` 33 B and `INSTALL_MASK` 82 B = `/usr/share/porttest/im/drop.txt /usr/share/porttest/im/*.log *.la /usr/share/info`). `MERGE_FLOW`'s vdb half copies `build-info/*` plus a fixed generated set (`CATEGORY`/`SLOT`/`repository`/`CONTENTS`/`COUNTER`/`metadata`) and has no code for either. One class by mechanism (a merge-time-Portage-owned aux file `mrg` does not synthesize); split only if the two are fixed independently. `normalize.py`'s `BINPKGMD5` content-blank does not hide it — the file is absent, so the presence check still fires. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l4.md` "## #31 S2"; `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l31-20260926T061556Z/candidate/{portage,mrg}.vdb.tar` + `.vdb/` listings. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track L, owner fixed with #171): portuale `80c6111d` (stack `556dcde4` #170 server-side INSTALL_MASK + BINPKGMD5/INSTALL_MASK shipped into the vdb; `2feb7769` env regenerated from the client merge-time env; `cbc8b407`…`25f25dc1` client config resolved on `--remote-binpkg`, server `make.globals` stacked under it, client `make.profile` resolved against the server repos, LC_ALL split like real), pmtest `2f0d705` (incl. `df84a67`, #189: the l31 candidate gets the reference's calling-env FEATURES). Gate 5 `l1-20260927T140327Z` green, `l31-20260927T140625Z` 0 hard / 0 unexplained. [I]

171. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G1 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, candidate run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) — remote vdb environment is the binpkg's build-time saved env, not a merge-time regeneration.** The two `environment` VDB rows (`porttest/setuid-1.0`, `porttest/phases-1.0`) differ beyond `diff.py`'s 200-char detail: post-normalise, `mrg` adds a stray `declare -- x=""` and its `FEATURES`/`PORTAGE_FEATURES` carry `buildpkg` where the reference does not. Both come from the gpkg build-time env: `build_info/environment.bz2` in `setuid-1.0-1.gpkg.tar` contains `declare -x FEATURES="… buildpkg …"` and `declare -- x=""`. Real Portage regenerates the saved env at merge (`PORTAGE_UPDATE_ENV=<vdb pkgdir>/environment.bz2`, `vartree.py:5334`), so the reference carries the merge-time env (`-buildpkg` per `layers/l1/consume.sh`, no `x`). `build_bundle` server-decompresses the binpkg env and `MERGE_FLOW` copies `build-info/*` (the original `environment.bz2`) plus `$UNIT/environment` as a plain `environment`, so `mrg` also writes a file shape real never writes (real's vdb has `environment.bz2` only; the real capture's `setuid-1.0/` has no plain `environment`). `normalize.py` converts both sides' `environment.bz2` to `environment` and overwrites mrg's plain copy, so the shape divergence is masked and only the content diff fires. Fix direction: regenerate the saved env from the merge phase env (the local path's `PORTAGE_UPDATE_ENV` behaviour, cf. normalize.py's L1-c comment) and drop the plain `environment`. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l4.md` "## #31 S2"; `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l31-20260926T061556Z/candidate/{mrg,portage}.vdb.tar`. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track L, owner Q8 a / Q9 b / Q10 b): portuale `80c6111d` (stack `556dcde4` #170 server-side INSTALL_MASK + BINPKGMD5/INSTALL_MASK shipped into the vdb; `2feb7769` env regenerated from the client merge-time env; `cbc8b407`…`25f25dc1` client config resolved on `--remote-binpkg`, server `make.globals` stacked under it, client `make.profile` resolved against the server repos, LC_ALL split like real), pmtest `2f0d705` (incl. `df84a67`, #189: the l31 candidate gets the reference's calling-env FEATURES). Gate 5 `l1-20260927T140327Z` green, `l31-20260927T140625Z` 0 hard / 0 unexplained. [I]

172. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G1 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, candidate run `l31-20260926T061556Z`) — remote `pkg_*` phases run without `MERGE_TYPE` (SIZE `/var/lib/porttest/phase.log` 322 vs 302).** The `porttest/phases` fixture appends `merge_type=${MERGE_TYPE:-?}` per hook. Real Portage 3.0.82.2 on a gpkg merge appends four lines (`pretend`/`setup`/`preinst`/`postinst`, all `merge_type=binary`) = 322 B; `mrg` runs the same four (`../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l31-20260926T061556Z/candidate/mrg.porttest_phases.stdout.txt` shows `PHASE_pretend=0`, so `pkg_pretend` does run for a binpkg) but `MERGE_TYPE` is unset (`?`) = 302 B — exactly four × the 5-char `binary`→`?` delta. Real sets it in the scheduler (`_emerge/Scheduler.py:1082`, `_emerge/Binpkg.py:92` `settings.configdict["pkg"]["MERGE_TYPE"]="binary"`) and it is a special phase env var; `phase_exports` sets `EMERGE_FROM=binary` but never `MERGE_TYPE`. Product fix: export `MERGE_TYPE` (`binary`/`source`/`buildonly` as appropriate) in the remote phase env. Not the version skew: the candidate phase runtime is the host-mounted `3rdparty/portage` checkout `73091925f` = `portage-3.0.82.2-1`; only the container's system `/usr/sbin/emerge` (recorded in `meta`, never invoked by `mrg`) is 3.0.81.3. Evidence: real capture on the same image + pkgcache (`L1_SKIP_PORTAGE_UPGRADE=1`) appends the 322-byte `merge_type=binary` log; `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/findings/l4.md` "## #31 S2". **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track L): portuale `33637422`; gate `l1-20260926T224047Z` green and `l31-20260926T230107Z` shows the phase.log SIZE row gone. [I]

173. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G2 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, runs `l32-20260926T083456Z`/`l32-20260926T083701Z`) — portuale's default `BINPKG_FORMAT` is `xpak`, real 3.0.82.2's is `gpkg`.** With no `BINPKG_FORMAT` in the environment, `emerge --oneshot --usepkg=n --color=n --buildpkg l32/faultpkg` names the archive `faultpkg-1.0-1.gpkg.tar` on real Portage 3.0.82.2 (`/usr/share/portage/config/make.globals:43` is `BINPKG_FORMAT="gpkg"`) and `faultpkg-1.0-1.xpak` on portuale. Portuale's `PackageOptions::default().binpkg_format` is hardcoded `"xpak"` (`rust/portuale/src/ebuild_package.rs:211`) and the surrounding comments (`:20-21`, `:131-132`) still assert `"xpak"` is "real `make.globals`'s own default: the first entry of `SUPPORTED_GENTOO_BINPKG_FORMATS`" — true of the *supported* tuple order (`portage/const.py:294`), false of the shipped default since the 3.0.7x line. `rust/portuale/src/ebuild.rs:357` layers env `BINPKG_FORMAT` over that default, so any `--buildpkg` without an explicit format produces the wrong name and byte format; the F2 corrupt-archive cell pins `BINPKG_FORMAT=gpkg` for its own scenario and records the unforced default side by side (`format-probe.txt`). Fix: default to `gpkg` (or read `make.globals`'s `BINPKG_FORMAT`) and correct the comment. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l32-20260926T083701Z/F2/candidate/{portage,portuale}.logs/format-probe.txt`; `findings/l5.md` "## #32 S2" class C. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `fc600a75` + pmtest `aeb20bd`; `main` `l32-20260926T231851Z` F2 shows the #173 row gone. Expected: 10240` · `File renamed to '..._checksum_failure_.<rand>'` · `>>> Failed to emerge l32/faultpkg-1.0, Log file:` — rc 1, clean root (no vdb entry, no `-MERGING-`, no installed file). 175. **DONE 2026-09-27 (filed 2026-09-26, P-G2 S2 of `batch-2026-09-25.md`, run `l32-20260926T083914Z`) — a failed binhost index fetch is fatal: portuale leaks raw wget output, omits real's `!!! [<repo>] Error fetching...` shape, and never falls back to the ebuild.** With only `[l32-500]` (`sync-uri = http://127.0.0.1:18765`) configured and a local stub answering 500 to every GET/HEAD, real emits `!!! [l32-500] Error fetching binhost package info from 'http://127.0.0.1:18765'` + `!!! [l32-500] HTTP Error 500: Internal Server Error`, then (a) with a stale local index (faultpkg removed from `PKGDIR`, `-k --getbinpkg`) selects a binary and aborts rc 1 via `>>> Emerging binary (1 of 1)` → `!!! Tried to use non-existent binary for 'l32/faultpkg-1.0'` → `!!! Likely caused by an outdated index. Run 'emaint binhost -f'.`; (b) with a completely empty `PKGDIR` it **falls back to the ebuild** (`>>> Emerging (1 of 1) l32/faultpkg-1.0::l32`), builds from source, rc 0, and installs `/usr/share/l32/faultpkg` (+ the vdb entry). Portuale's `emerge_getbinpkg::refresh_binhost_indexes` (`rust/portuale/src/emerge_getbinpkg.rs:63-104`) shells `wget` directly, so the log is the raw wget transcript (`--...-- http://127.0.0.1:18765/Packages.gz` … `500 Internal Server Error`), and its caller `pretend.rs:11395-11401` turns any refresh error into `emerge: binhost ...: wget failed to fetch "http://127.0.0.1:18765/Packages" (exit status: 8)` + `ExitCode::from(1)` **before resolution**, so both (a) and (b) abort and nothing is installed (comparator row `MISSING /usr/share/l32/faultpkg`, F3b). Fix direction: emit real's `!!! [<repo>] Error fetching binhost package info` / `!!! HTTP Error 500` message shape, treat a failed remote index refresh as non-fatal, and let the resolver fall back to the ebuild when no usable local binary exists. Evidence: `../pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l32-20260926T083914Z/F3/candidate/{portage,portuale}.logs/{binhost-merge.log,binhost-fallback.log,binhost-state.txt,binhost-fallback-state.txt}`; `findings/l5.md` "## #32 S2" class E. **DONE 2026-09-27** (Track M): portuale `de555da2` + pmtest `a278174`; gate `l1-20260927T065535Z` green and l32 F3 `l32-20260927T070047Z` is 0 unexplained. This is a feature (install-info spawn + dir-mtime memo + output), not the #158 S1 `REPLACING_VERSIONS`/`movefile` leave-alone mechanism that fixed the opposite `awk.1` direction, so `#158` S1's scope fix split it out rather than folding it in. It is the only class left on `l3-core` at `l3-20260927T080452Z` (2 rows: `dev-lang/python-3.14.6_p1` over `_sysconfig*` / `config-3.14/Makefile`, `dev-libs/icu-78.3` over `pkgdata.inc`); 09-26 folded it into its P-Z notes by owner ruling Q1, and the owner asked on 2026-09-27 for a number and a fix. Same family as #152's build-id keying. Fix in pmtest: `SIZE` inherits the tolerance when every file row under its package is tolerated; a negative test keeps it hard when any file row is hard. Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-09-27-batch0926-closeout-ledger.md` (#30 row). **Shipped 2026-09-27 (pmtest `1b32e49`, branch `backlog/191-l3-size-tolerance`): `diff_vdb` reports a differing vdb `SIZE` as `PAYLOAD` when `--tolerate-payload` is on and no hard row remains for any file the package owns (membership data-driven from either side's vdb `CONTENTS` via `_contents_map`; the package's own hard `CONTENTS` row also blocks, other vdb files such as `environment` never do) — grounded in real `doebuild.py:_post_src_install_uid_fix`, which sums `${D}` `st_size` per inode into `build-info/SIZE`, so the vdb `SIZE` is by construction its files' size aggregate. Self-test grows four cases (positive / one-hard-`MODE`-row negative / flag-off control / hard-`environment`-coexists tolerance); offline re-run on the `l3-20260927T080452Z` snapshots moves exactly the two rows `VDB` → `PAYLOAD` (347/345/2 + 149 payload → 345/345/0 + 151 payload, row-level diff otherwise empty). The coordinator flipped it after the `l3-core` re-run. [I]

Pinned by `test_251a_*`, `test_251b_*`, `test_251c_*` (fixtures `dev-libs/u251{m,p,y}`, `dev-util/u251{ta,tb,olda,oldb,q,r,c,d}`). *Original entry:* (a) the flat-list blocker disposition now takes the settling map, but a demoted branch plus Replacement rows is unexercised; (b) a `--deep` installed-cp collision (installed v1, merging v2 of a recorded node) is the only shape that could reach the installed deep-walk site, which takes an empty map by design (real keys by node identity, `Package.__hash__`); (c) with every branch demoted, real's in-bin promotion (`dep_check.py:738-802`) runs over `other` while suppression keeps the first-listed branch. Build each fixture with a real oracle and fix any divergence it shows. [A]

252. **DONE 2026-09-29 (portuale `backlog/252-mmprov-reprobe`; pmtest pins + portuale fix, paired) — the #211 `mmprov` pin did not match real; H-g213 won.** One host staged-fixture probe of the committed shape (installed `mmprov-1` `0/1` + `mmprov-2` `1/1` + `mmcons-1` bound `0/1=`, tree `mmprov-3` `1/2`, `emerge -uD mmprov mmcons` on real `/usr/sbin/emerge` 3.0.82.2, mount-namespace `make.local` shadow): real merges only `mmprov-3` (rc 0, `backtrack: 0/3`, no `mmcons-1` row, no `causing rebuilds` block). Mechanism, spied in a vendored-3.0.82.2 `ResolverPlayground` run: the `--update` request gains a greedy per-slot arg (`_select_files`, `depgraph.py:5380`, via `_greedy_slots`, `:5896` — `mmprov:0` recorded on the installed slot-0 instance), and the update probe's reverse-deps gate (`_slot_operator_check_reverse_dependencies`, `:2472-2538`) refuses every slot-1 candidate against it, so both `new_child_slot` probe forms return None and `slot_operator_replace_installed` stays empty. Ported as the `#252` edge gate in `slot_operator_rebuild_scan`'s new-slot arm (requested provider + `--update` + bound slot below the highest visible slot + older installed instance ⇒ no probe); the two pins (`test_oracle_slotop_update_probe_mismatched_upgrade_entry` + the pubgrub-bridge sibling) re-pinned to `{mmprov-3}` from the probed bytes (RED first, then GREEN). Residue **#268**: explicit slot/version request atoms (greedy adds nothing there) and the blocker-lookahead revision discards stay cuts. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-09-28, #213 review, g213 report §5, `docs/evidence/2026-09-28-batch0927-reports/g213-report.md`) — the #211 `mmprov` pin may not match real.** #211's item-2 pin for the `mmprov` shape says portuale MATCHES real, but that was grounded in source reading only, never probed; the g213 S0 ResolverPlayground data suggests real merges only `mmprov-3` with an empty replace set there. Run one container probe of the `mmprov` fixture shape; if real differs, correct the pin from the probe and fix the resolver. [A]

253. **DONE 2026-09-29 (half 1 fixed + pinned: branch `backlog/253-prune-conflict-missed`, pmtest `5a117ac` + portuale `bd4d6fbb`; half 2 resolved by S0 proof below — no ebuild shape exists to pin, the remaining drop paths are v2 `#24c`/`#212` territory; filed 2026-09-28, #213 review) — `prune_rebuilds` reads only the mask half of real's missed updates, and no pin shows a rebuild being dropped.** Real's trigger (`_emerge/depgraph.py:5763`) calls `_get_missed_updates` (`:1529-1565`), which chains `_runtime_pkg_mask` and `_conflict_missed_update`; portuale's predicate reuses `backtrack_missed_updates`, the mask half only, so a conflict-source-only shape under-fires the prune (safe direction: same rows, fewer passes). The #213 cell's rebuilds are genuine and come back, so nothing pins the drop half end to end. Build a droppable-rebuild shape and a conflict-source-only shape with a real oracle and pin both. **Half 1 DONE 2026-09-29 (branch `backlog/253-prune-conflict-missed`; pmtest pins + portuale predicate-chain fix, paired):** the predicate now ORs the conflict half (`pass.skipped_updates` -- the direct solve's removal rows, the only rows present at the trigger layer) exactly like real's `:1533-1536` truthiness; per-slot-highest display still collapses at settle. Pinned by `test_oracle_prune_rebuilds_conflict_missed_updates` (the #213 `pprov`/`pcons` cell plus the #90 reversed `slotconflictoldconsumer`/`slotconflictnewconsumer` pair, zero new fixtures): real 3.0.82.2 host staged-fixture probe (rc 0, `backtrack: 3/20`, five rows, the `WARNING` block with NO `!!!` tail -- the conflict-source-only lock) vs portuale `--json` `restarts == 3` (was 1), rows byte-identical, plus a `--backtrack=0` control (WARNING persists, no tail, no prune) and a Rust `collect_feedback` unit pair (fires on conflict-only, quiet on empty). The #213 pin stays green. **Half 2 resolved by S0 proof 2026-09-29: no honest ebuild shape drops a probe-scheduled rebuild on the re-resolve, so there is no pin to write.** Exhausted in a vendored-3.0.82.2 `ResolverPlayground` sweep (same-slot, new-slot requested/unrequested, pulled-consumer, USE-conditional, `||`, downgrade, keyword/visibility, and bug-622270 shapes; per-pass replace/eliminate tracing): the prune re-walk is identical once the replace set regrows (only the replace set and built-binary steers vary across a prune restart, and real's flag is read nowhere else but the pass comparison `backtracking.py:76` -- portuale's `apply_prune_rebuilds`/`:25328`/`:30355` read exactly those), and `_eliminate_rebuilds` rule 8 keeps every `:=` consumer whose binding broke -- which is exactly the probe's scheduling condition (only a binding-breaking atom pulls a new-slot child, and the live `:=` string can never equal the recorded bound string). The remaining drop paths are out of this slice's scope: binary built-mask no-refire + rule-7 drops (the soname/`slot_operator_mask_built` halves portuale has not ported -- probe-side territory, v2 `#24c`/`#212`'s documented cuts), and mask-killed provider updates (whose killer mask always carries rows, so the prune fires without the predicate fix -- no RED). Residues of this slice's probes: **#269** (the `abi_rebuilds` new-slot over-report) and **#270** (the `--backtrack=0` update-atomicity divergence). Original entry follows. [A]

254. **DONE 2026-09-30 (batch-2026-09-28 Track R; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-30-254-255/`; residue #277) — the conflict-fired ABI probe can rebuild where real reports the conflict.** Three rules ported. `slot_conflict_abi_probe` USE-reduces the replacement ebuild's atoms against its effective USE (real `_select_atoms_probe`): a `cflag? ( …:= )` with `cflag` off no longer accepts. The unsatisfied probe reads the replacement's atoms with every conditional branch active (real `validated_atoms`, `use_reduce(..., matchall=True)`), which is how real heals that shape after masking the conflict. `slot_conflict_abi_display_pairs` pairs a provider only when the rebuilt consumer pulls it (real `_compute_abi_rebuild_info` walks the replacement's graph children), so the healed shape prints real's rows without the "causing rebuilds" block. The other cuts cannot be observed in the conflict-fired path: the child-side `--exclude` applies only to an installed child (the conflict's other instance is merge-bound); the candidate is the in-graph conflict instance, so it is visible and needs no autounmask retry. Pinned by `test_254_conflict_abi_probe_evaluates_the_replacements_use_conditionals` (fixture `app-misc/abicondcons`) and unit cases in `slot_conflict_abi_probe_rebuilds_the_built_parent` / `slot_conflict_abi_display_pairs_follow_the_rebuild`. *Original entry:* `slot_conflict_abi_probe` (`portage-repo/src/lib.rs`) matches the replacement parent's live atoms as flat tokens, while real's `_slot_operator_update_probe` evaluates USE conditionals and `||` through `_select_atoms_probe` (`_emerge/depgraph.py:2667-2713`): a `flag? ( …:= )` with the flag off accepts in portuale and rejects in real (portuale rc 0 rebuild, real rc 1 conflict). Same class: only the parent end of `--exclude` is checked (real refuses on the child too, `:2590-2605`), the candidate child is not visibility-checked, and the autounmask-level retry (`:2453-2470`) is not ported (a missed rebuild when autounmask is on). Narrowing only: the probe iterates the two-instance conflict record, so a 3+-provider same-slot conflict (`:2299`) is under-explored. Build failing-first fixture arms per shape with a real oracle. [A]

255. **DONE 2026-09-30 (batch-2026-09-28 Track R; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-30-254-255/u255-otherslot.txt`) — the `r` flag's "not new" gate is keyed by cp, real's by slot.** `pretend.rs`'s provider gate now asks for an installed instance in the provider's own slot (real `_get_installed_best`): a provider installed only in another slot prints `NS` like real, not `rS`. Pinned by `test_255_forced_rebuild_marker_is_keyed_by_the_providers_slot`; the #211 conflict-mass pin's `somassb-2` row moved `rS` → `NS` with it (real's own row, blessed). The multi-provider attribution half is covered by #254's `required_by` gate, real's child linkage. *Original entry:* `output.py:812-816` sets `new` from same-`slot_atom` installed matches (`_get_installed_best`); `pretend.rs` keys the provider's newness off any installed version of the cp, so a provider installed only in another slot keeps a wrong `r`. No test covers it; the #214 shape (nothing installed) agrees. Relatedly, `slot_conflict_abi_display_pairs` pairs each replace-set reinstall with every merged same-slot/different-sub-slot provider of each recorded atom, where real derives the pair through the force-reinstall linkage: a multi-provider settle can attribute a rebuild to the wrong provider. Pin both with a real oracle. [A]

256. **DONE 2026-09-30, resolved by proof (batch-2026-09-28 Track R; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-30-256-257/`) — the unsatisfied probe seeds every hitting owner of a cp in one pass.** Real does the same: `_add_dep`'s `return 1` (`_emerge/depgraph.py:3453-3455`) ends only the probed edge, the walk continues and probes the next, so two hitting parents both join the replace set before one restart (real's playground on the two-parent shape: two probe hits, one `backtracking try`, in every argument order). Portuale's rows and restart count match; pinned by `test_256_unsatisfied_probe_seeds_every_hitting_parent_in_one_pass` (fixtures `app-misc/u256{pa,pb,prov}`). The one divergence the sweep found (a parent with no resolving replacement) is a different mechanism, filed as #276. *Original entry:* Real probes one dep edge and restarts on the first hit (`_emerge/depgraph.py:3453-3455`, `return 1`); `slot_operator_unsatisfied_probe` seeds all installed owners with a paired built want on the same cp. Shape: two installed owners with unsatisfied built wants on one provider cp, only one with a resolving replacement, or both resolvable where real's first restart already heals the second. Oracle the shape and pin the row set before deciding whether one-per-pass is load-bearing. [A]

257. **DONE 2026-09-30 (batch-2026-09-28 Track R; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-30-256-257/`) — no unsatisfied-probe path for a non-installed built parent.** `slot_operator_unsatisfied_probe_full` now takes a merge-bound binary parent too: a hit masks the binary (`!=cat/pkg-ver[binary]`, `SlotOperatorBuilt`, #212's form) and restarts, and the missing-dependency trigger tries this probe first for a built slot-operator atom, like real's `_add_dep` (`:3447-3455`). `--usepkgonly` keeps the abort (no ebuild replacement, real's `_iter_similar_available` yields binaries only). Pinned by `test_257_unsatisfied_probe_masks_a_stale_binary_parent` (fixtures `app-misc/u257{par,prov}` + a per-test `Packages` entry) and the Rust unit case in `slot_operator_unsatisfied_probe_seeds_a_replacement_parent`. *Original entry:* Real `_slot_operator_unsatisfied_backtrack` masks a non-installed built parent (`slot_operator_mask_built`, `_emerge/depgraph.py:2901-2903`) and restarts; the port `continue`s past owners with no installed instance, so the pass stays a dead end and the run aborts where real steers. #212's probe fires on a different trigger (a merge-bound binary's shifted built dep, `:2381-2398`). Shape: a binary package (`--usepkg`) whose recorded `:=` dep is bound to an abandoned provider sub-slot, with a same-slot ebuild that accepts the visible provider. [A]

266. **DONE 2026-10-02 (batch-2026-09-29 Track R / R7, merged `31953983`) — `minimize_children` reads the candidate's effective USE before collapsing. Original entry follows.** **OPEN (filed 2026-09-29, #236 Slice B / #107 D2) — `_minimize_children` never matches a use-dep atom against a not-yet-built package.** Real's elimination matches every selected package with `findAtomForPackage(pkg, modified_use=self._pkg_use_enabled(pkg))` (`_emerge/depgraph.py:4797-4807`); portuale's `minimize_children` does that for installed packages (vdb USE/IUSE, #107 D2) but counts a use-dep atom as matching only its own pick for an ebuild/binary candidate, so a collapse that needs the candidate's effective USE (e.g. `>=foo-1[bar]` alongside `<foo-3` on an ebuild parent, both satisfiable by the lower pick) keeps both selections where real drops one. Conservative (it can only keep a package real drops, never the reverse), and no L0 probe or fixture shows it; fix by evaluating the candidate's effective USE the way the walk's own selection does. [A]

Findings: a non-empty profile `virtuals` (chain order, `/etc/portage/profile` last) prints a Python `UserWarning` on stderr once per file at config load, for every action that loads the config (`-p`, `-s`, `--info`, `--version`, merges); a `/etc/portage/virtuals` (any content) prints the `!!! ... is deprecated and ignored.` block on stderr, but only for the validating actions (build, pretend, search, depclean, clean, unmerge, prune, rage-clean), not for `--info`, `--sync`, `--metadata`, `--check-news`, `--list-sets`. Fixed: `portage-profile/src/virtuals.rs` (grabdict gate, profile warnings at rank 6, `Config::user_virtuals_deprecated`), `pretend/early_actions.rs` `prints_user_virtuals_notice` gates the block in `pretend::run`. Tests: `cargo test --release -p portage-profile virtuals` (4), `-p portuale user_virtuals` (2); pmtest `test_513_*` (9 hermetic pins). The one non-identical byte is the Python location prefix (residue #548). Portage's quirk record: `LLM/portage-quirks.md` Q9. Residues #547-#554. Code notes: `LLM/code-notes/portage-profile/virtuals.md`, `LLM/code-notes/portuale/pretend/early_actions.md`. Tier 2. [A]

612. **DONE 2026-11-06 (pmtest `45ae4ba`, standalone pmtest commit; no product change) — was: OPEN (filed 2026-10-28, found in R3 gate) — corpus drift is flagged in `pytest-parallel` runs but not in serial runs.** After R2's bless a serial `-k test_pretend_case_exit_code` run is clean, while `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` still warns `CorpusDrift: stdout changed` on about ten rows (the `for <ROOT>/` header and the mg2top count) and a parallel bless does not clear them. Likely the same-args/env matcher collision (memory `corpus-matcher-same-args-env-collision`) or a worker race on the bless write. Warnings only; no test fails. Tier 2. [A] Oracle: the R3-gate parallel-vs-serial runs (serial `-k test_pretend_case_exit_code` clean after the R2 bless; parallel still warns `CorpusDrift` on ~10 rows; warnings only, no failure). **Findings 2026-11-06:** (1) the serial and parallel runs compared in the filing were not the same selection: `-k test_pretend_case_exit_code` does not include `test_expanded_corpus_replays`, which the full parallel run does, and the ~10 rows were expanded-corpus rows. Run on the same selection the two modes flag identical drift (checked on `-k test_pretend_case_exit_code` and on `test_expanded_corpus_replays`). (2) The real defect was in the bless: each xdist worker ran `write_blessed` from the contract corpus it had loaded at its own start, so the last worker to finish dropped the rows the others had blessed (a parallel bless cleared only one worker's drift). Fixed: `write_blessed` reloads the file under a directory lock and `save()` renames a temporary file into place; unit test `test_blessing_from_several_xdist_workers_keeps_every_workers_rows` was red before. Residues: #660, #661.

665. **DONE 2026-11-06 (portuale, standalone) — `portuale` now type-checks for `x86_64-unknown-freebsd` with zero errors and zero warnings (`LLM/tools/bsd_check.sh` exits 0).** Fixes: `mode_t` is `u16` there, so `sys_types::mode_t_of` narrows `std`'s `u32` mode before `mkfifo`/`mknod`/`chmod` and the `libc::S_IF*` matches (`ebuild_merge/tree.rs`, `helpers/gpkg.rs`, `helpers/doins.rs`); `libc::major/minor` are cast with `as u64`; `setgroups` takes `groups.len() as _`; `is_noexec` reads `MNT_NOEXEC` through `statfs` off Linux; the three xattr helpers keep their `libc` calls on Linux and report "no attributes" / `ENOTSUP` elsewhere (BSD `extattr_*` is not ported, so xattrs are not preserved on BSD); the two sandbox helpers only the Linux `pre_exec` block uses carry `allow(dead_code)` off Linux. Check only: FreeBSD was type-checked, not built, linked or run; other BSDs and borrowck-level surprises on a real BSD are untested, and the merge-path edits are type-only no-ops on Linux (`mode_t` is `u32`) but the merge-path safety gate (glibc and bash test merges) was not re-run. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-06, residue of #637) — `portuale` does not type-check for `x86_64-unknown-freebsd` (22 errors outside the namespace code).** Run `LLM/tools/bsd_check.sh`. Groups: `libc::{getxattr,setxattr,listxattr}` missing (`helpers/doins.rs:1256-1301`), `libc::ST_NOEXEC` missing (`ebuild_phases/runtime_paths.rs:171`), and `mode_t`/`dev_t`/`nlink` width differences (u16 on FreeBSD) in `helpers/gpkg.rs:973-1021`, `helpers/doins.rs:1488`, `ebuild_merge/tree.rs:333-359`, `ebuild_phases/commands.rs:1217`. rustc stops before borrowck, so more may follow. Fix with `cfg`/`as`-casts per group, then keep the script green. BSD support is a stated future portability requirement. Tier 3. [C] Oracle: `LLM/tools/bsd_check.sh` exits 0.

Needs its own oracle trace. Tier 2. Premise: the count matches after R6; the rc gap has no oracle trace yet (needs its own trace).

619. **DONE 2026-10-29 (branch `batch/2026-10-29-easy-six`; portuale `ad4b583e`; no pmtest half) — the tail entries #574–#619 now sit in their own Tier 1/2/3/4 sections (new `## Tier 3 — parity residues`), the three rule-(d) rows are gone, and the 42 rule-(c) rows remain (filed as #620).** Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-29, residue of #601 close-out) — every backlog entry appended after `## Tier 8` inherits Tier 8, so `doc_status_check.py` rule (d) flags the plan of any re-tiered item.** `## Tier 8 — resolver performance` opened the file's tail, and everything filed after it sat under that heading whatever its own `Tier N.` tag said; rule (d) then reported `02.575`, `02.589` and `02.601`, and would report every future tail entry whose plan prefix is not 08. Fix by giving the tail entries their own `## Tier N` headings (or moving them into the existing tier blocks) and re-running `doc_status_check.py`; renaming the plans would paper over the file's placement. Premise: the checker reads headings, not the entry text; the 2026-09-20 Tier 8 block is simply where the file's tail starts. Tier 2. [A]
