621. **DONE 2026-11-01 (branch `backlog/621-quiet-abort-preamble` in both repos, uncommitted) — the `--pretend --quiet` abort preamble prints nothing, like Portage.** `pretend/merge_list.rs::abort_preamble` printed the banner plus `Calculating dependencies ... done!` whenever `quiet` was set (only the timing line was gated). Portage's `_start_resolution_display` (vendored 3.0.83, `lib/_emerge/depgraph.py:12216-12262`) skips the banner when `--quiet` is in myopts (`:12219`), forces the spinner QUIET (`:12259-12260`), and the `backtrack_depgraph` finally reports only when `end_notice()` is true (`stdout_spinner.py`), which is false with nothing pending -- so no banner, no notice completion, no report. The function now returns the empty string under `quiet` (before the #614 `debug` arm; quiet wins there too, per the same gate). Pins: updated Rust unit test (`abort_preamble(true, …) == ""`, red pre-fix) and new contract test `test_quiet_abort_preamble_prints_nothing_like_portage` (`--pretend --quiet dev-libs/missingdep`: rc 1, empty stdout). Oracles: host Portage 3.0.83 live capture (empty stdout, rc 1) and the byte-identical post-fix portuale stdout on the same fixture atom; stderr disclosures unchanged (the `for <root>` vs `for <root>/` disclosure-line gap there is #395 territory, out of scope). No fixture change (existing `missingdep` covers the shape). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-29, residue of #614) — the `--pretend --quiet` abort preamble prints the banner and `Calculating dependencies ... done!`, where Portage prints neither.** Portage skips the banner under `--quiet` (`_emerge/depgraph.py:12219`) and forces the spinner to QUIET (`:12259-12260`), so an aborted `--pretend -q` run has no resolution display at all. `abort_preamble(quiet=true)` prints banner + notice, and the pins around it encode that shape. Oracle: a Portage 3.0.83 capture of the same abort atoms under `--pretend --quiet` (the Task 0 `capture.sh` shape). Tier 2. [A]

680. **FIXED on branch `fix-xs-rebuild-order` in both repos, uncommitted (filed 2026-11-07, found by the standalone health checker) — a slot-operator rebuild of an installed XS module was merged after the builds that need it usable.** Charter property 2 (PMS 8.1: a build-time dependency must be installed *and usable*, i.e. its RDEPEND closure met). In `emerge -puvDN @world` on the bed (same in `-pve @world`, `-pve @system`, `-pve app-misc/tmux`, `MULTI_emptytree-system`, `-puvDN --exclude sys-devel/gcc @world`; not `-puvD @world`, which does not upgrade perl) portuale upgraded `dev-lang/perl` 5.42.2 -> 5.44.0 at #182, built `dev-build/automake-1.19` at #260 (BDEPEND `sys-apps/help2man`, installed, which needs `dev-perl/Locale-gettext` at run time) and rebuilt `app-text/po4a` at #284 (DEPEND `dev-perl/Locale-gettext`), but rebuilt `Locale-gettext` -- an XS module bound to `dev-lang/perl:0/5.42=` -- only at #299. Its own needs (perl, the installed `sys-devel/gettext`) were met right after #182; not a cycle. Two causes in `merge_order`: (a) the installed `Locale-gettext` still counted as satisfying `po4a`'s DEPEND (`DepPriority::satisfied`), although the plan's perl upgrade breaks it, so cycle breaking under `drop_satisfied` (`ignore_satisfied_buildtime`) relaxed the edge; (b) nothing tied a build to the run-time deps of its build-time deps: `help2man`'s RDEPEND edge is a run-time edge the ladder relaxes, so `automake` became a leaf. Fix: `usable_installed_by_cp` (an installed package whose VDB `:SLOT/SUB=` binding the plan breaks satisfies nothing) feeds `build_digraph`'s `satisfied`; `add_usability_edges` adds, after the bias, a build-time edge from each build to every node its build-time deps reach through unsatisfied run-time edges; the redundant second `merge_order_bias` call in `serialize_merge_order` is gone. Tests: pmtest `test_680_slot_operator_rebuild_merges_before_the_builds_that_use_it` (fixture `p680*`, health checker, fails before: `p680build` #2 and `p680doc` #3 before `p680xs` #8) and two unit tests. Bed: l0-health wide (`l0-health-20261107T210124Z`), the six plans go clean, no plan gains a finding; L0 `l0-20261107T205456Z` 91 clean / 44 unexplained (was 43): `MULTI_deep-update-world` and `MULTI_emptytree-system` diverge at the same position as before but with `virtual/perl-CPAN` as portuale's pick, so the first leaves the `perl-cluster-hash-order-601` allowlist. Tier 2. Oracle: `pytests-contract-suite/health_checker.py` (property 2) on `differential-test-bed/logs/l0-health-20261107T195447Z/` and the `p680` fixture; Portage not consulted.

681. **DONE 2026-11-07 (found by the health checker property 4/1 run) — `--depclean` selected packages that kept packages still need at run time.** Two root causes: (a) the depclean walk matched installed packages with a `cat/pn-ver:slot` string that dropped the sub-slot, so every `:0/8=` atom failed to match (36 selected); fixed with `InstalledPackage::match_str(root)`, used by every depclean/prune/clean match. (b) the walk never followed `IDEPEND` (the last 4: locale-gen, eselect-*); added to the walk, prune and the unresolved check. Bed repro now keeps 320 of 320. Test: `depclean_cleanlist_keeps_sub_slot_dependencies_and_idepend_targets`. Original report: In the bed container with the seeded world file (`app-misc/tmux app-portage/eix app-portage/gentoolkit dev-libs/blake3`), `portuale --depclean -p` selects 36 packages, among them sys-libs/readline, sys-libs/zlib, dev-libs/gmp, mpfr, libffi, gnutls, libassuan; e.g. gnupg-2.5.21 keeps `RDEPEND sys-libs/readline:0/8=` with USE=readline. The checker reports 55 `removal-deps` warnings. Portage's `emerge -pc` in the same container keeps all 320 packages. Charter property 1 (a real depclean would break installed programs). Repro: `podman run --rm -v /var/tmp/bedbin:/usr/local/bin:ro --entrypoint /bin/bash localhost/test-portuale:latest -c 'printf "%s\n" app-misc/tmux app-portage/eix app-portage/gentoolkit dev-libs/blake3 > /var/lib/portage/world; ROOT=/ PORTAGE_CONFIGROOT=/ emerge -pvc --json --ignore-default-opts'`. Check first: USE-conditional and slot-operator RDEPEND edges of installed packages in the depclean graph.

682. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branch `backlog/682-merge-order-sort-total`; plan `LLM/02.682-merge-order-sort-total.opus.md`; cause confirmed: `NoVisibleCandidate` entries of `llvm-core/llvm` compared equal to every version; fix `version_order` in `merge_order/mod.rs` used at the three sites, plus the graph index as last sort key; residue #685) — was: found while debugging the `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n -p @world` failure of commit `bbcf5e7d`) — `portuale emerge` panics ("user-provided comparison function does not correctly implement a total order") in the merge-order candidate sort, and the same command gives different results between runs.** Repro on the 2026-11-07 tree: `portuale emerge -pvuDN --getbinpkg=n media-video/ffmpeg`, run it several times (one run in three aborts with rc 134; the others print an error about `~llvm-core/llvm-22.1.8[llvm_targets_XCore=]`, so the output also differs). `RUST_BACKTRACE=1` puts the panic in `portage_repo::merge_order::cycles::find_smallest_cycle` (the `cand.sort_by` closure, `crates/portage-repo/src/merge_order/cycles.rs:49`), called from `select_nodes` <- `serialize_merge_order` <- `topological_merge_order` <- `assemble_result`. Likely cause, not yet checked by a fix: the closure compares `(category, package)` and then `vercmp(...).unwrap_or(0)`, so a version that does not parse, or an entry with no version at all (`entry_version(..).unwrap_or_default()` gives `""`), compares equal to every other version of the same package; an order where `a < b`, `b == c`, `c < a` is not total and the standard sort may panic on it. `cand` is built from a `HashSet` (`mergeable`), so the iteration order, and with it whether the sort meets the bad triple, changes from run to run, which would explain the intermittence and the different outputs. A panic in the shipped binary is a wrong result for the user. Fix direction: compare the version with a total order (parse failure sorts first or last, deterministically), build `cand` from a sorted vector, and add a unit test with an unparseable version and an entry without a version among three nodes of one `cp`. Also check the other `sort_by` calls on `vercmp(..).unwrap_or(0)` in `merge_order/`. Tier 2.

683. **DONE 2026-11-09 (branch `backlog/683-or-upgrade-preference`; fix ports Portage `dep_check.py:771-775` installed-slots promotion guard into `promote_tied_alternative` (`crates/portage-repo/src/resolve/or_preference.rs`) with lazy `want_update` + 7 gate legs in `tests_162.rs`; tester fresh pre-merge: full workspace 3360 passed / 0 failed, promote group 19/19, fmt clean, clippy 0 warnings; NOT verified: host `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n -p @world` diff NOT RUN, L0 NOT RUN (residues, owner step)) — was: OPEN (filed 2026-11-08, found by comparing the plan of `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n -p @world` with Portage's after commit `bbcf5e7d`; owner asked for it) — a `||` choice under `--update` / `--deep` takes the alternative of the installed slot, where Portage takes the one that contains an upgrade, so the plans differ by two packages: `dev-lang/python-3.15.2_p1` and `dev-lang/rust-bin-1.99.0`.** Evidence on the 2026-11-07 tree: Portage lists `[ebuild NS] dev-lang/python-3.15.2_p1 [3.14.10]` under `media-libs/mesa-26.2.6` (python-any-r1: `|| ( ( dev-lang/python:3.15 … ) ( dev-lang/python:3.14 … ) … )`, 3.15 listed first) and `[ebuild NS] dev-lang/rust-bin-1.99.0 [1.98.1]` under `app-misc/asciinema-3.2.1`, and still upgrades `python-3.14.10` in the same plan; portuale picks the installed-slot alternative in both places and schedules neither. Portage's rule (read in `lib/portage/dep/dep_check.py`, `dep_zapdeps`, around the `want_update_pkg` / `new_slot_count` / `has_upgrade` code; the exact ordering of those keys is not yet checked): among the selectable alternatives it prefers one that upgrades a package, so a higher version in a new slot wins over the installed slot when updating. Owner ruling 2026-11-08: a package with a higher version in another slot may be ignored only when it is NOT in `@world` and `--deep` is NOT used; otherwise the better version is added, as long as the slot already present (and needed) is not affected. The ruling covers `||` choices and `@world` members only: 12 other cps in the dependency closure have a visible higher new slot (`atkmm`, `cairomm`, `glibmm`, `gtkmm`, `pangomm`, `libsigc++`, `ffmpeg-compat:7/8`, `gexiv2`, `commons-lang`, …) and Portage does not install them because no dependency picks them, so they stay out; `wine-staging-11.21` is in both plans already. Where to look: the `||` selection and `AltPreference` ranking (`portage-use-reduce`, `use_reduce_flat_disjunctive`; `resolve/or_preference.rs`, `resolve/disjunction_stack.rs`). Fix direction: add the has-upgrade preference to the ranking when `--update` is on, with a unit test per shape (a `||` whose first alternative is a new slot with a higher version, with and without `--deep`, with the installed-slot alternative unchanged in the plan). Risk: it moves merge-order cells; run the L0 bed and review the divergences before accepting. Done when `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n -p @world` on this host lists both packages and the 346 others are unchanged. Tier 2.

684. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-08, owner asked; resume after #683 ships; #682 shipped 2026-11-08) — keep experimenting with `experiment-pre-sort-atoms` (`crates/experiment-pre-sort-atoms`, commit `403b3a70`), or retire it.** State: an experimental program, kept out of a plain `cargo build` (`default-members`); build it with `cargo build --release -p experiment-pre-sort-atoms`. It collects the atoms of `@world` (world file, `world_sets` through `sets.conf`, `@system`, `@profile`) and their dependencies, follows the best visible ebuild of every slot an atom can match (`||` resolved to one branch, installed branches first), and prints for each package slot its versions in final order: visible first, then highest version, then repo priority, with the versions that satisfy every atom aimed at the slot in front; a slot nothing visible satisfies is INVALID (still listed), one another slot serves is "unneeded", `--unique` also marks slots with several satisfying versions. All cores, with a thread-shared memo for visibility and dependency lists (521 ms to 292 ms on this host before the 2026-11-07 repository update). Measured so far (before that update, so redo it): 5240 atoms, 2410 slots, 5 invalid; about 1% of the installed slots differ from the best version it predicts. What the work showed: it cannot speed up `portuale emerge` (after the update the time was in backtracking and merge order, `is_visible` about 3%, binary candidates about 3%), and the world update it was built next to turned out to be a resolver bug, fixed in `bbcf5e7d`. Ideas to take up, in this order: (1) re-run it after #682 and #683 and compare its predicted versions and its INVALID list with the plan of `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n -p @world`; (2) make its `||` choice follow the has-upgrade preference once #683 defines it, then diff the package sets of the two programs as a cheap cross-check of the resolver; (3) close its known gaps: it follows `BDEPEND` of installed packages whatever `--with-bdeps` says (the root of most of the 1% difference; use `InstalledReachability::for_run` as the resolver now does), USE dependencies (`[foo]`) do not filter versions, an atom without a slot follows every visible slot, sets of classes other than the static ones are skipped (`@security`, `@preserved-rebuild`, …); (4) give it automated tests, it was only checked by hand; (5) decide: promote the useful part (an INVALID-slot report as a health check) into a real subcommand or the health checker, or delete the crate. Done when the owner has picked (5) and the crate is either promoted with tests or removed. **Progress 2026-11-08 (branch `backlog/684-experiment-pre-sort-atoms`):** ideas 3 and 4 largely done. A slotless atom is now one requirement (installed slot, else the best slot), so the five false INVALID `:20` slots are gone (0 invalid); an installed package that stays as it is follows only `RDEPEND`/`PDEPEND` (`--with-bdeps` restores the rest); `[flag]`/`[-flag]` USE dependencies filter versions and parent-relative forms are evaluated against the asking package; `VariableSet`, `UnavailableSet`, `DowngradeSet` and `SubslotChangedSet` are read (`--set NAME`, `--show-seeds`), and `@rust-rebuild`, `@golang-rebuild`, `@changed-subslot`, `@live-rebuild`, `@unavailable` match Portage's own set members exactly on this host. Against the plan of `-uDNt --getbinpkg=n @world` its best versions differ for 2 of 2076 installed slots (both from `buildovl`, before the depcachedir read below). 18 unit tests. The cache-read half of the depend-phase fallback now lives in `portage-repo` (`depcache_aux_metadata`, `depcachedir`, `depcache_entry_path`, `parse_aux_entry`) and the experiment registers it, so a repository without `metadata/md5-cache` is read through `PORTAGE_DEPCACHEDIR`; with that `@downgrade` matches Portage (0 members). An ebuild with no md5-cache and no fresh depcachedir entry is read through the `portuale` binary: the hidden entry `portuale __aux-metadata REPO CATEGORY PF` runs the `depend` phase, prints the metadata and fills the depcachedir; the experiment spawns it (`$PORTUALE_BIN`, else `portuale` beside its own executable) instead of moving the 3000 lines of ebuild-phase code into a library. The fixture repo without `metadata/md5-cache` gives the same output as with it (`tests/no_md5_cache.rs`); the cost is one bash process per uncached ebuild. Sets added since: `OwnerSet` (`@module-rebuild`, `@x11-module-rebuild`; `@x11-module-rebuild` matches Portage's 8 members exactly), `ChangedDepsSet` and `RebuiltBinaries` (both empty in Portage on this host; Portage's `@changed-deps` is always empty by a bug, `LLM/portage-quirks.md` Q29, so the experiment builds the intended set through the new `portage_repo::installed_deps_changed` and finds 5 packages). `@preserved-rebuild` (`PreservedLibraryConsumerSet`) is read too: `needed_elf.rs` (the `NEEDED.ELF.2` linkage map) moved from the `portuale` binary into `portage_repo::needed_elf`, together with the read side of the preserved-libs registry (`preserved_lib_paths`, `prune_preserved_paths`) and `preserved_rebuild_atoms` (`portage_repo::preserved_libs`); the binary keeps a thin `needed_elf` shim for `generate_soname_deps`, which needs its own `fnmatch`. It is empty in Portage and in the experiment on this host; on a synthetic root with a registry entry Portage's linkage map found no consumer where portuale's `@preserved-rebuild` (pinned by the contract suite) names one, so it is not confirmed against Portage on non-empty data. Still unsupported: `@security` (`NewAffectedSet`: needs a GLSA reader, about 800 lines of `glsa.py`; empty on this host and unchecked against Portage). Idea 5 is taken for the INVALID report (#691: library crate `portage-world` and `mrg --check-world`); the experiment binary stays as a front end; the cross-check and the `emerge -uDNp @world`-like commands are #692 and #693. Tier 2. Premise: n/a — an experiment; the charter's health properties are the spec and Portage is not run as an oracle.
685. **FIXED 2026-11-08 on branch `backlog/685-xcore-llvm-abort`, uncommitted (plan `LLM/02.685-slot-scoped-pin.opus.md`; cause: `apply_enforced_pin` filed the pin `~llvm-23.1.4` of installed lld/lldb/clang-23.1.4 as a positive constraint on the whole `llvm-core/llvm`, so the slot-22 request `~llvm-22.1.8` of `clang-22.1.8` found no candidate; fix: a slot-naming positive constraint now spares the other slots, one predicate `extra_constraint_admits` replaces eight copies, and the pin is filed under the one broken slot; rc 0, same plan lines on every run; no pmtest fixture and no bed run yet) — original: filed 2026-11-08, found while fixing #682; owner asked) — `portuale emerge -pvuDN --getbinpkg=n media-video/ffmpeg` aborts with `there are no ebuilds to satisfy "~llvm-core/llvm-22.1.8[llvm_targets_XCore=]"` (rc 1); the host Portage 3.0.83 gives rc 0 on the same command.** Since the #682 fix the output is the same on every run (30 runs, one md5), so this is no longer an intermittence but a plain resolver result. portuale prints the error with the chain `llvm-core/clang-23.1.5-r1` [ebuild] <- `dev-lang/python-3.14.10_p1[tail-call-interp]` [ebuild] <- `app-accessibility/at-spi2-core-2.60.7` [installed], after `Dependency resolution took (backtrack: 4/20)`, then a skip block on `dev-libs/openssl:0` (4.1.0 against `app-crypt/rhash` wanting `openssl:0/3=`). Portage's run on the same host prints a different skip block (`app-misc/mime-types[nginx]` against `www-servers/nginx`) and exits 0. Not yet known: whether the `llvm-22.1.8` pin is a real constraint (clang-23.1.5-r1 depends on `~llvm-core/llvm-${PV}` or on a different version, so a `22.1.8` pin looks wrong for a 23.x clang) or an artefact of the installed `llvm-22.1.8` being kept by an earlier choice. First step: probe both package managers with identical effective options (see the `getbinpkg` probe trap in memory) and compare the llvm/clang choice; use `portuale emerge --debug` for the trace. Charter ground: an abort where Portage plans is a wrong plan for the user, so it counts even though the two skip blocks differ. Residue of #682 (not a sort problem). Tier 2.

686. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branch `fix-686-689-690` in both repos; `constraints_from_consumer` walked a `HashSet` of a consumer's dependency atoms, so which `[llvm_targets_X=]` pin was filed first, and so which row the block printed, changed per run; the atoms are sorted now, six runs of the repro give one md5) — originally OPEN (filed 2026-11-08, found while fixing #685) — the row order inside the `WARNING: One or more updates/rebuilds have been skipped` block changes from run to run** (`portuale emerge -pvuDN --getbinpkg=n --backtrack=0 media-video/ffmpeg`, four runs, four md5s; the plan lines are identical). The `~llvm-core/llvm-23.1.4[llvm_targets_X=]` rows come out in hash order. Charter: a result that changes between runs. Likely a `HashSet`/`HashMap` iteration in the skip-block printer; sort by a total key. Tier 2.
687. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branches `health-installed-state` in both repos; pmtest and portuale commits quote each other) — the system a real merge leaves is checked against charter properties 1 and 4.** `health_checker.py --installed ROOT [--baseline BEFORE.jsonl]` (pmtest `pytests-contract-suite/`) reads the VDB and files under ROOT as a plan that merges nothing: runtime and post-merge dependencies, blockers, `REQUIRED_USE` and one-package-per-slot (the plan checker's `check_final_state`), plus every installed ELF's needed sonames found by the dynamic linker (a preserved library is a file on disk, so it counts). A library installed outside the search path is a `private-library` note (a JDK, systemd), and files of an ABI the system cannot run (a vendored ppc64 or arm library) are skipped; without those two rules this host's real root gave 33 false positives, with them 0 failures on 2376 packages in about 30 s. `--baseline` is the output of the same run before the merge, so only what the merge introduced fails. The L1 bed runs it inside each consumer before and after the merge (`layers/l1/consume.sh`) and `compare/health-state-report.py` appends the result to `l1-report.txt`; a failure portuale introduced makes the run exit 1, Portage's result is a control. `compare/health-state-mutants.py ROOT` is its mutation test (5 mutants and a control, all as expected on this host's root), and `test_portuale.py::test_a_real_merge_leaves_a_healthy_installed_state` merges a package into a temporary root and checks it. Gate: `l1-merge-gate.txt` (glibc + bash), build then `L1_SKIP_BUILD=1 L1_CONSUME_REINSTALL=1`, both rc 0, merged 2/2, 0 hard findings, health 0 introduced (log `l1-20261108T213806Z`). Not covered, filed as #688 (property 3 skips binary packages) and #689 (the check does not yet run after the L3 source builds or the L32 life-cycle cells); order (2) and newest version (3) cannot be read from a final state. No product code changed. Premise: n/a — a new check; the charter's properties are the spec and Portage is not run.
688. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branches `health-best-version-binary` in both repos; owner asked, follows #687) — property 3 (newest visible version) now covers binary packages.** `health_checker.py` `check_best_versions` no longer skips a merged binary. It reads the local binary index (`PKGDIR/Packages`: version, slot, `KEYWORDS`, `LICENSE`, `USE`, `IUSE`, `BUILD_ID`) as a second candidate pool next to the repositories' ebuilds and applies the same mask, keyword, license and fit logic; a binary whose baked USE differs from the one the package would get is not a candidate (`--binpkg-respect-use`). Which pool counts comes from a new `options` object in `emerge --pretend -v --json` (`usepkg`, `usepkgonly`, `getbinpkg`, `getbinpkgonly`, as given; the only product change, `crates/portuale/src/pretend/json.rs`): a higher binary than a merged binary is a failure in every mode, a higher ebuild is a failure with `--usepkg` and not with `--usepkgonly`, and a higher binary than a merged ebuild is a failure only with `--usepkg`. For a plan without `options` (printed before this change) a higher ebuild beside a merged binary is only a `checker-limitation` note, because the run may have been `--usepkgonly`. Not read: a remote binhost (`--getbinpkg`), noted once per plan, and a flat PKGDIR without an index line, noted per package; filed as #690. Tests: seven unit tests of the candidate rules, a portuale end-to-end test of the `options` key for `--usepkg` and `--usepkgonly`, the mutation operator `older_visible_binary` (`health-mutants.py`) with a test, and the Rust unit test `candidate_options_json_names_the_four_binary_options`. Premise: n/a — the charter's property 3 is the spec and Portage is not run.
689. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branch `fix-686-689-690` in both repos; `layers/l3/build-and-merge.sh` and `layers/l32/run-cell.sh` run `health_checker.py --installed / [--baseline before]` around the build and the cell; `run/l3-source-parity.sh` and `run/l32-lifecycle.sh` mount the checker and grade portuale's introduced failures with `health-state-report.py` (Portage's run is the control); not yet run on a real L3/L32 bed, only `bash -n` and the L3 mock test) — originally OPEN (filed 2026-11-08, found while closing #687) — run the installed-state check after the L3 source builds and the L32 life-cycle cells.** #687 added it to the L1 bed only. L3 builds a real closure from source under both PMs and L32 exercises `-C`, `--depclean`, soname bumps (preserved libs) and fault injection; both leave a real root where `health_checker.py --installed / --baseline <before>` can run, and L32's preserved-libs cell is where property 4 matters most. Grade portuale's introduced failures and keep Portage's as a control, as `run/l1-merge-from-binpkg.sh` does. Premise: n/a — the charter's properties are the spec.
690. **DONE 2026-11-08 (branch `fix-686-689-690` in both repos; `health_checker.py` reads the cached index of every `binrepos.conf`/`PORTAGE_BINHOST` binhost (`var/cache/edb/binhost/<host>/<path>/Packages`, never the network) and the `.tbz2`/`.gpkg.tar` files of PKGDIR no index line lists, slot and USE from the file's own xpak/gpkg metadata; the `--getbinpkg` note remains only when no binhost is configured) — originally OPEN (filed 2026-11-08, found while closing #688) — property 3 does not read a remote binary index or a flat PKGDIR.** `check_best_versions` only knows binary candidates from `PKGDIR/Packages`. With `--getbinpkg` the run could pick a binary from a remote binhost the checker never sees, and portuale also reads a flat `PKGDIR` of `*.tbz2`/`*.gpkg.tar` files that have no index line (the fixture tree is one). Both are reported as `checker-limitation` notes today. Read the binhost indexes named in `binrepos.conf`/`PORTAGE_BINHOST` (a cached copy, never a network call from the checker) and fall back to the file names of a flat PKGDIR for version and slot. Premise: n/a — the charter's property 3 is the spec.
691. **DONE 2026-11-09 (branch `backlog/684-experiment-pre-sort-atoms`, owner asked; idea 5 of #684 taken for the INVALID report) — `mrg --check-world` lists what `@world` needs and no visible ebuild gives.** The calculation of the experiment is now the library crate `portage-world` (`analyse`, `seeds`, `Options`, `World`, `SlotSet`; the experiment binary is a thin front end), and `mrg` exposes it as a portuale-only action. It prints `INVALID cat/pkg:slot` (every version is masked or none fits the atoms aimed at the slot) and `NO MATCH atom  <- who asked` (no ebuild matches the atom with this configuration: the package is gone, no version is high enough, or no version has the USE a `[flag]` asks for), then `check-world: N atoms, M package slots, K unsatisfiable`; exit 1 when K > 0, 2 when the tree cannot be read. `NO MATCH` is new: before it the experiment ignored an atom that pointed at no slot at all, which is the case a health check exists for (a removed package in `@world`). On this host: 0.35 s, 4535 atoms, one finding, `app-misc/mime-types[nginx] <- www-servers/nginx-1.31.7-r1` (`mime-types-2.1.54` declares the flag, it is off; the same pair is Portage's skip block in #685). Tests: three in `crates/portuale/src/check_world.rs` (a clean world exits 0; a too-high version and a missing package are `NO MATCH`; a keyword-masked package is `INVALID`), the 20 unit tests moved with the code, the no-md5-cache test. Not done, on purpose: it knows nothing of blockers, `REQUIRED_USE` or backtracking, so a clean report does not promise that `emerge` succeeds; an ebuild whose `depend` phase fails is skipped without a word (to be counted and reported); no `--json` yet (the pmtest checker reads JSON); `@security` is still unread. Follow-ups: #692, #693. Tier 2. Premise: n/a — a new check; the charter's property 1 (dependencies hold) is the spec and Portage is not run as an oracle.
692. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-09, owner asked; follows #691) — cross-check `portage-world` against the resolver's plan, and mutation-test `--check-world`.** (a) Compare, for `-uDNp @world` and the same plan with `--getbinpkg=n`, the version the plan picks for each slot with the best version `portage-world` predicts, and list the disagreements; the last measure was 2 of 2076 installed slots, both from `buildovl`. A slot `portage-world` calls `INVALID` while the resolver plans it is the interesting direction: the plan is wrong or the program is. Where: pmtest next to `l0-health.sh`, one finding class in the L0 report, not a user command. (b) `--json` for `mrg --check-world`, the shape `health_checker.py` already reads. (c) A mutation test in the style of `health-mutants.py`: break one ebuild (mask it, drop a keyword, raise a `>=` bound, remove a package that `@world` names) on a copy of the fixture tree and require the check to fail, with a control that stays green. (d) Count the ebuilds whose metadata could not be read and say so, instead of dropping them. Tier 2. Premise: n/a — charter properties 1 and 3 are the spec.
693. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-09, owner asked; follows #691) — commands equivalent to `emerge -uDNp @world`, `-ep @world` and `-up @world` built on `portage-world`.** The idea is a fast plan of what would be installed, without the backtracking resolver: `-uDNp` = the best visible version of every slot `@world` reaches, deep, with new USE; `-ep` = every package of the closure, installed or not (`--emptytree`); `-up` = only `@world`'s own atoms, no deep. Home: `mrg` (the charter lets it have its own solver and share crates), never `emerge`, whose options keep Portage's meaning. First step, before any code: say what the output must contain (the order, the `[ebuild N U ]` rows or a simpler list), what it is compared with (the resolver's plan, #692) and what it does not do (blockers, slot conflicts, `REQUIRED_USE`, merge order). Ordering is the open question: `portage-world` orders versions inside a slot, not packages against each other. **Progress 2026-11-09 (owner chose the option shape: `--sort alpha|depend|resolver`):** `mrg --plan-world [--sort alpha|depend|resolver]` is the `-uDNp @world` equivalent: one row `[ebuild  N     ] cat/pkg-ver:slot` (or `U`/`D` with the installed version) per slot `@world` reaches whose best visible version is not the installed one. `alpha` sorts by `cat/pkg:slot` and says nothing of build order; `depend` puts a slot after the slots it depends on (`portage_world::Order::Depend`, a depth-first walk in name order over the first pass's edges, a cycle cut where the walk meets it; the edges do not tell `RDEPEND` from `PDEPEND`); `resolver` runs `emerge --pretend --update --deep --newuse --getbinpkg=n @world` as a child process and sorts by the position of each row in that plan (about 20 s here; a slot the plan lacks comes last). On this host: 236 rows in 0.36 s (`alpha`, `depend`) against 240 `U`/`N` rows in the resolver's plan; the resolver adds 107 reinstalls (`R`: slot operators, USE changes) that this command does not see, and this command lists `dev-util/spirv-headers` which the resolver does not (look at it under #692). Bug found on the way and fixed: an atom without a slot aims at every slot of its package, so the first version listed every old `app-shells/bash` slot as new; only slots the first pass followed count (`World::reached`). Tests: 4 in `plan_world.rs` on the fixture tree (alpha exact; depend keeps dependencies first; resolver equals the resolver's own order, which differs from alpha there; `--sort` alone is a usage error), 3 on the dependency walk in `order.rs`. Still to do for #693: `-ep` (whole closure, `--emptytree`), `-up` (no `--deep`), `[ebuild R]` rebuilds, and whether `depend` should keep `PDEPEND` out of the edges. Tier 2. Premise: n/a — a design item; nothing is claimed against Portage.

Fixed: `unsatisfied_system_runtime_deps` fills the set when a `@system` (deep) entry lands at `ROOT=/`, `note_landed` drains it as the children land, and `system_deps_hold_new_builds` stops the dispatch loop from starting a new build while the set is non-empty and a build is running. 582. **DONE 2026-10-26 (plan 02.589 Task 4, branch `fix/2026-10-26-575-residues`, no pmtest half). Filed 2026-10-26, residue of #575 plan Task 4 — binpkg verification has no `RMD160`/`WHIRLPOOL` hasher.** Portage verifies every valid checksum key a `Packages` record carries (`lib/portage/dbapi/bintree.py` `_get_digests`, `lib/_emerge/BinpkgVerifier.py` `_start`); on the reference host that includes `RMD160` (hashlib) and `WHIRLPOOL` (bundled pure-Python, dropped by `_filter_unaccelarated_hashes` unless it is the only one). portuale's `binpkg::digest::VERIFIED_DIGESTS` covers the other eight, so a record carrying `SIZE` plus only `RMD160` or only `WHIRLPOOL` is checked on size alone. Also, `PORTAGE_CHECKSUM_FILTER` is applied to the eight verifiable names only, so a filter that keeps only one of those two keys picks a different survivor than Portage. Fix: add the `ripemd` crate (and a whirlpool hasher if the owner accepts the dependency), or refuse such records. Evidence: `LLM/evidence/2026-10-25-review/t4/S0.md`. Tier 3. [I] Oracle: `LLM/evidence/2026-10-25-review/t4/S0.md` (reference-host digest set incl. `RMD160`/`WHIRLPOOL`).

583. **DONE 2026-10-26 (branch `fix/2026-10-25-review`: portuale `af8494bf`, no pmtest half). Filed 2026-10-26, found in #575 plan Task 6 — a versioned `slotmove` atom is dropped instead of applied.** Portage's `parse_updates` (`lib/portage/update.py:203-219`) accepts any non-blocker atom as the `slotmove` package, for example `slotmove >=dev-libs/foo-2 0 1`. `dbapi.move_slot_ent` (`lib/portage/dbapi/__init__.py`) then matches it with `match_from_list`, so only the matching versions are reslotted. portuale's shared `portage_repo::parse_updates_content` (`crates/portage-repo/src/updates.rs`) takes a bare `cat/pkg` only and treats a versioned one as a no-op. As a result, the global-updates vdb and binpkg `move_slot_ent` paths skip that line, and the interim applied-updates scan no longer counts it as a valid directive. Dependency-string rewrites (`update_dbentry`, `update.py:69-74`) skip a versioned slotmove in Portage too, so `update_ents` and the read-path metadata remap are already right. Task 6 deleted the local copy in `pretend/global_updates.rs` that did accept it. Fix: keep the atom in `UpdateCmd::SlotMove`, match versions in `move_slot_ent` only, and add a fixture line with a versioned slotmove. Tier 3. [I] Oracle: Portage `parse_updates`/`move_slot_ent` (`lib/portage/update.py:203-219`, `lib/portage/dbapi/__init__.py`) + the versioned-slotmove fixture line (unit-pinned).

584. **DONE 2026-10-26 (branch `fix/2026-10-25-review`: portuale `2b52ead2`, pmtest `e687e6d`). Filed 2026-10-26, found in #575 plan Task 11 — global updates do not run for `--deselect`, `--list-sets`, `--regen` and `--metadata`.** Portage's `run_action` (`lib/_emerge/actions.py:3933-3960`) calls `_global_updates` for every action except help/info/sync/version. portuale's `pretend::run` handles those four actions in `validate_and_early_actions` (`crates/portuale/src/pretend/mod.rs`) and returns before the `global_updates_force` call, so on a root with unrecorded `profiles/updates` files they neither print the block nor apply the moves, while Portage does both. Fix: move the updates call ahead of these early actions, keeping the `--version` and `--help` skips, and add one pin per action on a fresh root. Tier 3. [I] Oracle: Portage `run_action` (`lib/_emerge/actions.py:3933-3960`) + the four per-action pins (portuale `2b52ead2`, pmtest `e687e6d`).

585. **DONE 2026-10-26 (plan 02.589 Task 2, branch `fix/2026-10-26-575-residues`). Filed 2026-10-26, owner ruling on the #575 plan Task 11 question — write a fresh or rewritten `mtimedb` in Portage's full shape.** Portage's `MtimeDB._load` (`lib/portage/util/mtimedb.py:15-23`, `:79-90`) fills `starttime` (0), `version` (""), `info`, `ldpath` and `updates` ({}) on every load, drops any key outside `_MTIMEDBKEYS` with `Deleting invalid mtimedb key: <k>`, and `commit` writes the whole dict (tab indent, sorted keys). portuale's `crates/portuale/src/mtimedb.rs` writes only the sections it manages and keeps every other key untouched, so a new file has different bytes and a stray key survives. Fix: on write, emit the default keys Portage would have, and drop and warn about keys outside the set, the way Portage does; pin the bytes of a fresh file and of a file with a stray key against host Portage. Tier 3. [I] Oracle: host Portage `MtimeDB` bytes for a fresh file and a stray-key file (pinned byte-identical).

586. **DONE 2026-10-26 (branch `fix/2026-10-25-review`: pmtest `19ecaea`, no portuale code). Filed 2026-10-26, owner ruling on the #575 plan Task 11 question — guard the contract suite against changing the host when run as root.** Global updates now run before every emerge, so a suite run as root with `PORTAGE_RUNNING_ROOT=/` (several tests set it on purpose, and about 146 call sites build their env from `os.environ` without `fixture_env`) would apply the host's unrecorded `profiles/updates` moves and write `/var/cache/edb/mtimedb`. The owner chose two guards in pmtest's `conftest.py`: (A) refuse to start when `os.geteuid() == 0` unless `PMTEST_ALLOW_ROOT=1`, with a clear message; (B) a session sentinel that fingerprints the host's `/var/cache/edb/mtimedb` (and the running root's vdb top-level mtime) at session start and fails the session if it changed at the end, for any user. Both must work under `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` (xdist: the controller checks once). Standalone pmtest change. Tier 3. [I] Oracle: the two `conftest.py` guards (as-root refusal message unless `PMTEST_ALLOW_ROOT=1`; session sentinel over `/var/cache/edb/mtimedb`).

Tier 3. [C]

630. **DONE 2026-10-30 (branch `slice/630-namespaces` in both repos: portuale `3442b48d`..`5dd9717b` — `b8aef41f` review touch-ups, `d67d4cf7` pid kept dropped, `5dd9717b` tests/evidence; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change) — a demoted phase keeps Portage's `net`/`ipc`/`mount` namespaces by unsharing raw in its own `pre_exec` while still root, then demoting in the same child.** `phase_isolation` no longer zeroes the wrappers when demoted (only the `usersandbox` fs rule stays demotion-dependent, `free = droppriv and not usersandbox`, `doebuild.py:2233-2240`); the demoted spawn bypasses the `unshare(1)` shim — which would demote the wrapper process instead of the phase, and carries `--map-root-user` — via a direct spawn with the same fully-built argv (`libsandbox` prefix preserved), and `pre_exec` runs `libc::unshare(clone_flags_for(&iso))` → `mount --make-rslave /` (ignored; Portage warns, `process.py:1215-1225`) → `sethostname("localhost")` (ignored, `:1246-1261`) → the existing `setgid → setgroups → setuid → umask`, all `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`-gated, with no subprocess, allocation or print in the child (the hostname `CString` is parent-built). No userns appears on the path because the child is root at `pre_exec` and needs no uid map (`process.py:1061-1084`): **the original entry's full-uid-map userns restoration sketch is superseded — Portage never creates a userns here (no `CLONE_NEWUSER`), so do not "restore" it.** Pins: `clone_flags_mirror_portages_unshare_bits`, `degrade_message_names_the_demoted_phase`, `demoted_phase_isolation_drops_pid_keeps_net`, and the root-gated `demoted_phase_with_network_sandbox_has_no_egress` (RED pre-fix with the host default route present; GREEN post-fix on an empty `/proc/net/route` plus a failed S0 connect probe, with the `Isolation::default()` control still seeing the host route) and `demoted_usersandbox_spawn_stays_demoted` (non-isolating control: passes pre-fix too, child still the portage uid). Acceptance on the final binary: `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0, `Completed (1 of 1) app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0::gentoo` (`LLM/evidence/2026-10-30-630/hyperfine-acceptance.log`; rc is report-only, the log has no rc echo line), matching the green Portage single-phase oracle (`s0-portage-unpack.log` rc=0). Limits, all filed: a demoted `pid-sandbox` stays dropped with its own once-per-process parent-side warning — `CLONE_NEWPID` without Portage's `pid-ns-init` re-exec is fatal, not degraded (first fork becomes init, its exit tears the namespace down, later forks fail `ENOMEM`, `task-2-pid-gap.log`; #635), so `/proc` keeps the host view and a demoted phase's own `readlink /proc/self/ns/pid` equals the host's; `lo` stays down in a demoted netns (no in-`pre_exec` setup; Portage configures it via netlink, #636). Close-out verification on this tree: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, workspace `cargo test --release` 1369 passed / 1 failed (known host-state `embedded_runtime`) / 3 ignored (`--no-fail-fast` confirms no other red), `sudo <test-binary> demoted_` 7 passed / 0 failed (five root-gated demoted tests plus the two plain unit tests the same filter matches), pmtest `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed with zero corpus drift — the 7 are the known host-state `mrg_remote` family (root-only `/etc/portage/gnupg.getuto.tmp/private-keys-v1.d`, `pulling /etc/portage` tar exit 2 as uid 1000) and the #632/#633 unexplained-once flakes did not recur; the default basetemp is still poisoned by root-owned residue (#634), so the run used `PMTEST_BASETEMP=/var/tmp/pytest-parallel-630`. The L0 differential bed was not run: its probes are resolver-only and never execute phases, so the spawn-path change is unobservable there (portuale pretend output byte-identical, zero corpus drift); L1 is not triggered (no merge-path file touched), and the next resolver-touching slice re-triggers it. The `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]` demoted-namespace path is reasoned-only (no BSD toolchain on this host, so it has never been compiled); the non-Linux compile check is filed as #637. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of #627) — a demoted phase skips the `net`/`ipc`/`pid` namespaces that Portage applies to it even while demoted.** Portage's `_exec` unshares at `process.py:1061` while still uid 0, then demotes in the same child, so `FEATURES=network-sandbox` still blocks egress for `unpack`/`prepare`/`configure`/`compile`/`test`; portuale's demoted phases run with no namespace wrappers at all (`sandbox.rs` `phase_isolation` zeroes them when `demoted`), because a single-id `--map-root-user` ns cannot host the demoted uid and `setuid` out of it is not possible. libsandbox still applies with `usersandbox` and DAC as `portage:portage` always applies, so this is a confinement gap, not an ownership one. Restoration needs a full-uid-map userns (Portage's own map) with an in-namespace `setuid`, rather than the current single-id `--map-root-user`. Oracle: a demoted phase with `network-sandbox` set observing egress that Portage would refuse. Tier 3. [C]

631. **DONE 2026-10-30 (branch `slice/631-umask`: portuale `4de57c98`; no pmtest half) — a demoted phase applies `022` itself, in Portage's order, instead of inheriting the caller's mask.** The demotion `pre_exec` (`commands.rs`) now calls `libc::umask(0o22)` after the `setuid` block, mirroring `_exec`'s `setgid → setgroups → setuid → umask` (`process.py:1265-1274`; the keyword comes from `doebuild.py:2178`), on the `droppriv: Some(…)` path only, so root `setup`/`install` keep the inherited mask. `umask(2)` returns the previous mask and cannot fail: no error branch, and `pre_exec` stays async-signal-safe. Pinned by the root-gated `demoted_spawn_applies_the_portage_umask` — caller mask set to the hostile `002`, demoted child reports `0022` and creates `644 f` / `755 d`, the `droppriv: None` control still reports `0002`, and the parent's mask reads back unchanged after the restore guard drops; SKIP-passes when `euid != 0` or the `portage` user is unresolvable, so a plain suite run stays green. `cargo test --release -p portuale ebuild_phases`: 146 passed, 3 ignored; whole workspace and the pmtest contract suite (4617 passed, 2 skipped, 2 xfailed) at close-out: proven host-state reds — 1 cargo `embedded_runtime` + 7 pytest `mrg_remote`, root-only `gnupg.getuto.tmp` → tar exit 2 as uid 1000 — plus two unexplained-once flakes that passed alone (2 gpkg oracle tests under one no-fail-fast cargo run, `zstd: unsupported format`, #632; 1 pty timeout `test_terminal_title_absent_without_a_tty_gates`, #633), zero corpus drift, and the default pytest basetemp poisoned by root-owned residue (`PMTEST_BASETEMP` workaround, #634; owner action `sudo rm -rf /var/tmp/pytest-parallel`). Limit: a demoted phase still gets no `net`/`ipc`/`pid` namespaces — #630.** Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of #627/#628) — a demoted phase keeps the caller's umask instead of Portage's 022.** Portage's droppriv keywords carry `"umask": 0o22` (`doebuild.py:2173-2179`) and `_exec` applies it (`process.py:1273-1274`) in the same pre-exec sequence (`setgid → setgroups → setuid → umask`) before `execve`; portuale's demotion `pre_exec` (`commands.rs:767-778`, #627) sets gid/groups/uid but never calls `umask`, so a demoted phase inherits whatever umask portuale was started with and creates `0664`/`0775` files where Portage makes `0644`/`0755`. Task 2's brief specified only the three-id sequence, so this fell outside that slice; the hyperfine acceptance did not expose it because the merge normalises staged modes. Follow-up: add `libc::umask(0o22)` in the demotion `pre_exec` (after `setuid`, mirroring `_exec`), or record a `LLM/portage-quirks.md` entry if the divergence is judged intentional. Oracle: a demoted phase started under a 002 caller umask creating a file and a directory, modes compared against Portage's. Tier 3. [C]

632. **DONE 2026-11-06 (portuale, standalone) — the flaky `helpers::gpkg` oracle pair was a `TempDir` name collision, not a shared `/dev/shm` race.** Cause (reproduced, 5 failures in 320 concurrent runs of the oracle tests, 0 in 480 after the fix): `TempDir::new_in` named a directory `portuale-td-{tag}-{pid}-{nanos}` and used `create_dir_all`, so two tests of one process that read the same clock tick got the same path, adopted each other's directory, and one test's `Drop` deleted the other's scratch root (hence `zstd -d` on a truncated stream, the vanished `portage-binpkg-gpg.lock` / `pubring.kbx`, and an `oracle_empty` that saw `oracle_non_utf8_name`'s image). The stale-dir sweep was ruled out (a logging probe never fired). Fix: a per-process sequence number in the name (`{tag}-{seq}-{pid}-{nanos}`, still parsed by the sweep) and an exclusive `create_dir` that retries on `AlreadyExists`; test `concurrent_temp_dirs_never_share_a_path` (16 threads x 2000 guards). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of the #631 close-out run) — the `helpers::gpkg` oracle pair failed once in a full no-fail-fast `cargo test`, then passed alone and on rerun.** `oracle_plain` died at `gpkg.rs:1750`'s `assert!(out.status.success(), "{tool} -d failed")` after `zstd: /*stdin*\: unsupported format`, and `oracle_signed` at `gpkg.rs:2143` with `flock: cannot open lock file …/dev/shm/portuale-td-gpkg-case-465337-…/portage-binpkg-gpg.lock: No such file or directory` (`GPGException`). Both cases likely share one `/dev/shm/portuale-td-gpkg-case-*` scratch root (only `oracle_signed`'s case dir appears in the log; unconfirmed for `oracle_plain`), so the working hypothesis is a data/race in that shared scratch namespace (a truncated/foreign zstd stream into `decompress`, `gpkg.rs:1740-1755`; a lock file unlinked under a concurrent signer) — not established. Both pass alone (`cargo test --release -p portuale --bin portuale helpers::gpkg::tests::oracle` → 8 passed, 0.43 s) and both passed in the rerun. Observed: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-631-umask/task-2-report.md`, Step 3, `cargo-test-nff.log` (616.96 s). Next: reproduce under `--test-threads=1` and under parallel load, give each oracle case its own temp root, and if the lock-file unlink is the trigger hold the lock for the signer's lifetime. Oracle: a second failing run that names the colliding case directory, or a green `--test-threads=1` run with per-case temp roots proving the shared scratch root is the trigger. Tier 3. [C]

633. **DONE 2026-11-06 (pmtest `240fc55`, standalone) — `test_terminal_title_absent_without_a_tty_gates` timed out because `_run_on_pty` raced the child's exit; it was the test, not the product and not load alone.** Reproduced with 32 parallel pytest processes (14 failures in 160; 0 in 160 after the fix). When the child closes the pty slave the master read raises `EIO` and the loop breaks; the helper then called `proc.poll()` once, and a child still tearing down returned `None`, so the helper killed it and raised `pty child timed out; got so far: <full output>` at once (not after 120 s). It now `wait`s out the remaining deadline and only then kills. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of the #631 close-out run) — `test_terminal_title_absent_without_a_tty_gates` timed out once in the full pytest suite; unexplained, passes alone once.** The test printed its full intended output, then failed `pty child timed out` in `pytest2.log`; it passes alone (`scripts/pytest-parallel.sh -k test_terminal_title_absent_without_a_tty_gates` → 1 passed in 3.64 s). The close-out report called it load-related, but that was never measured — no second reproduction, no isolation from a pty/read race — so the cause is unexplained. Observed: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-631-umask/task-2-report.md`, Step 3. Next: rerun the full suite; if it recurs, instrument the pty read/exit wait (child reaped while EOF never arrives, or the gate's own timeout too short) before attributing it to load. Oracle: a second full-suite failure with the pty read/reap instrumented, or no recurrence across repeated full runs. Tier 3. [C]

634. **DONE 2026-11-01 (branch `backlog/634-basetemp-guard`, pmtest only, uncommitted) — `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` reclaims its basetemp up front and exits actionably when it cannot.** The script resolves the effective basetemp (explicit `--basetemp` in argv wins, else `PMTEST_BASETEMP`, else `/var/tmp/pytest-parallel`), `rm -rf`s it before pytest starts, and when residue survives (root-owned files from an earlier root run, which this user cannot remove) prints the path with the two fixes (`sudo rm -rf <path>`, or `PMTEST_BASETEMP=<fresh-dir>`) and exits 2 -- pytest never starts, so no `INTERNALERROR` rc 3. Verified both ways: a sudo-poisoned scratch basetemp exits 2 with the message in under a second (pre-fix the same invocation `INTERNALERROR`d with `PermissionError`), and a default run from a cleaned `/var/tmp/pytest-parallel` completes (4619 passed, only the 7 known host-state `mrg_remote` reds). No product change; standalone pmtest commit (no portuale half). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of the #631 close-out run) — the default pytest-parallel basetemp is poisoned by root-owned residue, so a default run aborts before any test.** `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh`'s session-start cleanup of `/var/tmp/pytest-parallel/popen-gw6/test_merge_skips_an_identical_0/root/…` hit root-owned files from an earlier root suite run (`…/usr/share/binpkgrmpkg`, `root root 755`, 09:50 today) and died `PermissionError` → rc 3 `INTERNALERROR`; the close-out suite ran with `PMTEST_BASETEMP=/var/tmp/pytest-parallel-631`. The default path is still poisoned. Owner action: `sudo rm -rf /var/tmp/pytest-parallel`. Observed: `.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-631-umask/task-2-report.md`, "Host-state issue found (not fixed, per brief)". Next (optional, same item): make the script report an unremovable basetemp path and exit actionably instead of `INTERNALERROR`. Oracle: a default `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` run completing from a cleaned `/var/tmp/pytest-parallel`. Tier 3. [C]

635. **DONE 2026-10-30 (branch `slice/635-pid-init` in both repos: portuale `0554fa96`..`f71215de` product+tests, `1cfcba8f` fix round, `ca8ab480` evidence only, plus this docs-only close-out; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-10-30-635/`) — a demoted `pid-sandbox` phase runs as PID 1 of its own pid namespace with a namespace-local `/proc`, via a fork-and-reap dance inside the demotion `pre_exec`, with no helper binary and no re-exec.** Cause: `CLONE_NEWPID` in the demoted `pre_exec` without Portage's PID-1 re-exec is fatal, not degraded — the first fork becomes init, its exit tears the namespace down, and later forks fail `ENOMEM` (rc 254; `LLM/evidence/2026-10-30-630/task-2-pid-gap.log`). Fix shape: `phase_isolation` keeps `pid` for demoted phases and the retired "not applied" warning is gone; the demoted `pre_exec` blocks the forward set (`INT`/`TERM`/`HUP`/`TSTP`/`CONT`, Portage `process.py:1132`) in a stack-local `sigset_t` before `fork`, the grandchild is pid 1 by fork order and does `mount --make-slave /proc` plus a fresh `proc` mount (`process.py:1228/1226-1245`; either failure is `Err`, like Portage), unblocks the inherited mask, then demotes and execs; the middle `setsid`s, `close_range(3, ..)`s, and runs a zero-timeout `sigtimedwait` forward loop with `waitpid(WNOHANG)` polling, re-`_exit`ing with the child's code or re-raising a fatal signal like `pid-ns-init:140-144` (it never returns). `spawn_bash_script` takes `ParentGroup` for demoted+pid spawns so the chained `NewSession` hook cannot `setsid` the grandchild out of kill reach (ParentGroup kills by pid, `scheduler_registry.rs:157-160`). Pins: `demoted_phase_isolation_keeps_pid_with_net` (unit) plus the root-gated `demoted_pid_ns_has_init_reaping_and_local_proc` (multi-fork; `rc 0`, `id -u` still the portage uid, pid ns differs from the host, `/proc/1/comm == bash`) and `demoted_pid_ns_forwards_fatal_signals` (trap turns a forwarded signal into `exit 7`), RED before and GREEN after; serial run `sudo <test-binary> demoted_pid --test-threads=1` 2 passed, and the wider serial `demoted_` filter 8 passed. Acceptance (`app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0`, `src_compile` is the forking phase): `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0 and the same with `FEATURES="sandbox usersandbox userpriv network-sandbox pid-sandbox"` rc=0, both `>>> Completed (1 of 1)` (`hyperfine-acceptance.log`, `hyperfine-pid-sandbox-acceptance.log`), matching Portage's own pid-sandboxed `ebuild … compile` rc=0 with `>>> Source compiled.` (`portage-pid-oracle.txt`). **Those logs are supporting only, not a standalone PID-1 proof:** the pid log carries no invocation or `FEATURES` header — the root tests are the pin. Close-out verification on this docs-only tree: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, workspace `cargo test --release --no-fail-fast` 3271 passed / 1 failed / 5 ignored across 32 binaries (only the known red below), pmtest `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed, zero corpus drift (nothing blessed); the default basetemp was still poisoned by root-owned residue (#634), cleared with `sudo rm -rf /var/tmp/pytest-parallel` and re-ran. The single cargo red is the known host-state `embedded_runtime::tests::a_phase_running_merge_works_from_an_extracted_runtime` (`mrg: pulling /etc/portage from localtest failed (exit 2)`, network-dependent, disclosed in #630/#631); the seven pytest reds are the known `mrg_remote` family (root-only `/etc/portage/gnupg.getuto.tmp`, `pulling /etc/portage` tar exit 2 as uid 1000). Not run: the L0 differential bed (its probes are resolver-only and never execute phases, so the spawn path is unobservable) and L1 (no merge-path file touched). Residue decision: **no new item filed for `setsid`/`ctty` parity** — the middle's `setsid` matches `pid-ns-init:96`, and the only remaining source difference, `TIOCSCTTY` on a tty stdout (`:107-116`), cannot diverge: every demoted phase's stdout is a `Tee`/`Capture` pipe (never a tty), and on an inherited tty after `setsid` the ioctl answers `EPERM`, exactly the case `pid-ns-init` swallows (probe: `open(/dev/tty)`=ENXIO after `setsid`, EPERM for the parent-session tty). Dispositions, not residues: the utf8 re-exec (`pid-ns-init:9-14`) is N/A (no Python on this path); the autogroup-nice restore (`:93-107`) is moot (no `nice`/`setpriority` anywhere in `crates/portuale/src`); a SIGTERM-trapping grandchild subtree is not filed — init death is namespace teardown (`SIGKILL`) and no test shows a delivery gap. Still open from #630 and unchanged: `lo` stays down in a demoted netns (#636), and the non-Linux demoted path has never been compiled (#637). Tier 3. [C] Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of #630) — a demoted `pid-sandbox` phase is not PID 1, `pid` is not applied at all for demoted phases, and `/proc` keeps the host view.** Applying `CLONE_NEWPID` in the demoted `pre_exec` without Portage's PID-1 re-exec is fatal, not degraded: the first fork becomes the namespace init, its exit tears the namespace down, and every later fork fails `ENOMEM` (proven in `LLM/evidence/2026-10-30-630/task-2-pid-gap.log`: the first `bash -c` prints the new ns, the second dies `fork: Cannot allocate memory`, rc 254; reproduced as root and as portage alike, and byte-identically by an exact `unshare(NEWPID|NEWNS) → rslave → setuid → exec bash` mirror). Portage instead re-execs the phase through `bin/pid-ns-init` (`process.py:1111-1145`), which stays PID 1 for the phase's lifetime and mounts a fresh `/proc`; portuale therefore drops `pid` for demoted phases with a dedicated parent-side warning and never remounts `/proc` in `pre_exec` (without the re-exec a mount there would show the host view). Restoration shape: the re-exec (or a fork-then-unshare, `--fork`-shaped init) plus the `/proc` mount — do not "fix" this by re-enabling the raw flag, which kills every forking phase. Oracle: a demoted pid-sandboxed phase's `readlink /proc/self/ns/pid` differs from the host's, a multi-fork script exits 0, and `/proc/self` shows the namespace's own PID view. Tier 3. [C]

636. **DONE 2026-11-01 (branch `slice/636-loopback` in both repos: portuale `df585d7b` netlink builders+sender, `086de529` fix round (v6 bytes, ACK drain skip, GETLINK pin), `8b883d5c` spawn pins+evidence, plus this docs-only close-out; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-10-31-636/`) — a demoted `network-sandbox` phase gets a working loopback in its fresh netns via raw netlink in `pre_exec`, so localhost-dependent phases connect like Portage instead of seeing `lo` down.** Cause: #630's demoted path is a raw `unshare(2)` in `pre_exec`, where no subprocess may run, so Portage's `_configure_loopback_interface` (`process.py:838-871`: the `ip link set lo up` equivalent plus the bug-#690758 `AI_ADDRCONFIG` addresses) had no counterpart and `lo` stayed `down`/`unknown`. S0 ruling (`LLM/evidence/2026-10-31-636/`): `lo` flags read `0x9` in both states and `bind` succeeds either way (`s0-lo-down-probe.log`), so only a real connect discriminates — `CONNECT-FAIL … ENETUNREACH` before (`s0-lo-connect-probe.log`) against Portage's `CONNECT-OK 0x9` after `CFG-DONE` (`s0-portage-lo-oracle.log`). Fix shape: `sandbox.rs` mirrors `util/netlink.py`'s wire layout (`Nlmsghdr`/`IfInfoMsg`/`IfAddrMsg`/`RtAttr` with compile-time size asserts, `lo_nl` consts) and builds the four messages allocation-free on stack arrays — `lo_getlink_req` 38 B, `lo_link_up_msg` 32 B, `lo_add_v4_msg` 32 B, `lo_add_v6_msg` 44 B (the first sketch's 40/52 was wrong: `create_nlmsg`/`create_rtattr` pad nothing); `commands.rs::configure_loopback_netlink` opens `AF_NETLINK`/`NETLINK_ROUTE`, GETLINKs `b"lo"`, reads `ifi_index` at offset 20, then link-up, `10.0.0.1/8`, and `fd::1/8` iff the parent-side `host_has_ipv6()` (Portage `has_ipv6`, `process.py:823-835`) saw IPv6; one ACK/`NLMSG_ERROR` drained per request, every failure silent, no `Err`. Called from the demoted `pre_exec` when `iso.net`, after `sethostname` and before the pid fork, so the pid-1 grandchild inherits the already-configured shared netns. Pins: unit `lo_netlink_messages_mirror_portage_bytes` (header fields + address bytes) and the root-gated spawn tests `demoted_netns_loopback_accepts_localhost` / `demoted_pid_netns_loopback_works_in_pid1` — a real TCP round-trip (listener on `0.0.0.0`; a 127-bound listener RSTs the `10.0.0.1` leg, reproduced standalone against Portage's own `RtNetlink`) to both `127.0.0.1` and `10.0.0.1`, with #630's no-egress property re-asserted; serial `sudo <test-binary> demoted_ --test-threads=1` 10 passed / 0 failed. Acceptance is regression gate only: `app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0`, `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0 and the same with `FEATURES="sandbox usersandbox userpriv network-sandbox"` rc=0, both `>>> Completed (1 of 1)` (`hyperfine-acceptance.log`, `hyperfine-netsandbox-acceptance.log`); no ebuild on this host exercises localhost end to end, so those logs prove no merge regression, not loopback — the spawn tests are the localhost pin. Close-out verification (docs-only delta, product identical to `8b883d5c`): `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, workspace `cargo test --release --no-fail-fast` 3274 passed / 1 failed / 5 ignored across 32 binaries (only the known host-state red below), pmtest `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed, zero corpus drift (nothing blessed); the default basetemp was again poisoned by root-owned residue (#634), cleared with `sudo rm -rf /var/tmp/pytest-parallel` and re-ran. The single cargo red is the known host-state `embedded_runtime::tests::a_phase_running_merge_works_from_an_extracted_runtime` (`mrg: pulling /etc/portage from localtest failed (exit 2)`, disclosed at #630/#631/#635); the pytest reds are the known `mrg_remote` family, and the #632/#633 unexplained-once flakes did not recur. Honest limits: best-effort, never fatal — a failed request leaves `lo` down, exactly the pre-#636 state; `pre_exec` cannot print, so Portage's `Unable to configure loopback interface: <errno>` warning has no counterpart and the ACK error code is ignored where Portage abandons the remaining steps (filed as #638); the reply buffer is 512 B against Portage's 8 kB (truncation keeps the head the fixed offsets read, enough for one `lo` `RTM_NEWLINK`); the non-Linux demoted path is still uncompiled (#637). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of #630) — `lo` stays down inside a demoted `network-sandbox` netns, so localhost-dependent phases fail where Portage succeeds.** Portage brings loopback up via netlink (`_configure_loopback_interface`, `process.py:838-871`: the `ip link set lo up` equivalent plus the `AI_ADDRCONFIG` workaround addresses, bug #690758), and the root `unshare(1)` shim does the same via `ip`; the demoted path is a raw `unshare(2)` in `pre_exec`, where no subprocess may run (async-signal safety) and no netlink setup is implemented, so `lo` is `down`/`unknown` (S0 `s0-lo-state.log`). The guaranteed property — no default route, egress blocked — is what #630 pins; localhost connections are not. Oracle: `ping -c1 127.0.0.1` (or a localhost `connect`) inside a demoted network-sandboxed phase, portuale vs Portage. Tier 3. [C]

637. **DONE 2026-11-06 for its own scope (portuale, standalone; whole-crate BSD build filed as #665) — the Linux-only namespace code was compiled for a BSD target and is clean.** `LLM/tools/bsd_check.sh` runs `cargo check -Zbuild-std=std,panic_abort --target x86_64-unknown-freebsd -p portuale` (std built from the installed `rust-src` with `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1`; needs network for std's own deps). The `clone_flags_for` non-Linux stub and the demoted `pre_exec` region in `ebuild_phases/sandbox.rs` produce no error. The crate as a whole does not check green: 22 errors elsewhere (#665), so the "`cargo check` green" half of the oracle is not met. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-30, residue of #630) — the Linux-only demoted-namespace path has never been compiled for a non-Linux target.** The `clone_flags_for` non-Linux stub and the demoted `pre_exec` namespace block are both behind `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`; Linux compiles and tests them, but BSD support is a stated future portability requirement and no BSD target has ever been checked, so the non-Linux side is reasoned-only. A compile check is the cheapest oracle: a BSD target's `cargo check` green (or a documented equivalent, e.g. a CI cross-check job) with the stub compiling and the Linux-only block absent. No BSD toolchain exists on this host. Oracle: `cargo check --target <bsd-triple>` green, or the documented equivalent, against the current tree. Tier 3. [C]
638. **DONE 2026-11-01 (branch `slice/638-loopback-warn` in both repos: portuale `b962d453` first-failure sender + `CLOEXEC` report pipe + parent append + tests, `cb61ad50` review touch-ups (Linux gate on the injected test, S0 probe log), `de71ebcb` hyperfine acceptance log, plus this docs-only close-out; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-11-01-638/`) — a demoted netns loopback setup failure is now audible: the child reports its first failed netlink step to the parent over a `CLOEXEC` pipe and the parent appends Portage's `Unable to configure loopback interface: <strerror>` warning to the phase log, while the phase still runs best-effort.** Cause: #636's constraint that `pre_exec` may not print left every netlink failure silent, where Portage's forked `_exec2` prints that warning and skips the remaining requests (`process.py:861-871`), so a build log could not distinguish "loopback configured" from "link-up failed". Fix shape: `configure_loopback_netlink(has_ipv6, fail_at)` returns the first failure as `(step, errno)` — `0` ok / `1` GETLINK / `2` link-up / `3` v4 / `4` v6 — and stops there (Portage warn-and-abandon); a nonzero per-ACK `NLMSG_ERROR` is that step's failure (Portage `parse_message`, `lib/portage/util/netlink.py:52-56`), a non-`RTM_NEWLINK` GETLINK reply is `EPROTO` unless the kernel named an errno, and `EINTR` counts as failure (safe direction). The demoted child writes those two bytes to a `pipe2(O_CLOEXEC)` fd whose write end lives only in the stored `pre_exec` closure; after the phase is reaped and the pumps join, the parent reads the pair and appends `loopback_warn_msg` — Portage's text verbatim, the failed step deliberately unnamed (`process.py:867-871`) — to the `Capture`/`Tee` log (`OpenOptions::append`, errors ignored) or `eprint!` for `Inherit`/`Foreground`. Best-effort is preserved: only pipe creation can fail the spawn, and a failed `lo` setup still never fails the phase. Pins: root-gated `demoted_loopback_failure_warns_and_continues` (`PORTUALE_TEST_LOOPBACK_FAIL=linkup` → rc 0, no `10.0.0.1` route in `/proc/net/fib_trie`, warning as the log's last line; the clean control → route present, real localhost TCP round-trip, no warning; serialized by `LOOPBACK_HOOK_LOCK` because the hook is process-global) and `loopback_warn_msg_matches_portage_text` (fixed prefix + one trailing newline; body from the same libc `strerror` as Portage's `os.strerror`). S0 (`LLM/evidence/2026-11-01-638/s0-fib-trie-probe.log`): a fresh netns shows `0` hits for `10.0.0.1` in fib_trie, while the host shows `|-- 10.0.0.1` followed by the `/32 host LOCAL` local-route line — the exact shape the injected/control test greps (the `[|]` character class avoids ERE alternation). Acceptance is supporting only: `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0, `>>> Completed (1 of 1) app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0::gentoo` (`hyperfine-acceptance.log`, hyperfine 1.21.0). The failure path cannot trigger on this host, so that log carries no warning and proves only that the always-write/read/append plumbing did not regress a real merge — the spawn test is the pin, the acceptance run report-only. Close-out verification (docs-only delta, product identical to `de71ebcb`): `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, workspace `cargo test --release --no-fail-fast` 3276 passed / 1 failed / 5 ignored across 32 binaries, `sudo <test-binary> loopback --test-threads=1` 4 passed / 0 failed, pmtest `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed with zero corpus drift (nothing blessed); the default basetemp was again poisoned by root-owned residue (#634), cleared with `sudo rm -rf /var/tmp/pytest-parallel` and re-ran. The single cargo red is the known host-state `embedded_runtime::tests::a_phase_running_merge_works_from_an_extracted_runtime` failure (`mrg: pulling /etc/portage from localtest failed (exit 2)`, disclosed at #630/#631/#635/#636); the seven pytest reds are the known `mrg_remote` family (same exit-2 cause). Honest limits: the abort-the-rest shape is reviewer-pinned, not test-pinned — failing link-up also leaves the v4 route absent, so the injected cell's `V4_ROUTE=0` is consistent with both "stopped" and "ran and failed"; the message is identical by construction (same libc), not oracle-compared, so only the fixed prefix and trailing newline are asserted; `errno` is truncated to a byte (real Linux errnos < 134). Residues filed: #639 (a `.gz` log gets the warning as raw appended bytes, bypassing the gzip pump) and #640 (warning placement/visibility: appended after the phase output where Portage writes it before `execve`, and log-file-only for `Tee`/foreground spawns where Portage tees it to the terminal). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-01, residue of the #636 close-out) — a demoted netns loopback setup failure is silent: Portage's `Unable to configure loopback interface: <errno>` warning has no counterpart, and each request's ACK error code is ignored where Portage abandons the remaining steps.** `configure_loopback_netlink` (`crates/portuale/src/ebuild_phases/commands.rs`) is best-effort by #636's Global Constraints (`pre_exec` may not print), so a failed link-up or address add leaves `lo` down with no line anywhere; Portage's `parse_message` raises on a nonzero `NLMSG_ERROR` (`lib/portage/util/netlink.py:52-56`) and `_configure_loopback_interface` catches it, prints that warning on stderr and skips the rest (`process.py:867-871`) — visible in the phase log because Portage configures loopback in its forked `_exec2`, before `execve`. Fix shape: report from the child with a raw async-signal-safe `write(2)` to fd 2 (the phase's log by then) and stop at the first failed request, matching Portage's text and abort-the-rest; or hand the error to the parent over a pre-opened pipe if the no-printing rule is kept. Oracle: a spawn whose netlink traffic is forced to fail (test-only injection, or a seccomp profile blocking `NETLINK_ROUTE`) shows Portage's warning line and no later requests; today portuale prints nothing. Tier 3. [C]

## Tier 4 — `--solver=` real-tree correctness (pubgrub / resolvo)

33. **`--solver=resolvo` cycle linearization** — `install_order` can't order any closure with a toolchain cycle (glibc↔gcc↔perl); `solver_bridge.rs` prints raw ids. Non-functional on real targets. `docs/history/solver-backends-analysis.md`. [J]

Feed the resolved graph instead. `solver_bridge.rs`. [J]

587. **DONE 2026-10-26 (branch `fix/2026-10-25-review`, in the #575 close-out commit; found while cleaning `/var/tmp` after the plan). Filed 2026-10-26 — detached-signature verification leaked one empty temp directory per checked file.** `binpkg::gpg::verify_detached_signature` created its `portuale-sign` scratch directory with `TempDir::new(..).keep()`, so the directory outlived the call (seen as `portuale-td-portuale-sign-*` under the suite's temp root) and the `.sig` file was removed through a `let _ =`. The guard now lives for the call and removes both. Tier 4. [I] Oracle: the `portuale-td-portuale-sign-*` scratch dirs observed under the suite temp root (one per checked file, pre-fix).

588. **DONE 2026-10-27 (plan 02.589 Task 5, branch `fix/2026-10-26-575-residues`, no pmtest half). Filed 2026-10-26, residue of #587; scope corrected 2026-10-26 by plan 02.589 Task 0 — tests leak their scratch directories through `TempDir::new(..).keep()`.** The first count named seven in-file test modules; a recount (`grep -rn '\.keep()' crates --include=*.rs`, without `portage-util/src/temp_dir.rs`) finds **304 calls in 92 files**, 28 of them outside `tests/` directories (in-file `#[cfg(test)]` helpers and possibly runtime code). Every `cargo test` run leaves directories under the temp root. Fix: sort the calls into test and runtime ones; in tests, return the `TempDir` guard and keep it alive (the repo rule), as the newer tests do; a runtime call stays only where the directory must outlive the process. `portage-util/src/temp_dir.rs`'s own test of `keep` is the one legitimate test call. Plan: [`02.589-575-residues.opus.md`](02.589-575-residues.opus.md) Task 5. Tier 4. [I] Oracle: the 304-call `TempDir::keep()` recount (`grep -rn '\.keep()'`) + the guard-alive convention (plan 02.589 Task 5).

616. **DONE 2026-10-29 (branch `batch/2026-10-29-easy-six`; pmtest `dd8657c`; no portuale half) — the test pins the vdb directory's mtime to an old fixed value before fingerprinting, so `mkdir`'s timestamp is unambiguous on a coarse-mtime tmpfs.** Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-28, residue of #600 / plan 02.589 R6) — `test_host_guard.py::test_fingerprint_notices_a_vdb_mtime_change` fails in some full-suite runs.** It passes alone and as a file. It compares a `tmp_path` directory's `st_mtime_ns` before and after a `mkdir`, so a coarse-grained mtime on a busy tmpfs makes the two fingerprints equal. Fix by forcing the mtime with `os.utime` in the test. Tier 4. Oracle: 20/20 host runs (before and after) on ext4 + the `os.utime` pin (pmtest `dd8657c`).

53. **DONE 2026-09-15 — Merge order violated a hard `BDEPEND` edge in `app-containers/podman`'s L0 probe (#48 residue).** The backlog's original `:=`-slot-operator/F-A1 attribution was **wrong** (disproven in planning, `history/06.053-merge_order.opus.md` §1): the trigger was `dev-lang/go`'s own `|| ( >=go-N >=go-bootstrap-N )` BDEPEND. `merge_order.rs::build_digraph`'s `alt_suppressed` re-derives which `||` branch "wins" for scheduling-edge purposes independently of the actual resolver's choice, with no equivalent of the resolver's own `circular_self` exclusion (`b5b256f`, finding D) — so an alternative satisfied only by matching the *owning entry's own not-yet-merged graph node* trivially counted as "in graph" and beat the bootstrap branch the resolver had actually picked, adding a permanent self-edge that stalled `go` behind every dependent whose own edge to it could legitimately relax. Fixed (`a5921eb`, S2) by excluding a `||` alternative's match against the owning entry's own index unless that entry is itself installed. Fixture: `fixtures/repo/{dev-lang,dev-go,app-misc}/mo*` (`app-misc/mopodman` failing / `app-misc/mopodmanok` nearest-passing, `e05fd28`); pins: 3 `CASES` entries + a dedicated order-assertion contract test + a Rust unit test on `build_digraph` directly. Verified clean against real Portage two ways: the fixture oracle (`TEST/logs/l0-fx-20260915T152731Z`, `UNEXPLAINED 0`, byte-identical deduplicated merge order — this also fixed 2 unrelated harness gaps in `TEST/layers/l0-fixture-oracle/{stage,in-container}.sh`, `dbeea82`) and the live `app-containers/podman` L0 probe (0 portuale invariant violations, was 1). Interesting side-finding: on the isolated fixture, real does **not** hit the circular-dependency abort the original live evidence showed — it picks the bootstrap branch cleanly too, confirming that abort is a discovery-order effect of the full ~100-package graph, not inherent to this dependency shape; full parity with real's abort (a separate, larger change to finding D) stays open as a follow-up if ever wanted. Findings: `TEST/findings/l0.md` "#53 S0"-"S3". [A]
