259. **DONE 2026-09-29 (portuale `14456168`, branch `backlog/259-260-test-hygiene` fast-forwarded into `main`) — the pseudo-terminal prompt tests can hang forever.** `wait_pty_output`/`wait_pty_status` (`rust/portuale/src/pretend.rs`) poll `try_wait` against a bound (60 s default, `PORTUALE_TEST_PTY_WATCHDOG_SECS` override) and kill the child at expiry, failing with a `watchdog:` prefix carrying the child's output — the race-chase hook the incident lacked. All 11 `pty_pair`+`wait_with_output`/`wait` sites swapped. Pinned by `pty_watchdog_kills_a_hung_child_and_fails_the_test`. The root race is still unexplained and is chased from the first watchdog timeout's captured output (residue). Original entry follows. **the pseudo-terminal prompt tests can hang forever.** `ask_read_news_yes_spawns_eselect_and_continues` (`rust/portuale/src/pretend.rs`, the `pty_pair` + `wait_with_output` shape shared by the `--ask`/`--read-news` tests) waits for the child with no timeout. A `cargo test` run from the #230 worktree left the child blocked reading its pty (`wait_woken`) and the test binary waiting on it for 20 hours, until the owner spotted the idle process (PIDs 95126/49163, killed 2026-09-29). Two full `cargo test` runs the same day passed, so it is an intermittent race, cause not found (`read_prompt_line` reads one canonical line per `read`, the `eselect` stub reads no stdin). Fix: a watchdog that kills the child and fails the test after a bound, then chase the race with the watchdog's output. [I]

260. **DONE 2026-09-29 (portuale `14456168`, branch `backlog/259-260-test-hygiene` fast-forwarded into `main`) — the test suites leave top-level `/tmp` entries behind and exhaust the tmpfs inodes.** Every creator now goes through `portage_util::TempDir` (`rust/portage-util/src/temp_dir.rs`): one `portuale-td-{tag}-{pid}-{nanos}` name family, `Drop` for the unwind/success paths, and a stale sweep of dead-pid dirs at the first `TempDir::new` of each process — the only cleanup that runs after `panic = "abort"` or SIGKILL. 159 sites across 28 files converted (the `portage-profile` fixed-name sites gained unique names); product `bin_dir()` keeps its `atexit` path deliberately. Measured before: 16,393 `portage-repo-masters-test-*` + 768 `emerge_build_test_*` + ~500 more (~85k top-level entries on a 1,048,576-inode tmpfs). Residue: the pre-existing pile under the *old* names is not swept (no `portuale-td-` prefix) and wants one manual `find` sweep; the `tmp????????` 0-byte files date from 2026-09-25 and an S0 showed the current suites create none (45 -> 45). Original entry follows. **the test suites leave top-level `/tmp` entries behind and exhaust the tmpfs inodes.** Entries named `portuale*`, `emerge*`, `true_y_or_n_*`, `portage*`, `ebuild*`, `eselect*`, `mrg_*`, `resume*`, `ask_*`, `remote*`, `tmp_???????`, `tmp????????` accumulate across `cargo test` / pytest runs until `/tmp` (1,048,576 inodes) is full, which fails unrelated tests and every tool that needs a temp file. The owner cleared them with a `find /tmp -maxdepth 1 -mmin +30 … -exec rm -rf {} +` sweep. Fix: find each creator (Rust tests using `std::env::temp_dir()` with a name and no cleanup on panic; binaries' own temp dirs) and give it a guard that removes the directory on drop, or route it under the test's own temp root. [I]

261. **DONE 2026-09-30 (batch-2026-09-29 Track B / B1 S1, pmtest `f71de02`, merged `2683540`) — L3's control pair is not noise-free at `L3_JOBS=28`.** Two real-Portage builds of `sys-libs/glibc-2.43-r2` differed in the split-debug set of the `getconf` binaries: control-a had no `.debug` / `.build-id` entries for `/usr/bin/getconf` and `…/getconf/POSIX_V6_LP64_OFF64` (8 MISSING + 8 CONTENTS rows), and the `POSIX_V6_LP64_OFF64` payloads differ across all four builds. Real against real, so not a portuale finding; the candidate side tolerates the getconf payloads already. The previous run at `-j20` (`l3-20260927T080452Z`) had a clean control. Decide from more runs whether this is a `-j` race in glibc's getconf install (hardlinked variants split once by estrip) or random, then either teach the comparator this class with its evidence or pin `L3_JOBS` for glibc. The second `-j28` run, `l3-20260929T082541Z`, had a clean control (1/1/0; the `getconf` rows only as tolerated payload), so the split is intermittent. **DONE 2026-09-30 (batch-2026-09-29 Track B / B1 S1, pmtest `f71de02`, merged `2683540`) — `compare/diff.py` now classifies the class as explained (`HARDLINK_DEBUG_ID = "#261-hardlink-debug-race"`): a `.debug`/`@buildid` MISSING or CONTENTS row whose object is one of a hardlink set (same md5 on both sides' CONTENTS `obj` lines) counts as explained when each side has exactly one `.debug` for the set, whatever its name; everything else stays hard. TDD `compare/test-diff-tolerance.py` 4/4 (the race explained + three near-misses stay hard). Offline re-diff of the saved `logs/l3-20260930T141623Z` control pair: **hard 17 / explained 17 / UNEXPLAINED 0** (was 17/1/16), all 16 getconf rows named `(#261-hardlink-debug-race)`, no other row changed class. Live `l3-smoke` `l3-20261001T010343Z` rc 0, 0 unexplained (the race did not trigger that run). Blanket-ignoring `.debug`/`@buildid` rejected so #38 stays under test.** Resolution filed 2026-09-30 (owner decision; not started).** Cause, from real source: `estrip` handles ELF files in parallel jobs (`bin/estrip:515-674`, `__multijob_*`, job count from `MAKEOPTS`), hardlinks to one inode serialise on a per-inode lockfile (`:216-229`), and `save_elf_debug` names the `.debug` after the basename of whichever job takes the lock *first* (`dst_basename=${src_basename}.debug`, `:125-158`); later links reuse that name through `readlink`. So in a hardlink set (glibc's `getconf` / `POSIX_V6_*` family) the name that carries the `.debug` / `@buildid` is decided by the scheduler. That is real non-determinism, not a portuale finding. It showed up again between portage and portuale in the `l3-smoke` cell of `l3-20260930T205549Z` (`-j28`, disk): 8 MISSING + 8 CONTENTS rows, `POSIX_V6_LP64_OFF64.debug` on the portage side against `getconf.debug` on the portuale side, the same pairs for `@buildid`. The clean re-run `l3-20260930T211117Z` had 0. Fix: teach `compare/diff.py` this class. A `.debug` / `@buildid` MISSING or CONTENTS row whose object is one of a hardlink set (the same md5 on both sides' CONTENTS `obj` lines for the linked paths) counts as explained when each side has exactly one `.debug` for the set, whatever its name. Everything else stays hard. Blanket-ignoring `.debug` / `@buildid` was considered and rejected: `splitdebug` stays on in the L3 determinism block so #38 (portuale's split-debug output and build-ids equal to the oracle's) stays under test. Build-ids are content hashes, not per-build unique, and differing bytes already land in the tolerated payload bucket. Verify with a `-j28` `l3-core` run with `L3_CONTROL=1` (the control pair clean) and a smoke run, plus a synthetic `diff.py` unit case. [I]

## Tier 7 — GLEP compliance gaps (filed 2026-09-15)

Surfaced by an audit of portuale against the 12 GLEPs flagged as technical
(78, 82, 74, 79, 81, 53, 59, 61, 64, 68, 83, 84), cross-referenced against
both portuale's Rust source and the vendored real Portage it's validated
against. Full per-GLEP writeup, including the ten that are solidly covered
or correctly out of scope (74/61/79/81/68/83/84 delegate to `gemato`/
repoman/pkgcheck/social process, none of which portuale implements or
needs to), is in `docs/glep-compliance-review.md`. Two genuine, narrow gaps:

55. **DONE 2026-09-15 — honour `layout.conf` `cache-formats` (GLEP 82 gap).** `RepoConfig` now carries the resolved `cache_formats` (lowercased + split; empty -> auto-detect `md5-dict` then `pms` from directory presence, real `parse_layout` `config.py:1564-1577`), and `pregen_md5_cache_enabled` is "the first **known** format is `md5-dict`" (real `iter_pregenerated_caches` + `get_pregenerated_cache` keep only the first yield). `has_usable_md5_cache` is that predicate plus `FEATURES=metadata-transfer` absent (real `porttree.py:322`), resolved through the same config stack the CLI uses; `repo_aux_metadata` skips the md5-cache rung entirely when false and goes straight to the depcachedir/depend provider (mrg-director's `Md5Cache::category` follows the same flag, listing `*.ebuild`). `--regen` follows the writer half (S0 cell h): real `egencache --update` writes `metadata/cache` for `pms` and both dirs for `pms md5-dict`, so `regen_writes_md5_cache` gates a repo to a message + exit 1 rather than writing the wrong directory. Pins: Rust units (cells a-f, g, L3) + Rust-only `tests/test_portuale.py` end-to-end for cells c/e (valid masking entry ignored under `pms`, trusted under `md5-dict pms`, depcache written), g (env `FEATURES=metadata-transfer`) and the L3 skip. **Narrowing (L1): no `pms` reader** — a `pms`-first repo falls to the depcachedir/depend phase, which yields the same metadata real reads from a valid `pms` cache (slower, not different). Evidence: `TEST/findings/l2.md` "## #55 S0"; plan `docs/history/07.55-layout_conf_cache_formats.opus.md`; L0 after the change byte-identical to the pre-change baseline (`TEST/logs/l0-20260915T180851Z`, clean 100 / parity 0.833). [C]
56. **DONE 2026-09-15 — gpkg outer-container tar walk trusts only regular-file members (GLEP 78 hardening).** S0's crafted-archive matrix (`scripts/gpkg_crafted_members.py`; `TEST/findings/l2.md` "## #56 S0") established what real actually does: its in-archive `tarfile.extractfile` never leaves the container (a symlinked `Manifest` -> archive-internal `KeyError`; every link/special member fails the Manifest size check or the structure check first), while portuale's `tar -xf` + filesystem walk followed a symlinked `Manifest`/`metadata.tar.zst`/prefix (cell c listed the host `/etc`, 266 dirents), blocked on a FIFO member and read a char-device member unboundedly. S1 (`de72588`) adds `outer_regular_member`/`outer_prefix_dir` (`symlink_metadata`, no follow) and the shared `walk_outer_members` behind `read_gpkg_metadata`/`extract_gpkg_member`; `verify_gpkg_manifest` uses the same helpers and checks every trusted member before reading the Manifest; a non-regular `gpkg-1`/`Manifest`/`metadata.tar*`/`image.tar*`/`.sig` is an error naming the member and its type at every site (never skipped for a later regular one), and the three outer `tar -xf` calls gained `--no-same-owner` (N3). Pins: the typed `build_gpkg_entries` builder + 7 Rust tests (S0 cells a, b, c, d, f, h across all three sites; cell e root-only), the committed fixture still reads and verifies. L2 porttest `TEST/logs/l2-20260915T195259Z` hard=0, 0 unexplained both directions. **Superseded by #58 S5 (`c33137b`, 2026-09-16):** `walk_outer_members`, `outer_regular_member` and `outer_prefix_dir` no longer exist — the outer container is read in process with the `tar` crate and the trusted-name check is a tar-header type check (`read_outer_members`); the seven crafted-cell tests keep their assertions, re-pointed at the new path. Detail: [`history/07.56-gpkg_outer_container.opus.md`](history/07.56-gpkg_outer_container.opus.md). [K]
58. **DONE 2026-09-16 — no gpkg member (outer container, inner `metadata.tar`, inner `image.tar`) can make portuale read or write a host path (Tier 7 #58; supersedes #56's post-extraction helpers).** K0-K7 answered by the owner 2026-09-16: the inner metadata tar is read **in process** with the `tar` crate (`default-features = false`: zero new transitive crates, no C linkage), real `tarfile.extractfile` semantics ported exactly, and the owner widened the slice to the inner `image.tar` (K2) and the outer container (K7). S0 (`5e6fcf7`) built the crafted inner/image cell matrix and the crate spike (findings `TEST/findings/l2.md` "## #58 S0"); S1 (`1507808`) added the reader (regular/unknown -> bytes, symlink -> `dirname/linkname` normalized over the whole archive last-match, hardlink -> earlier members only, depth cap, 64 MiB/entry bomb guards, last-wins + duplicate flag); S2 (`28f83cf`) wired `read_gpkg_metadata` to it and regenerated the committed fixture without the inner `metadata/` directory member real's writer never emits and its reader rejects (real now reads it 13/13); S3 (`ebe1286`) moved `extract_gpkg_member(metadata)` off `tar -xpf` onto resolved regular-file writes (`create_new`, header mode masked to `0o7777` minus setuid/setgid), refusing duplicates (K5) and nested keys (K4); S4 (`f96804c`) added real `tar_safe_extract`'s rule set as an in-process pre-scan of the scratch `image.tar` before GNU tar unpacks (owner-approved extra rule: a member whose path traverses an earlier symlink member, S0 cell i18), plus the S0 i19 regression pin (symlink, hardlink, sparse, xattr); S5 (`c33137b`) moved the **outer container** in process too (`read_outer_members` header checks: one level deep, one prefix, no duplicates, trusted names regular), `verify_gpkg_manifest` streams member digests through the new `portage_fetch::verify_digests_reader`, and #56's `walk_outer_members`/`outer_regular_member`/`outer_prefix_dir` plus every outer `tar -xf --no-same-owner` are deleted — `binpkg.rs` now has one tar mechanism, GNU tar only unpacks the image. Parity: every S0 cell real accepts reads identically and every cell real raises on now errors (S0 verdict table); documented differences (all stricter, none reachable from real's writer): an in-archive metadata symlink is written as a **regular file** holding the target's bytes (K2), nested keys error on merge (K4), and a `..` component errors on read (K5). Tests: 20+ new Rust units/end-to-end crafted-archive tests plus the re-pointed #56 seven-cell suite (cell c's needle moved to the structure error: the crafted prefix member has zero slashes, which real's own `f.count("/") != 1` rule refuses before any type check); full pytest 1693 passed; L2 porttest both directions rc 0 with 0 unexplained (`TEST/logs/l2-20260916T004658Z` S4, `TEST/logs/l2-20260916T063732Z` S5); read-path timing on a synthetic 200 MB gpkg 26-34 ms with no scratch copy. **Deferred residue filed as #60/#61.** Note: this Tier 7 entry shares its number with the pre-existing Tier 2 #58 (slot-conflict exit code); renumbering not done here (flagged to the owner per the plan). Detail: [`07.058-check_metadata_member_types.md`](07.058-check_metadata_member_types.md). [K]

60. **DONE 2026-09-16 — gpkg populate-path read errors: real's invalid class is skipped, the uncaught class still aborts (Tier 7, #58 S0 residue).** `BinpkgError { Invalid, Fatal }` (`bcfae67`, S1) now tags every error site of the populate-reachable readers (`read_gpkg_metadata`/`read_xpak_metadata` + helpers; `Display` + `From<BinpkgError> for String` keep the merge path byte-identical) exactly where real's `bintree._populate_local` catch (`bintree.py:1185-1199`) splits: `PortagePackageException`/`SignatureException` -> Invalid (outer structure/marker/duplicates, missing or unsupported metadata member, metadata caps, decompressor missing/failed, unsafe/outside-`metadata/` inner names), everything real does not catch -> Fatal (inner-tar parse/read errors, unresolved links, directory/FIFO/device members, link cycles, IO), and **every `.xpak` read error Fatal** (real's `tbz2.scan()` -> None -> `AttributeError` on `None.get`). `populate_local_pkgdir` (`fb7e509`, S2) prints real's own `!!! Invalid binary package: '<path>', <reason>` (the reader's `'<path>: '` prefix stripped for real's one-path shape) and continues, while a Fatal file still returns Err -> the CLI's existing `emerge: scanning <pkgdir>: <msg>` rc 1. Live on the S0 setup: cell i8's crafted archive no longer hides `dev-libs/binaryonlypkg` (rc 0 with the warning; rc 1 before), cell i1 still aborts. Pins: `binpkg_error_classes_mirror_reals_caller` + the re-classed crafted-cell tests + `populate_local_pkgdir_skips_invalid_files_and_keeps_the_pool` / `..._aborts_on_fatal_files` (S1/S2), and the hermetic contract test `test_pkgdir_scan_skips_real_invalid_class_and_aborts_the_rest` (`d7ce18d`, S3). S0 (`c0eda75`) holds real's source classes, the live `--mode real` probe and the pool-poisoning reproduction. Plan: [`history/07.60-gpkg-populate-read-errors.opus.md`](history/07.60-gpkg-populate-read-errors.opus.md). [K]

61. **DONE 2026-09-16 — `musl/smoke_test.sh` restored: builder, source portability, MSRV guard, and the stale assertion layer (Tier 7 infra).** The original diagnosis was partial; the bed had three walls, all reproduced on the real tree (`TEST/findings/musl.md` S0 matrix) and all fixed on `backlog/61-musl`. **(1)** alpine:3.22.1's rustc 1.87 vs the workspace's 1.95 floor (`gentoo-interner`/`portage-atom*`/`portage-solver`; pubgrub 1.92, smol_str 1.89 behind them) aborts cargo per build graph. **(2)** the `x86_64-alpine-linux-musl` target is dynamic-musl by default, so `+crt-static`'s `-Wl,-Bstatic ... -Wl,-Bdynamic` wrapper left gcc's appended trailing `-lc` dynamic while `-static` was in force (`attempted static link of dynamic object .../libc.so`). **S1** (`2b2f19d`) moves the base to `alpine:3.24.1` (rustc 1.96.1, first stable release over the floor; Alpine's own rust/cargo apks kept, no rustup) and adds a trailing `-C link-arg=-Wl,-Bstatic` (lands after rustc's `-Bdynamic`, before gcc's libraries); the same tree's five binaries are `INTERP=0 NEEDED=0`. **(3)** `pretend.rs::apply_scheduling_policy` built a glibc-shaped `libc::sched_param` literal, E0063 on musl; **S2** (`c550214`) zero-inits and assigns `sched_priority`, matching CPython's `os.sched_param` conversion behind real `actions.py:3379`. **S3** (`9616339`) declares `rust-version = "1.95"` workspace-wide (all 15 members inherit) so an old builder fails with Cargo's own message (verified on 3.22.1). **S4** (`df403fd`) rewrote the script's pilot-era exact strings as structural assertions (rc + exact cpv order + markers; byte-exact output stays the contract suite's job), replaced the obsolete `--jobs` and `ebuild`-stub checks (`--nobindeps` refusal, `ebuild --help` + bad-phase refusal) and routed every command through a `capture` helper so `set -e` no longer aborts at the first expected-nonzero rc (`blockerpkg`). **S5** (`23d51fc`) silences the musl-only `libc::time_t` deprecation. S0 (`7052b35`) holds the wall matrix, the link-line root cause and the base/toolchain table. Verification: `bash musl/smoke_test.sh` **PASS, 23/23 checks, builder warning-free**. Plan: [`history/07.61-musl-smoke-toolchain-drift.opus.md`](history/07.61-musl-smoke-toolchain-drift.opus.md). [K]


258. **DONE 2026-09-29 (portuale `121be612` + review fixes `f500002c`, merge `042a8daf`; pmtest `ea45669`, merge `6becb5a`; muse-spark-ro round 1 REQUEST_CHANGES, round 2 APPROVE; L1 merge gate and `l32` 0 unexplained; filed 2026-09-29, `l3-20260928T195717Z` candidate) — GLEP 42 news: `Display-If-Profile` / `Display-If-Keyword` were always satisfied, and the state files lacked real's group bits.** The #196/#231 notices made every real merge run the news update, so the L3 candidate showed `news-gentoo.{unread,skip}` at `644 0:0`, 1158 bytes (34 items) against real's `664 0:250`, 513 bytes (15 items). ## Tier 8 — resolver performance (filed 2026-09-20)

Profile of `portuale emerge -puD --getbinpkg net-libs/rest` on this host
(2088 installed packages; real Portage 3.0.82.2) at `ce68f77`: portuale
7.71–7.92 s wall / 5.79–6.00 s user / 1.83–1.92 s sys vs real
5.70–5.89 s / 4.97–5.00 s / 0.19–0.24 s. The remaining cost is not the
graph walk but per-visit recomputation and vdb re-scanning: 1.20 M
`statx` (906,881 of them from `installed_candidates` scans), 6.05 M
`apply_updates_to_cp`, 71,572 `effective_use_flags` calls each cloning
its memoised set. Every entry is **output-preserving** — byte-identical
output over the existing contract suite + harvested corpus is the guard
— and re-measures the same workload; `performances-tuning.md` carries
the numbers. Plan for #102–#104 + #108:
[`08.102-108-perf-memoisation.md`](08.102-108-perf-memoisation.md).
Tag [P] = performance; detail lives in `performances-tuning.md`, not a
`scope-backlog.md` section.

102. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/102-108`, S1 `bf52b1a`) — per-cp `installed_candidates` cache + inverted move map.** 7.71–7.92 s → 5.45–5.66 s wall, `statx` 1,202,010 → 297,382, byte-identical output; merge-path safety gate green (L1 glibc+bash re-merge, 0 unexplained). Original entry follows. **`installed_candidates` re-scans the vdb on every call (12,684 calls → 906,881 dir entries stat'ed, ~110 k `SLOT` reads, 6.05 M `apply_updates_to_cp`).** `portage-repo/src/lib.rs::installed_candidates` (`:5595`) does `read_dir_entries(var/db/pkg/<cat>)` + `e.path().is_dir()` (one `statx` per entry) + `SLOT` reads, and `installed_cp_sources` (`:760`) replays every `profiles/updates/` move through `apply_updates_to_cp` (`:749`) on every call — the whole ~1.9 s sys plus ~0.5–1.0 s user (`binary_deps_changed` 10.7 % + direct `installed_candidates` 4.5 %). Fix: cache per `(root, cat, pkg)` validated by the scanned category dirs' mtimes (every portuale vdb mutation replaces or removes the package dir: `ebuild_merge::write_vdb_entry_from_dir` `ebuild_merge.rs:2387-2390`), invert `installed_cp_sources` to a one-time reverse map, and use `DirEntry::file_type()` instead of `path().is_dir()`. The merge path reads it (`bind_slot_operator`, `inject_libc_dep`), so the glibc+bash safety gate applies. Plan S1. [P]
103. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/102-108`, S2 `9be30f8`) — `Rc<HashSet>` out of `effective_use_flags`.** 4.78–4.85 s → 4.51–4.55 s wall (same live env), byte-identical output. Original entry follows. **`effective_use_flags` clones its memoised `HashSet` on every call.** 71,572 calls / 70,237 hits × ~200 flags ≈ 14 M `String` clones (`portage-repo/src/lib.rs:3178`: `hit.as_ref().clone()`); `HashSet<String>::clone` is 2.46 % flat plus a large malloc/memmove/SipHash share. Fix: return `Rc<HashSet<String>>` (the shape `read_md5_cache`/`list_candidates` already use with `Arc`), auditing the ~31 call sites (~15 production). Plan S2. [P]
104. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/102-108`, S3 `ef19952`) — `resolved_use_mask_or_force` memo.** 4.51–4.55 s → 4.13–4.19 s wall, byte-identical output. Original entry follows. **`resolved_use_mask_or_force` is recomputed per candidate (29,774 calls → 826,643 `config_entries_matching`).** `portage-repo/src/lib.rs:3602` walks every `use_mask_force_levels` entry and rebuilds the set each time (twice per `forced_or_masked_flags_unfiltered`, 13,433 calls); 23 % of the run sits under `binpkg_respect_use_ok`, 9.9 % in this function. Fix: memoise on `(which, stable, candidate_str, config fingerprint)` — `use_mask_force_levels` is fixed at config resolution (`portage-profile/src/lib.rs:3373`) and only `autounmask_use` mutates during backtracking (not read here). Plan S3. [P]
105. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `feat/parallel`) — the USE-context digest is frozen at config resolution.** `resolve_config` now computes the immutable part once into `Config::use_context_base` (`portage-profile::use_context_base_fingerprint`, excluding `autounmask_use`); the memo keys hash that `u64` plus the live `autounmask_use`, the one field the `'backtrack` loop mutates (the mutation creates a clone, which correctly inherits the base). Hand-built configs (`Config::default()`, tests) have no base and fall back to the full content hash, so an in-place edit of any context field is still seen. Interleaved, same tree: 3.00–3.16 s → **2.44–2.50 s** wall / 2.46–2.56 → **1.89–1.97 s** user (second shape `sys-devel/gcc`: 3.00–3.08 → 2.43–2.47); `use_context_fingerprint` and `metadata_key_accepted` both leave the profile's top list; byte-identical over the full contract suite + corpus. The plan's "bump on mutation" is realised as freeze-at-resolution + per-call `autounmask_use` hashing. Original entry follows. **Re-ranked 2026-09-21 (#109/#110 S6): still open, and now the #1 named single-threaded cost.** `use_context_fingerprint` is **23.77 % with children** (0.38 % self; closure 9.29 + `hash_sampled` 7.23) on the post-batch profile — `binpkg_respect_use_ok`'s 28.82 % is largely this function plus the already-memoised USE machinery. Do this next. **`use_context_fingerprint` re-hashes ~20 config fields on every `effective_use_flags` call (71,572 calls, ~8 % of the run: closure 3.4 + `hash_sampled` 2.6 + 2.0).** `portage-repo/src/lib.rs:3239`; cache the immutable part per config generation (bump it on the `'backtrack` loop's `autounmask_use` mutation) instead of re-hashing the big `HashSet`s for every EUF key. Plan §5. [P]
106. **Re-ranked 2026-09-21 (#109/#110 S6): effectively closed by #102–#108.** `candidate_positions` is **0.31 % with children** on the post-batch profile (the `cp_bucket_index` cache holds). Deprioritise; do not fold into #104. **`candidate_positions` allocates, extends and sorts per call (826,643 calls, 99.99 % `cp_bucket_index` hits).** `portage-repo/src/lib.rs:2939` does `format!("{category}/{package}")` + `Vec` clone + `extend_from_slice(other)` + `sort_unstable` on every lookup while the index itself is already cached. Fix: key `by_cp` by `(String, String)` (or a stack `cat/pkg` buffer) and/or return a borrowed iterator. Fold into #104 if cheap. Plan §5. [P]
107. **DONE 2026-09-29 (batch-2026-09-28_236_107 Slice D: portuale `9cc71b16` + pmtest `5a5210e`; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-29-107-reprobe/`).** After #236 the reference workload still restarted once and warned: installed mesa's dynamic-deps pair on `libdisplay-info` carries use-deps, and the collapse counted a use-dep atom as matching only its own pick. `minimize_children` now checks an installed package's use-deps against its vdb USE/IUSE (real `findAtomForPackage(pkg, modified_use=_pkg_use_enabled(pkg))`): real's rows, no warning, `restarts: 0` on the live host, pinned hermetically by `test_107_use_dep_dynamic_deps_pair_settles_in_one_silent_pass`. *Earlier history:* **PARKED 2026-09-21 after an S0 diagnosis and a prototype; not a local fix.** **Re-probe 2026-09-27 (batch-2026-09-27 P-R4 step 1, new host, release `main` @ `8b7890cd`, evidence `docs/evidence/2026-09-27-107-reprobe/`): the restart is still there, and on this host it is visible in the output.** Portuale's `--json` reports 1 restart by default and 0 with `--backtrack=0`; real prints `backtrack: 0/20`. Both merge the same set (`dev-libs/md4c-0.6.0`, `net-libs/rest-0.10.2`) and both withhold `media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0` (weston-16.0.0's `<media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0:=` pin), but real prints **no** skipped-update warning — its selection sees the installed consumer's atom and never tries 0.4.0 — while portuale's second pass prints `WARNING: One or more updates/rebuilds have been skipped due to a dependency conflict:` plus the conflict block. So the "two-pass output is byte-identical to real" claim below held on the old host only. The fix is still the complete-graph parent-atom model (#25); stays PARKED (owner B5 of batch-2026-09-27: diagnosis only). §A triage 2026-09-27: see scope-backlog.md §A triage. Separately, real takes `net-libs/rest` as a binhost binary where portuale builds it: filed as #192. Diagnosed on the reference workload: the extra pass is the `Config@23328` feedback from `reverse_dependency_constraints` -- pass 1 picks `media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0`, the post-pass scan finds installed `dev-libs/weston-16.0.0`'s recorded `<media-libs/libdisplay-info-0.4.0:=`, and pass 2 settles by withholding the update (the skip warning). Real does it in one pass because its complete graph carries the reachable installed consumer as a nomerge node, so the consumer's atom is among the parent atoms when `_select_pkg_highest_available` chooses the candidate. Cost of the extra pass: `--backtrack=0` 2.03-2.06 s vs default 2.44-2.53 s (~0.4 s wall/user, ~16 %), and the two-pass output is byte-identical to real. **Prototype (reverted):** a selection-time reverse-pin map built once per resolve from reachable consumers' runtime atoms, consulted at `resolve_pretend` with a fallback when the pins leave no candidate, removed the restart (`restarts` 0) but **changed output**: the reachable-consumer set carries ~100 cps' worth of recorded atoms on this host (including tight pins like `~dev-util/spirv-tools-1.4.357.0`, `~llvm-core/llvm-21.1.8`), and applying them wholesale dropped the `app-crypt/gcr` update and the withheld-update warning. The post-pass scan only fires on pins the *selected candidate breaks*, only when holdable, and skips consumers this pass replaces/removes; reproducing that mid-walk is the complete-graph parent-atom model (#25's domain), not a selection tweak. Revisit only if/when that model lands; the prize does not justify the resolver-selection risk today. **Re-ranked 2026-09-21 (#109/#110 S6, then #105): unchanged and now clearly first-order.** `run_pass` is 81.20 % with children on the post-#105 profile (85.44 % before it) and the second pass is still there — every memoisation slice leaves its share higher and removes the I/O around it. Still not to be won by skipping the pass. **The backtracking loop runs a second full `run_pass` where real reports `backtrack: 0/20` (≈30 % of the run).** On the §1 workload portuale's feedback loop restarts (`--backtrack=0` cuts user 5.87 → 4.11 s at baseline, 2.9–3.1 → 2.2 s after #102–#104+#108) while real resolves in one pass; the trigger is the libdisplay-info blocker the #68 blocker family already owns. This is an **algorithmic/parity** item, not memoisation: do not win it by skipping the pass (that silently changes which run is reported). Diagnose the feedback trigger against real's `_backtrack_depgraph` and decide with the owner. Plan §5 (rule in §4). [P]

108. **DONE 2026-09-20 (branch `backlog/102-108`, S4 `9c40b32`) — `local_binpkg_index` memo (`Arc<BinaryIndex>`).** 4.13–4.19 s → 3.98–3.99 s wall, byte-identical output. Original entry follows. **`local_binpkg_index` rebuilds the whole binary index per call (3,056 calls, 2.45 % of the run).** `portage-repo/src/lib.rs:2272` clones `config.scanned_binpkgs` (or re-reads `$PKGDIR/Packages`) and rebuilds the `by_cp` map on every call from 3 sites. Fix: thread-local memo keyed by `(pkgdir, scanned identity, quickpkg root)` — the `cp_bucket_index` `(ptr, len)` + first/last fingerprint pattern — returning `Arc<BinaryIndex>`; the `None` arm should use the existing `cached_binary_index`. All inputs are set once before resolution, so the key is stable within a run. Plan S4. [P]

109. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `feat/parallel`, S1–S4 `bcc69fd`/`c909bfa`/`6f158d7`/`5759a29`) — the vdb consolidated `metadata` snapshot is read like real `_aux_get`, with a per-instance mtime-keyed aux memo.** Interleaved on the reference workload (same 27-package plan): 3.93–3.98 s wall / 2.86–2.90 s user / 1.07–1.08 s sys / 174 MB RSS → **3.07–3.30 s / 2.51–2.63 s / 0.56–0.67 s / ~182 MB** (best-of 3.07 vs 3.93). `strace`: `openat` 160,351 (40,651 ENOENT) → 33,685 (105), `read` 202,281 → 30,041, `close` 119,772 → 33,652; `statx` 294,450 → 339,804 — the per-call validity stat is kept by design (real stats too) plus `vdb_pkg_dir`'s `is_dir()`, filed as #112. S1 normalises `read_vdb_string` like real (`" ".join(myd.split())`, the one output-moving slice, byte-identical in the end); S2 moves the field set/version to `portage-repo`; S3 adds `vdb_aux_get` (S3 alone was a small regression — per-call snapshot reads; S4's memo is the win); S4 the memo. Full contract suite byte-identical (1739 passed, 3 pre-existing movepkg fixture-state failures, corpus drift list unchanged); merge-path gate green at S2/S3/S4 (L1 glibc+bash re-merge, 0 unexplained). pmtest: `d817882` (S1 fixture + pin), `3587c41` (S3 stale-snapshot fixture + pin). Original entry follows. **Read the vdb consolidated `metadata` snapshot like real `_aux_get` does (plus a per-instance mtime-keyed aux memo).** After #102–#108 the residual cost is the vdb: `strace -c` on §1's workload shows 160,351 `openat` (**40,651 ENOENT**) + 202,281 `read`, of which ~88 k successful opens + ~40.5 k failed probes are per-key `/var/db/pkg/CAT/PF/{USE,RDEPEND,repository,SLOT,BDEPEND,DEPEND,IUSE,IDEPEND,PDEPEND}` reads from `read_vdb_string`/`read_vdb_flag_set` (top of the successful-open distribution; the ENOENT table is the same shape). Portuale already *writes* the consolidated file (`ebuild_merge::write_consolidated_metadata_file`, byte-identical to real's 23-field `_METADATA_FILE_FIELDS`) but never reads it. Real `_read_metadata_file`/`_aux_get` (`vartree.py:115-187`, `:975-1053`) accept it when `#format=1` and `#dir_mtime == st_mtime_ns`, serve any in-set key with no `open()`, normalise non-`CONTENTS`/`NEEDED.*` values with `" ".join(myd.split())`, and memoise per `(cpv, dir mtime)` in `_aux_cache["packages"]`, filling all 23 fields on a miss. Slices: S1 normalise `read_vdb_string` like real (prerequisite, the one output-moving change); S2 move `METADATA_FILE_FIELDS` + `METADATA_FILE_FORMAT_VERSION` + `in_metadata_file` down into `portage-repo` (one definition, writer imports it); S3 add `vdb_aux_get` (stat once, validate the snapshot, serve in-set keys from it, fall back to the per-key read) behind both helpers; S4 thread-local aux memo keyed on the package dir's `st_mtime_ns`, keeping the per-call `statx` (the validity signal). Safe by construction for added fields (a new per-field file bumps the dir mtime); the residual in-place-rewrite hole is real's own documented one. Expected sys ~1.0 s → ~0.2–0.5 s; merge-path safety gate applies to S2/S4. Plan: [`08.109-110-vdb-metadata-snapshot.md`](08.109-110-vdb-metadata-snapshot.md). [P]

110. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `feat/parallel`, S5 `d034557`, review fix `54e265f`) — `metadata_key_accepted` memoised.** S0's re-profile put it at **23.30 % with children** on the post-#102–#108 binary (up from the plan's pre-batch 21.7 %, so the gate was a go); after the memo it is 13.13 %. Interleaved, same plan: user 2.96–2.99 s → 2.47–2.55 s. Keyed on `(MetadataKey, use_context_fingerprint(config) + candidate.iuse/keywords, candidate_str, value_str, accept_lists_fingerprint)` — two deliberate additions to the plan's literal key: the accept-list fingerprint (`use_context_fingerprint` does not hash the accept lists, and the crate's own masking units build configs differing only in `accept_properties`), and the candidate's own `IUSE`/`KEYWORDS` (a review finding: `candidate_str` does not encode them, so a `Packages`-index binary and the ebuild for the same CPV collided on a conditional `PROPERTIES`/`RESTRICT`; regression test added). Empty-value early return stays before the lookup. Full suite byte-identical, corpus drift list unchanged. Original entry follows. **Memoise `metadata_key_accepted` (21.7 % of the run with children pre-#102–#108; S0 re-profiles before this is written).** 13 call sites, pure in its eight arguments; its cost is `use_flags_if_conditional` (→ already-memoised `effective_use_flags`), `resolve_accept_tokens`, `all_mentioned_tokens`, `resolve_acceptable_tokens` and `use_reduce_flat`. The two `&[…]` accept arguments are never arbitrary — every site passes `(config.accept_properties, config.package_properties)` or `(config.accept_restrict, config.package_accept_restrict)` — so add an explicit `enum MetadataKey { Properties, Restrict }` parameter and key the memo on `(MetadataKey, use_context_fingerprint(config), candidate_str, value_str)`, the `RUMF_CACHE` shape. Keep the empty-value early return before the memo lookup. Guard: byte-identical output over the contract suite + corpus. **Gated**: if S0.3's re-profile shows the share collapsed with #102–#108, record that and stop (`performances-tuning.md`'s "honest non-result" precedent). Plan S5. [P]

112. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `feat/parallel`) — one `statx` per `vdb_aux_get`, not two.** `vdb_pkg_dir_meta` returns the `Metadata` the path resolution already paid (`Some` only for a real directory, following symlinks exactly like the `is_dir()` it replaced) alongside the resolved `PathBuf`; `vdb_aux_get` consumes it for the validity/`st_mtime_ns` check, and `vdb_pkg_dir` stays a thin wrapper for the other two callers (`CONTENTS`, `environment.bz2`). [P]

113. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `backlog/113-116-vdb-seam`) — `shuffle_seed` reads `PORTUALE_SHUFFLE_DIRS` once per process.** The per-directory-read `std::env::var` + `String` allocation is now a single `OnceLock<Option<u64>>` parse at first use (the `sandbox_binary` shape). Because the freeze makes a mid-process `set_var` inert by construction, the old in-process env-toggling unit test is replaced: the unit test pins the frozen value plus the sorted default, and `rust/portage-util/tests/shuffle_seed_freeze.rs` (its own test binary) pins the freeze and that the seeded Fisher-Yates still executes. Output byte-identical over the full contract suite + corpus; no pmtest pin moved. Original entry follows. **`shuffle_seed` reads `PORTUALE_SHUFFLE_DIRS` on every `read_dir_entries` (42,524/run) and allocates a `String` each time.** `portage-util`'s `shuffle_seed` is called per directory read; the env var is test/CI-only and fixed for the process, so a `OnceLock` read at first use (the established env-free pattern) removes the allocation and the `set_var` race surface `cleanroom/parallelism.md` §1.6 names. Trivial, but it is real work in the measured path. [P]

114. **DONE 2026-09-21 (branch `feat/parallel`) — `is_visible` memoised.** A temporary counter measured ~40 k calls against **1,503 distinct inputs** (96 % repeat) on the reference workload, each re-formatting the candidate string and re-deriving the mask/license/keyword/property/restrict verdicts. It memoises per `(visibility fingerprint, candidate identity + full metadata)` in a thread-local map; the config side is a second frozen base (`portage-profile::is_visible_base_fingerprint`: `package.mask`/`.unmask`, the LICENSE/PROPERTIES/RESTRICT accept lists, `package.accept_keywords` in full, `license_groups`) plus the live `autounmask_use`, with a live-digest fallback for hand-built configs. Interleaved, same tree: 2.50–2.64 s → **2.08–2.09 s** wall / 1.91–1.98 → 1.47–1.52 s user (second shape `sys-devel/gcc`: 2.44–2.52 → 2.03–2.07); byte-identical over the full contract suite + corpus. Original entry follows. **`is_visible` is itself memoisable per `(candidate, config)` (13.12 % with children after #117; was 19.63 % after #110, 11.81 % after #105).** Now the top named function. Its children are `metadata_key_accepted` (memoised), `license_accepted`, `keywords_accepted` and the mask checks; each is memoised or cheap individually, but the composite still re-derives the candidate string and re-hashes the config per call. A `(candidate_str, config generation)` memo returning the bool (or a `MaskReason` for the masking-reason consumers) is strictly bigger than #110's; do it only if a re-profile shows the residual worth it. 118. **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-21 (honest non-result) — sharing the binary-candidate pools.** The profile put `list_remote_binary_candidates` at 13.79 % with children (7.92 % `binary_candidates_from_index` materialisation, 4.44 % one-time remote `Packages` parse). Implemented the full plan — thread-local `Rc<Vec<Candidate>>` memos per `(index ptr, len, first/last CPV, cp, remote)` plus the remote shadowing memo, borrowed filtering at the two hot call sites, pools cleared at each `run_pass` to bound retention — and measured it: **2.11–2.13 s → 2.07–2.10 s median over 8 alternating pairs (~0.03 s, inside the run-to-run spread) at +16 MB RSS** (183 → 199 MB); process-lifetime retention reached ~0.06–0.1 s in one batch but +22 MB with no reliable repeat, and the profile still showed `binary_candidates_from_index` at 3.18 % plus per-call key overhead (`cached_binary_index` 5.33 %). The 7.92 % is therefore the *first* materialisation per `(cp, visit)`, not redundant repeats — the pools are queried a couple of times at most per cp, so a memo cannot amortise them; only cheaper construction could (micro-opts like hoisting the per-entry `format!("{category}/")` prefix are all that remain, well under the noise floor). Reverted; recorded so it is not re-attempted. The next named costs are `repo_aux_metadata` (13.06 %), `binpkg_respect_use_ok`'s memo residual (12.48 %), `binary_deps_changed` (10.58 %) and the `statx` pair (#112). [P]

Now: `Option<String>` return (`None` = unchanged, so `read_md5_cache` skips its insert and `installed_dep_string` keeps the raw string — no output allocation when no `profiles/updates/` command matches), the output buffer built only once a token actually changes with untouched runs copied verbatim (whitespace, `||`/`(`/`)`, `use?` byte-identical), and `update_move_targets` as a `category -> package names` map with borrowed lookups. Profile: the function's subtree 7.50 % → 4.38 %, `repo_aux_metadata` 14.42 % → 10.08 %; but the honest wall effect is small — paired 15-run delta **+0.017 s mean / +0.020 s median (9/15 wins)**, user neutral. Residual: `parse_atom`'s memo-hit struct clone per token; a whole-string literal prefilter would need a `regex` dependency waiver portage-repo does not have (not taken). [P]

128. **DONE 2026-09-21 (batch `2026-09-21.md` §3 H3) — `portage-fetch`'s stub-script tests no longer flake with `ETXTBSY` under parallel load.** Reproduced 7/30 `cargo test --release -p portage-fetch --lib` runs on unmodified source. `tempdir()`'s name collision was real but was not the mechanism: failures persisted with the counter added, on unique paths. `strace -f -tt` capture: thread A holds `wget-p.sh` open `O_WRONLY` from t0 to t2; thread B's `Command` fork (`clone3(CLONE_VM|CLONE_VFORK)`) lands between them at t1; `fork` shares the file description, so A's close does not drop the inode's write count — B's child pins it until its own exec drops the inherited `O_CLOEXEC` fd — and A's child then `execve`s the same script first: `Text file busy`. Fix: `tempdir()` gains a process-wide `AtomicUsize` (unique whatever the clock does) and the test module's `SPAWN_SERIAL` mutex is held across each stub-script write (`stub_fetch_script`) and across each serialized spawn (`download_serialized`), so no fork can happen while a script write-fd is open. Verified: 30/30 + 30/30 lib loops, 30/30 of the three-test filter that failed 6/30 before, and three whole-workspace `cargo test --release` runs green. Test-only: no product code, no pmtest pin, no merge-path gate. [P]

To do: (1) decide whether `gather_deps` is needed at all: if `gather_bounded` covers it, delete both functions and rewrite those tests against `find_smallest_cycle` or `gather_bounded`; (2) check the tests still pin what they claim (they assert the closure of a node under `None`/`Some(Ignore::NOptional)`, including the "1 escapes" case, and a bounded search that gives up early now also returns `None`, so they must not be read as a pin of Portage's `gather_deps`); (3) check that `find_smallest_cycle_pins_the_ladder_and_smallest_pick` and `..._keeps_the_first_of_two_equal_rings` (`tests/cycles.rs`) still pin the tie-break (smallest closure, then (cp, version, index)) now that the candidates are no longer sorted; (4) the `// Portage: depgraph.py, _serialize_tasks (gather_deps)` citation and `LLM/code-notes/portage-repo/merge_order/*.md` must follow. Tier 8.

## Deliberate cuts — do NOT pick these (documented non-goals)

- Backtrack **timing** report line `Dependency resolution took X s (backtrack: N/M)` — non-deterministic, portuale is a deterministic tool. [A]
- Static `--pretend` preamble (`These are the packages that would be merged, in order:` / `Calculating dependencies ... done!`) — unpinned today, and aligning it would move every display pin for zero resolution signal; the timing half is the cut above. Backlog #59 K2. [A]
- `soname` slot-conflict reason key — unreachable: `portage-dep` can't parse soname atoms, dep flattening drops them. [A]
- Third-party mirror list **shuffle** (real's `random.shuffle`; portuale stays deterministic) — every candidate is digest-verified regardless, so shuffle order affects only which mirror is tried first (load balancing), not correctness. Decided 2026-09-14 (D4). [E]
- `--autounmask-write` / any config-writing autounmask mode — "never writes `/etc/portage`". [Part 3]
- PyO3 / in-process FFI; EAPI 0/1/2/3/4/6; `bsd_chflags`; RPM; `emerge --sync`; GLSA/`@security`; `equery`/`portageq`; `clap` for the emerge/ebuild parsers; `os.listdir()`-order directory merge. [Part 3]
- `--metadata` (portuale reads `md5-cache` directly); `xpak` for `mrg`. [Part 3]
- Cycle `--tree` **nesting** — real's `[nomerge]` rows, duplicated nodes and row-counted `Total:` inside a circular-dependency tree. Portuale's tree model dedups by design (Gate G0.2); `large_cycle_count`, elementary-cycle enumeration (`merge_order.rs::elementary_cycles`) and the flat cycle re-display already match real (the `cyc4a` pin). Decided 2026-09-14 (D1, former backlog #21). [A]

The two `test_emerge_resume_*` resume-clear assertions (`not mtimedb.exists()`) were the failing surface. [I]

Residues: **#290**-**#293**. *Earlier PARKED text follows (history; its gap-(b) reading and its `lib.rs` line numbers are stale -- the trigger fn is `built_slot_operator_rebuild_trigger`, the walk arm and `direct_solve_arg_mode` moved).* **PARKED 2026-10-01 (owner, batch-2026-09-29 R3 S0 / B9): researched residue, not a local fix.** The atomicity rule is **not local** to the slot-operator handling, so this is not a port of one gate: it is the **ungated general** in-pass slot-conflict solver plus general request semantics. S0 (2026-10-01, `main` @ portuale `f81c85bd`, real 3.0.82.2 with `--debug` traces, the `mmprov`/`mmcons` shape at `--backtrack=0`) found the decline in `_solve_non_slot_operator_slot_conflicts` (`lib/_emerge/depgraph.py:1774-2115`), called unconditionally from `_process_slot_conflicts` (`:2116`) inside `_resolve_conflicts` (`:9443`) -- and that solver explicitly *excludes* slot-operator conflicts (`:1789-1793`). On pass 0 at **both** budgets it `remove:`s `pprov-2` and `keep:`s installed `pprov-1`, because installed `pprov-1` is reachable from `non_conflict_node` via the `>=pprov-1:0/1=` parent edge while the request atom's or_tuple is already satisfied, and `is_arg_parent` is false for every parent (`_want_installed_pkg(pprov-1)` is true: the explicit `app-misc/pprov` request does not count, via `_iter_atoms_for_pkg`'s higher-visible-slot skip, `:4985-4997`). Removal is followed by an in-pass `_create_graph` re-run and `_conflict_missed_update` (`:2097-2115`), which renders the WARNING block. **A port needs three non-local changes**, each with blast radius beyond this shape: (a) the walk's `built_slot_operator_rebuild_trigger` exemption (`portage-repo/src/lib.rs:15292`, applied at `:28070`) never even records the conflict at bt0 -- true at bt>0, false at bt0; (b) portuale's `direct_solve_arg_mode` (`lib.rs:22128`, pinned by `direct_solve_arg_mode_166_*`) returns `True` here (a requested cp excludes its installed instance from every match list), so the `>=…:0/1=` parent matches nothing and `protect_all` (`:22281-22285`) removes nothing -- real reaches the opposite only via the higher-slot arg skip, which is general request semantics over **every** requested-vs-installed contention; (c) real re-resolves within the pass after `_remove_pkg`; portuale's repoint arm documents exactly that as not-done (a keeper merge dep would need a second walk -- the documented non-leaf staleness, `:30137`). Gating (a) alone flips one wrong answer (silent partial upgrade) into another (protected conflict, still merging `pprov-2`) -- the partial approximation owner B9 of batch-2026-09-29 forbade. **No product change, no pin change, nothing blessed** (R3 left both worktrees clean). When it is un-parked: the pin to extend is `test_oracle_prune_rebuilds_conflict_missed_updates`'s `--backtrack=0` control (its `TBD-270` comment), from "the WARNING persists" to real's full row set `[sct-1.0, oldc, newc]`. *Original entry follows.* **OPEN (filed 2026-09-29, #253 pin control, batch-2026-09-28 Track R) — at `--backtrack=0` real withholds the provider update itself where portuale upgrades partially.** Same #253-half-1 probe with `--backtrack=0`: real 3.0.82.2 (rc 0, `backtrack: 0/0`) merges only `[sct-1.0, oldc, newc]` -- no `pprov-2`, no `pcons-1` -- plus the conflict `WARNING`; portuale merges `pprov-2` as a plain `U` (no `r`, no `pcons` row). Real declines the whole provider update when its consumer rebuild cannot be scheduled without backtracking (all-or-nothing); portuale upgrades the provider and skips the rebuild (partial). Pre-existing (the trigger change cannot reach it: the scan stays off at `--backtrack=0`); the #253 pin's bt0 control locks only the WARNING persistence. Probe the atomicity rule (which updates real declines at bt0 and why) and port it. [A]

291. **DONE 2026-10-03 (first half; unit-pinned, no live cell) — filed 2026-10-03, #270 review, plausible not constructed) — the bt0 lone-record re-file can mis-file parents of an installed instance.** `merge_same_slot_conflicts` with `refile_singletons` builds the match string from tree metadata (`slot_conflict_meta`) while `build_slot_conflict` reads the installed instance's sub-slot/repo from the vdb; an installed instance whose sub-slot differs from the tree's (or whose version left every repo) fails a puller atom with an explicit sub-slot, so the parent drops from that instance. Also files a non-discriminating parent under every instance it satisfies (real's `all_parent_atoms` is one list), so a surviving lone conflict at bt0 can gain a duplicate parent line. Needs a binpkg-recorded built atom to construct. [A] **Resolved 2026-10-03:** `merge_same_slot_conflicts` now matches puller atoms against an installed instance's own recorded sub-slot (`inst.sub_slot`, read from the vdb by `build_slot_conflict`) instead of `slot_conflict_meta`'s tree value; unit pin `merge_same_slot_conflicts_refiles_a_built_puller_under_the_installed_sub_slot` (fails without the change). **Second half closed 2026-10-03 (no change; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-03-292/`):** 12 bed cells (unsolvable pair + a bare `slotconflicttarget` argument, or + the bare-RDEPEND `slotconflictnewconsumer`; bt0 and default; both argument orders) show no duplicate parent line on either side, and real lists no bare-atom parent under any instance, so the hypothesised divergence does not materialise and the `hits_a else hits_b` rule stays. The cells exposed two other pre-existing divergences, filed as #298.
292. **DONE 2026-10-03 (first half; unit-pinned, no live cell) — filed 2026-10-03, #270 review, plausible) — missed-update block order when a higher version replaces a lower one.** Real keeps the `(root, slot_atom)` key at its first insertion position even when a higher pkg overwrites the value (`_get_missed_updates`, `depgraph.py:1544-1560`); `collapse_skipped_updates` keeps the surviving row at its own position, and `first_seen`/`block_rank` key without the slot. Slot X (lower, recorded first), block Y, then X's higher pkg: real X,Y; portuale Y,X. Also real's relative order of withhold rows vs conflict rows is unverified (withholds are appended sorted behind the direct rows). [A] **Resolved 2026-10-03:** `collapse_skipped_updates` now orders each slot's surviving rows at the slot's first row position (real's dict key order, `_get_missed_updates` `depgraph.py:1544-1560`, confirmed by reading the source); unit pin `collapse_skipped_updates_keeps_a_slots_first_position` (fails without the sort). No bed cell was built: the shape needs two missed slots with the higher pkg of the first slot arriving after the second block, which no committed fixture produces. **Second half closed 2026-10-03 (evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-03-292/`):** a bed cell with a conflict removal and a reverse-pin withhold in one run (installed world `whtarget-1.0` pinning `~whblocker-1.0` + the old/new consumer pair; either argument order, bt0 and default) shows real printing the conflict block first and the withhold second (both are `_conflict_missed_update` entries in solving order, `backtrack: 0/20`). Portuale led with the withhold at the default budget: its backtrack mask also carries the withheld version and mask rows chain first. Fix: a slot the pass already holds moves to the pass row's position (rows inside the slot keep their order; mask-only slots still lead). Pin `test_oracle_292_pin_withhold_block_follows_the_conflict_block` (fails without the change). cargo, pytest 2241 (no corpus drift), 13/13 lists green.
293. **DONE 2026-10-03 (no code change; evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-03-293/`, successor #295) — filed 2026-10-03, #270 review, plausible) — the rebuild-trigger exemption gates on the configured budget, not the pass's remaining budget.** `ctx.backtrack_max > 0` keeps the exemption in a pass that has exhausted its budget (bt 3/20 final run), where real's last no-backtrack run would not exempt. Only `--backtrack=0` is pinned and probed; a bed cell on an exhausted-budget shape is needed before touching it. [A] **Resolved 2026-10-03:** the exhausted-budget bed cells were built (B/A/C at `--backtrack` 1/2/3) and they do diverge, but not through this gate: real's last no-backtrack run makes `--backtrack=2` print the `--backtrack=0` rows while portuale never exhausts (it budgets mask steps, real counts restarts), so portuale's final pass is not reached on any constructible shape. A pass-level `backtracking()` flag set before the best-run re-pass was tried and moved no cell; reverted. The real divergence is filed as #295.
294. **DONE 2026-10-03 (branch `backlog/bed-empty-bintree`; found while probing #243) — the fixture-oracle bed's real saw no binary packages at all.** The committed `pmtest/fixtures/pkgdir/Packages` header (`TIMESTAMP: 0`, `PACKAGES: 2`) had no `VERSION:` line, so real's `bintree._pkgindex_version_supported` was False and the index was discarded; with the default `FEATURES=pkgdir-index-trusted` `populate()` runs `_populate_local(reindex=False)` and never scans the files, leaving the bintree empty (live: `--usepkgonly` on any fixture binary printed "no binary packages to satisfy"; `_populate_local(reindex=True)` found all 18). Fix: `VERSION: 0` as first header line. Effects: g212's control cell now shows real's genuine `backtrack: 1/20` mask-built restart (#212 confirmed for real); the #243 cell diverged for real (see #243); the other 12 lists stayed green. The earlier S0/g243 reports' conclusions about real's rejector came from the empty tree. [A]
295. **DONE 2026-10-03 (evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-03-295/`, residue #297; pmtest addc620) — `--backtrack=N` budget accounting: real counts restarts, portuale counted mask steps.** Landed: `Backtracker` depth is real's `max(1, (N+1)//2)`; the loop stops when `restarts >= N` before feeding back (real `backtracked >= max_retries`); the best-run re-pass runs with the `--backtrack=0` gates (a `ResolveRequest` clone with `backtrack_max 0`, reported budget kept); `apply_prune_rebuilds` also clears `slot_operator_forced_upgrade` (the #236 A forcing is the replace-set's probe upgrade, real's prune drops it with the set); a best-run that still wants the `prune_rebuilds` restart (replace set non-empty, missed updates, no latch) sets `GraphResult::prune_unresolved` and exits 1 after display (real `depgraph.py:5763-5775` returns failure when it cannot restart). All 7 #293 cells (B/A/C at bt1/2/3) now match real rows and rc; `testBacktrackingGoodVersionFirst` at bt1/bt2 now rc 1 like real (the contract test no longer pins bt2 as portuale's threshold). Original entry follows. *(original title)* OPEN (filed 2026-10-03, #293 probe) — `--backtrack=N` budget accounting: real counts restarts, portuale counts mask steps.** Evidence `docs/evidence/2026-10-03-293/` (cells B/A/C at bt 1 and 2): where the search needs 3 restarts, real at `--backtrack=2` ends on its last no-backtrack run and prints the `--backtrack=0` rows (`[sct, oldc, newc]`, rc 0), at `--backtrack=1` prints the full list but exits 1 (`backtrack: 1/1`); portuale prints the full list rc 0 for every N >= 1. Real: `_backtrack_depgraph` (`backtracked` per loop iteration, `max_depth = max(1, (retries + 1) // 2)`, final `allow_backtracking=False` run); portuale: `Backtracker` depth = N steps, documented deviation at `ResolveRequest::backtrack_max`. Fix direction: count passes like real and run the post-exhaustion best-run pass with the `--backtrack=0` gates (a `ctx.backtracking()` flag over the 13 `backtrack_max > 0/== 0` pass gates was prototyped and is a no-op until the accounting changes); rare in practice (default is 20), so low priority. 556. **DONE 2026-10-24 (filed 2026-10-24, from the merge-gate VDB findings) — binary-merge hooks (`pkg_setup`, `pkg_preinst`, `pkg_postinst`) run without the config-derived calling environment, so the vdb `environment` of bash and glibc lacks `QEMU_SOFTMMU_TARGETS=""` and `QEMU_USER_TARGETS=""`.** The two baseline findings of the L1 merge gate are this one cause, and both started with the Portage 3.0.83 reference (absent in every 3.0.82.2 report). Portage 3.0.83 widened `environ_whitelist_re` to `^(CCACHE_|DISTCC_|QEMU_).*` (`special_env_vars.py:256`, upstream `f7bfe34de`, bug 978685), so the empty `USE_EXPAND` variables of the QEMU families now pass the calling-env filter into the saved environment; the binpkg's own `environment` has no such lines. Fixed here: `environ_whitelisted` now accepts `QEMU_*` (`sandbox.rs`, unit test `environ_whitelist_keeps_qemu_prefix_like_portage_3_0_83`). That alone does not clear the findings, because `run_phase_from_saved_env` builds `extra_env` only from `EMERGE_FROM`/`MERGE_TYPE`, repo name and replace versions, so the hooks never see the run-wide `build_env` (only `instprep` does; checked with a temporary print: `instprep` had `QEMU_SOFTMMU_TARGETS=x86_64`, `QEMU_USER_TARGETS=`, `setup`/`preinst`/`postinst` had none). Remaining work: pass the whitelisted part of `build_env` to the binary hooks, and give it the per-package `USE_EXPAND` overlay from the binpkg's `IUSE`/`USE` (`phase_environ_pkg`), because Portage writes `QEMU_SOFTMMU_TARGETS=""` while the run-wide value here is the profile's `x86_64`. This is a merge-path change: it needs the glibc and bash gate afterwards and should be checked for other newly visible variables. Done: the whitelisted `build_env`, with `USE_EXPAND` recomputed from the binpkg's `IUSE`/`USE` (`binary_hook_env` in `ebuild_merge/from_binpkg.rs`), now reaches every binary-merge hook and `instprep`; unmerge hooks are unchanged. The glibc and bash gate went from 2 findings to 0 (`LLM/evidence/2026-10-24-vdb-env/gate-before.txt`, `gate-after.txt`). Oracle: `pmtest/differential-test-bed/logs/l1-20261023T231321Z/{portage,portuale}.vdb/pkg/app-shells/bash-5.3_p15/environment` (diff is exactly the two `QEMU_*` lines at 1923-1924; glibc 4133-4134) and host `/usr/lib/python3.14/site-packages/portage/package/ebuild/_config/special_env_vars.py:256`. Tier 2. [A]
557. **DONE 2026-10-25 (plan `LLM/superpowers/plans/2026-10-25-557-558-560-561.md` Task 1; branch `residui-514-555`) — phase processes now run under Portage's `spawn` `opt_name`: new pure `phase_opt_name` (`[<cat>/<pf>]` + the `bash`/`sandbox` suffix, `fakeroot` never emitted, empty falls back like Portage's `mycpv is None`) threaded from both spawn sites (`run_phase.rs`, `misc_functions.rs`) through `SpawnBashScriptArgs` into `sandbox_wrapped_command`, which sets `CommandExt::arg0` on the direct path and `exec -a` on the unshare shim path (interpreter `sh`→`bash`, a bashism; outer `unshare` keeps its name); the in-process `--shell brush` backend stays silent (no child to rename), documented as a cut. Pins: 5 argv0/opt-name tests incl. the `SANDBOX_ACTIVE` re-exec cover (137 `ebuild_phases` green, clippy clean); live `ps` on a scratch `sleepy` ebuild shows `[dev-libs/sleepy-1.0] bash|sandbox …/ebuild.sh compile` on both suffixes. Oracle: `doebuild.py:spawn (:2243-2261)`, `process.py:_exec (:1027-1036)`.** Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-10-24) — phase processes show the bare binary in `ps`, not Portage's `[<cat>/<pf>] sandbox ebuild.sh <phase>`.** Portage names every spawned phase `[{mycpv}] bash|fakeroot|sandbox` (`lib/portage/package/ebuild/doebuild.py:spawn`, `:2243-2261`: `keywords["opt_name"] = f"[{mycpv}]"` plus the ` bash` / ` fakeroot` / ` sandbox` suffix, `[{CATEGORY}/{PF}]` fallback when `mycpv` is None) and `portage/process.py:_exec` (`:1027-1036`) execs with `myargs = [opt_name] + mycommand[1:]`, so `ps` shows e.g. `[www-client/firefox-158.0.1] sandbox /usr/lib/portage/python3.14/ebuild.sh compile`. Portuale's `sandbox_wrapped_command` (`crates/portuale/src/ebuild_phases/sandbox.rs:301-346`) builds `Command::new(&argv[0])` with the real binary name and never overrides argv[0] — both the direct path and the `unshare … sh -c … portuale-sandbox` shim path. Fix sketch: thread the entry's `CATEGORY`/`PF` (or mycpv string) to the spawn site and set argv[0] via `std::os::unix::process::CommandExt::arg0` to `[<cat>/<pf>] sandbox|bash` matching Portage's three-way suffix (fakeroot only if/when portuale gains that wrapper); keep the `CATEGORY`/`PF`-empty fallback shape Portage has. The in-process `--shell brush` backend has no child process to rename — document as a cut. Oracle: live `ps -ef` (or `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`) during a slow `src_compile` on both managers; real-execution feature, so a Rust fixture-driven e2e test (spawn a sleeping phase, read the child's cmdline), no contract pin. Tier 2. [D]

639. **DONE 2026-11-01 (branch `slice/639-loopback-warn` in both repos: portuale `2d71328f` child-side `write(2)` report + warning table + pipe deletion + test rewrites, `9e21296a` bare-`strerror` text without `io::Error`'s ` (os error N)` suffix (review fix 1), `0152e2c3` `strerror_r` table builder + testable `loopback_warn_line` (review fix 2), `f82c3b38` head/`.gz` pins + S0 fd-2 evidence + hyperfine acceptance log, plus this docs-only close-out; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-11-01-639-loopback-report/`) — under `FEATURES=compress-build-logs` the loopback warning is now part of the phase's gzip member instead of raw text appended past it, so the log still decodes to its end and every reader sees the line (the foreground-terminal oracle-not-run caveat lives in #640's entry).** Cause: #638 appended the warning in the parent after the pumps joined, with `OpenOptions::create(true).append(true)`, but a `.log.gz` is written only through `open_log_file`'s pump (one member per phase; readers wrap it back with `gzip.GzipFile(mode="rb")` in the failure tail and QA scan), so the raw tail was either unreadable or corrupt — Python's `GzipFile` reads *across* concatenated members (it does not stop at the first), so the pump's one-member-per-phase log is read whole, and what those readers cannot take is a raw, uncompressed tail past the members. Fix shape: S0 (`s0-fd2-preexec.log`, a `Command::stderr(File)` probe) showed fd 2 inside `pre_exec` IS the `Command`-configured stderr (`dup2` runs before hooks on rustc 1.99.0); that is proven for the `File` destination the probe exercised, while the pipe-backed `Tee` and the un-redirected `Inherit` legs are extrapolated from the same `dup2` ordering (low risk, stated as extrapolation, not probed). With that, the demoted child writes the line itself with one `write(2)` to fd 2 and the pipe leg is deleted — `pipe2(O_CLOEXEC)`, the closure's write `File` with its 2-byte payload, the post-wait read and the parent `OpenOptions::append` are all gone, so no path can bypass the pump. The text is a parent-built 135-entry table (`loopback_warn_table`, index `errno - 1`, XSI `strerror_r` into a caller-owned stack buffer, Portage's bare `strerror` body), looked up child-side by the allocation-free `loopback_warn_line` (`checked_sub`/`get`) with a `const` `LOOPBACK_WARN_FALLBACK` past the end: `Unable to configure loopback interface: <strerror>\n` (`process.py:867-871`). Pins: root-gated `demoted_loopback_warning_survives_gzip_log` (injected `linkup` through `Capture` to `build.log.gz`; `MultiGzDecoder` reads the member to the end and carries both Portage's warning and `PHASE_RAN=1` — a raw tail append breaks exactly this) and `loopback_warn_table_matches_portage_text` + `loopback_warn_lookup_falls_back_out_of_range` (prefix, one trailing newline, no `(os error` suffix, 135 entries, errno 0/200 → fallback). Acceptance is supporting only, not the pin: `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0 (runner-reported; not in the log), `>>> Completed (1 of 1) app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0::gentoo` (`hyperfine-acceptance.log`, hyperfine 1.21.0) — a plain host `emerge` runs no demoted netns spawn, so it cannot reach the warning leg; it proves the rewritten reporting path did not regress a real merge, and the spawn tests are the pin. Close-out verification (docs-only delta, product identical to `f82c3b38`): `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --release --all-targets` zero warnings, workspace `cargo test --release --no-fail-fast` 3278 passed / 1 failed / 5 ignored across 32 binaries, `sudo <test-binary> demoted_loopback --test-threads=1` 2 passed / 0 failed, pmtest `scripts/pytest-parallel.sh` 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed in 193 s (rc=1; the seven are the known reds named below) with zero corpus drift (nothing blessed); the default basetemp is still poisoned by root-owned residue (#634: `PermissionError` on `/var/tmp/pytest-parallel/popen-gw3/test_merge_skips_an_identical_0/root/usr/share/binpkgrmpkg`, `root:root 755`), so the sanctioned `PMTEST_BASETEMP=/var/tmp/pytest-parallel-639` workaround was used. The single cargo red is the known host-state `embedded_runtime::tests::a_phase_running_merge_works_from_an_extracted_runtime` (`mrg: pulling /etc/portage from localtest failed (exit 2)`, disclosed at #630–#638); the pytest reds are exactly the seven known `test_mrg_remote_*` tests (same `mrg: pulling /etc/portage from localtest failed (exit 2)` cause); #632/#633 did not recur in this run. Honest limits: `pre_exec` still prints nothing in the stdio sense — one raw `write(2)` of a live table line is the entire reporting channel (`let _`, a failed report never fails the spawn), and no allocation, stdio or subprocess runs there; the table body is the C library's (XSI `strerror_r`, the same glibc text `os.strerror` returns), so the unit test pins the stable prefix, the single newline and the absence of the `(os error` suffix, not the C-locale wording; only `Capture` is pinned end to end, while `Tee`/`Inherit` reach the terminal by construction; the abort-the-rest shape stays reviewer-pinned, not test-pinned (a failed link-up also leaves the v4 route absent, so `V4_ROUTE=0` fits both "stopped" and "ran and failed"). Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-01, residue of the #638 close-out) — with `FEATURES=compress-build-logs` the loopback warning is appended to the `.gz` build log as raw text after the gzip members, bypassing the pump.** `spawn_bash_script`'s parent-side report matches `PhaseLog::Capture(path) | PhaseLog::Tee(path)` and writes `loopback_warn_msg` with `OpenOptions::new().create(true).append(true)` (`crates/portuale/src/ebuild_phases/commands.rs`), but under `compress-build-logs` every log path ends `.log.gz` (`emerge_build::build_log_path`, `emerge_build/logs.rs:100`) and that file is otherwise written only through `open_log_file`'s gzip pump (one member per phase); readers wrap it back with `gzip.GzipFile(mode="rb")` (failure tail, QA scan), so the raw tail is unreadable to them. Portage's warning goes through the phase's logging pipe, so it is compressed with the rest. Evidence: the code path above plus the #638 test, which pins only a plain `injected.log`; no `.gz` cell exists. Oracle: a demoted+net `Capture` spawn with `PORTUALE_TEST_LOOPBACK_FAIL=linkup` writing to a `.gz` path — `gzip -t`/the failure-tail reader must still read the warning; today the bare append either breaks decompression or is silently lost. Fix shape: route the report through the same gzip-aware sink (append a member, or re-open through `open_log_file`'s writer), never a bare `OpenOptions` append. Tier 3. [C]

640. **DONE 2026-11-01 (same branch and commits as #639 above: `2d71328f` child-side `write(2)` replacing the post-wait `OpenOptions::append`/`eprint!` split, `9e21296a` bare-`strerror` text (review fix 1), `0152e2c3` `strerror_r` table builder + `loopback_warn_line` (review fix 2), `f82c3b38` head-placement and `.gz` pins + S0 fd-2 evidence + hyperfine acceptance log, plus this docs-only close-out; pmtest clean, no commit, no pretend-output change; evidence `LLM/evidence/2026-11-01-639-loopback-report/`) — the loopback warning now lands where Portage's does: before the phase's output, because the child writes it in `pre_exec` before `execve`, and on the tee stream in a foreground `Tee` spawn, so it is visible on the terminal as well as in the log (S0 + by-construction; foreground oracle not run).** Cause: #638 could only report after the phase was reaped, so the line was appended after all phase output where Portage's forked `_exec2` writes it before `execve` (`process.py:861-871`), and its `Tee` arm wrote the file only — a foreground `ebuild … compile` showed nothing on stderr where Portage's logging pipe tees the line (the `Inherit`/`Foreground` arm reached the terminal but not the log). Fix shape: one child-side `libc::write(2, line.as_ptr(), line.len())` to fd 2 inside the stored `pre_exec` closure (demoted + `net`, after `configure_loopback_netlink`, before the pid fork branch) — S0 shows that fd is the `Command`-configured stderr for the `File` destination it probed (`s0-fd2-preexec.log`), so one write reaches whichever sink the `Command` points fd 2 at: the build-log pipe (and so a gzip member), the shared tee pipe (our stdout plus the log, in order, the write preceding every phase byte), or the inherited terminal; only that `File` leg is proven directly, while the pipe-backed `Tee` and the un-redirected `Inherit` legs extrapolate the same `dup2` ordering (low risk, stated as extrapolation, not probed). The result is ignored, so a failed report never fails the spawn, and no `println!`/`print!`, stdio, allocation or subprocess runs in the child; the parent's append/`eprint!` match arms and the `pipe2` read loop are deleted rather than kept as a fallback, because a second reporting path would re-open #639's pump bypass and #640's tail placement. Pins: root-gated `demoted_loopback_failure_warns_and_continues` now asserts the warning is `lines().next()` in the `Capture` log (head, pre-exec, before `V4_ROUTE=0`), the clean control still showing `V4_ROUTE=1` + a real localhost TCP round-trip + no warning; `demoted_loopback_warning_survives_gzip_log` pins the same position inside a decodable gzip member (`MultiGzDecoder` to the end, warning and `PHASE_RAN=1`). Acceptance is supporting only, not the pin: `sudo ./target/release/portuale emerge -1 app-benchmarks/hyperfine` rc=0 (runner-reported), `>>> Completed (1 of 1) app-benchmarks/hyperfine-1.21.0::gentoo` (`hyperfine-acceptance.log`, hyperfine 1.21.0) — no demoted netns spawn runs on a plain host `emerge`, so it cannot reach the warning leg. Close-out verification is #639's (same docs-only delta): workspace `cargo test --release --no-fail-fast` 3278 passed / 1 failed / 5 ignored, `sudo <test-binary> demoted_loopback --test-threads=1` 2 passed, pmtest 4618 passed / 7 failed / 2 skipped / 2 xfailed in 193 s (rc=1; the seven are the known reds named below) with zero corpus drift. Honest limits: the head assertion is on a `Capture` log — `Tee`'s terminal delivery is by construction (fd 2 is the tee pipe, drained to our stdout and the log by `open_tee_log`'s single pump), not directly pinned, and `Inherit` keeps whatever fd 2 the parent had; the Portage-vs-portuale foreground oracle filed on the OPEN entry below (same forced failure on a foreground run, comparing the warning's position in `build.log` and its presence on stderr) was **not** run, so the terminal half rests on the S0 probe plus by-construction reasoning — the tee pipe carries the child's stderr to our stdout — rather than on an oracle comparison; the abort-the-rest shape is reviewer-pinned, not test-pinned (a failed link-up also leaves the v4 route absent, so `V4_ROUTE=0` fits "stopped" and "ran and failed" alike); `pre_exec` still prints nothing beyond that single raw `write(2)`, and a toolchain that ordered `pre_exec` before `dup2` would put the line on the parent stderr — visible, never lost (a code comment, not a residue). No new residue was filed, after explicitly evaluating the two candidates: the multi-member reader is `MultiGzDecoder`, the right reader for a concatenated-gzip log rather than a tolerance gap, and the toolchain-reorder behaviour degrades visibly, so documenting it in the code and here is enough. Original entry follows. **OPEN (filed 2026-11-01, residue of the #638 close-out) — the loopback warning lands at the end of the phase log instead of Portage's head placement, and for `Tee`/foreground spawns it never reaches the terminal.** Portage configures loopback in its forked `_exec2`, before `execve` (`process.py:861-871`), so the warning is the first thing in the phase's log and shares the phase's stderr stream (teed to the terminal by Portage's logging pipe in a foreground run). Portuale can only report after the phase is reaped: the parent appends to the `Capture`/`Tee` file after the pumps join, so the line lands after all phase output — the #638 test asserts exactly that ("the warning is appended after the phase output") — and the `Tee` arm writes the file only, so a foreground `ebuild … compile` shows nothing on stderr where Portage's line is visible; `Inherit`/`Foreground` use `eprint!` (terminal-only, no log). Evidence: the test's last-line assertion and the match arms in `spawn_bash_script`; no oracle run compares positions. Oracle: the same forced failure through Portage and portuale on a foreground run, comparing the warning's position in `build.log` and its presence on stderr. Fix shape: report before the phase's output (e.g. a child-side async-signal-safe `write(2)` to the phase's stderr, the alternative #638's own spec named) or have the parent prepend to the log and echo to the tee stream. Tier 3. [C]

641. **DONE 2026-11-02 (branch `backlog/641-conflict-path-r` in both repos) — walked consumers whose `||` menu keeps an installed instance now file the slot-conflict backtrack, so the upgraded provider regains host Portage's red `r`.** Host prints `[ebuild r U] p569prov-5.44` + `[ebuild rR] p569virt-1` (slot conflict → `backtracking due to missed slot abi update` files both `(parent, installed)` and `(child, installed)` into `slot_operator_replace_installed`; `forced reinstall atoms` names both). Three coordinated changes: (1) the rev-dep scan's post-merge view applies to walked consumers too (it used to require an unwalked one), so a built pin unsatisfiable post-merge is seen; (2) a walked pin additionally needs a `||` menu arm matching a kept installed instance (`or_menu_keeps_installed`) -- otherwise the merge supersedes the instance and the atom stays report-only, which is exactly host Portage's behavior for plain built pins (slotbind's `:2/2=` merges `U` with no rebuild; the kept-instance unit twin stays green); (3) the conflict ABI probe's `||` group no longer vetoes when an arm matches the kept installed instance instead (Portage records only `:=`/soname atoms in `_slot_operator_deps`, so menu pins never join its probe loop), gated on `--update`. Pins: the two #598 rows back to host `r U` (with doc notes naming the conflict path), plus a red-pre-fix Rust unit twin for the probe's installed-arm acceptance.
